## Added

- **cli** ([Release v3.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.21.0)): **Cratis AI update hint.** In a repository with the Cratis AI corpus installed (`.cratis/ai.json` and `.cratis/ai.manifest.json`), an interactive command may end with `↑ Cratis AI update available: 12 new commits since cc9c631 - run 'cratis ai update'`. The hint appears only when the installed commit is behind the latest commit on Cratis/AI's default branch, so a corpus installed from a local checkout or another branch never triggers it. The answer is cached per installed commit next to the CLI version check and rechecked hourly. The hint is skipped when output is redirected, in AI agent environments and completions, while running `ai install`/`ai update`/`ai uninstall`, and when `CRATIS_NO_UPDATE_CHECK` is set (#197).
- **cli** ([Release v3.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.21.0)): **`cratis render` sends Screenplay's typed-context descriptors to Stage**, so generated code can use typed contexts (#192).
- **Arc** ([Release v22.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.31.0)): A named Arc authorization policy can opt in to being evaluated for unauthenticated callers: `AddArcAuthorizationPolicy<TPolicy>(name, evaluatesAnonymous: true)`. The policy then receives an empty, unauthenticated principal and decides whether to allow the guest. This works the same with Arc's `[Authorize]` and ASP.NET Core's `[Authorize(Policy = ...)]`, for commands, queries and observable queries. (#2761)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.31.0)): `AddArcAnonymousAspNetAuthorizationPolicy(name)` gives the same opt-in to a policy registered through ASP.NET Core. It is checked at startup: the name must resolve to exactly one ASP.NET Core policy that doesn't require an authenticated user. Otherwise startup fails with `InvalidAuthorizationConfiguration`. (#2761)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): Render Screenplay ESM v3 reducers with verified portable C# bodies as Chronicle reducers and typed context adapters. Supported deterministic, finite operations are admitted explicitly; unsupported or mismatched bodies fail with actionable diagnostics.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): Generate read-model and command specifications for reducer-backed models, including collection event expectations and deletion. The Billing payment reducer is an executable example.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): Guard every non-null reducer output so its identifier matches the event-source key; mismatches fail rather than silently routing a keyed query to unrelated state.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.12.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.12.1)): A packed-consumer check that restores and builds `Cratis.Orleans` for .NET 8, 9 and 10 and fails when consumers would resolve mixed Orleans versions. It runs on pull requests, in the daily package update, and before a release is cut.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.43.0)): `ChronicleCommandScenario` and `ChronicleQueryScenario` evaluate supported flat projections in-process through `ReadModelScenario` from `@cratis/chronicle` 6.19.0, so `given.forEventSource(...).events(...)` now populates projection-backed read models as well as reducer-backed ones. See [Test Chronicle commands and queries](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/blob/v0.43.0/Documentation/testing/chronicle.md) for the boundary.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.43.0)): A pinned instance wins over history, and a reducer wins over a projection. Projection seeds replay in the order they were given across event sources.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.43.0)): A projection outside the supported boundary throws the SDK's `UnsupportedProjectionOperation` unchanged; test it with `ChronicleKernelScenario`.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.22.0)): `ReactorScenario` in `@cratis/chronicle/testing` delivers `given` and `when` events in order using production handler dispatch and artifact activation. An `artifactActivator` receives per-event `run` metadata and `complete()` is called after delivery, including on failure.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.22.0)): Inspect returned events through `produced`, `sideEffects`, `results`, `shouldHaveProduced`, and `shouldNotHaveProduced`. Handler failures reject the delivery and prevent further deliveries.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.22.0)): Unsupported replay, redelivery, command recording, tag-filtered reactors, self-subscribed appends, store/read-model/event-log services, and `waitForCompletion` reject with `UnsupportedReactorOperation` or `UnsupportedEventSequenceOperation`, directing tests to a kernel-backed scenario. Unsupported calls fail delivery even when caught by a handler.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.22.0)): Reactor scenario examples cover injected dependencies, services, and multiple event sources.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.12.0)): `Cratis.Orleans.Chronicle` package with `EventSourcedStateMachine<TModel>`: an Orleans state machine whose model is a Chronicle read model derived from events. It reads through `IEventStore.ReadModels`, never writes, and needs no `[StorageProvider]` (#34)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.12.0)): `DefineProjection(IProjectionBuilderFor<TModel>)` for declarative projections; model-bound projections on `TModel` also work. Projections are registered through Chronicle's explicit registration API (#34)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.12.0)): `DefineTransitions(IStateMachineTransitions<TModel>)` with `.On<TEvent>().TransitionTo<TState>()` and optional model guards. Definitions are inspected at startup (#34)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.12.0)): One fluent Chronicle reactor per state machine, subscribed to the union of the projection's and the transitions' event types. Each delivered event refreshes the grain's model, calls `OnModelChanged(previous, current, context)`, then applies matching transitions (#34)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.12.0)): Grain keys `{eventStore}/{namespace}/{eventSourceId}` isolate tenants. `IGrainFactory.GetEventSourcedStateMachine<T>(...)` resolves a grain; `ResolveState(model)` derives the current state from the model on activation, so no state is persisted (#34)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.12.0)): `AddEventSourcedStateMachines()` for `IServiceCollection` / `ISiloBuilder` (#34)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.14.0)): `IProjections.Register<TReadModel>(define, id)` registers a read model together with a declarative projection defined through the same `IProjectionBuilderFor<TReadModel>` an `IProjectionFor<T>` uses, without assembly discovery. The read model behaves like any other through `IReadModels`, including passive (on-demand) read models (#4287)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.14.0)): `IProjections.Register<TReadModel>()` registers the model-bound projection of a read model that discovery did not find (#4287)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.14.0)): `IReactors.Define(id, reactor => ...)` and `IReactors.Register(id, reactor => ...)` declare reactors fluently with typed `On<TEvent>` handlers — `(TEvent)`, `(TEvent, EventContext)` and their `Task`-returning forms — and a catch-all `Subscribe((object, EventContext) => ...)`. `IReactorDefinition` exposes the reactor id, event types and whether it subscribes to all events (#4288)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.14.0)): `ChronicleOptions.ExplicitArtifacts` registers explicit projections, read models and fluent reactors before the client connects; they are included in every registration pass, including after a reconnect (#4287, #4288)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.11.0)): `Cratis.Orleans.StateMachines`: the generic Orleans state machine, extracted from Chronicle with the same behavior — `StateMachine<TStoredState>`, `State<TStoredState>`, `IState<TStoredState>`, `IStateMachine<TStoredState>`, `NoOpState`, `StateMachineState`, allowed transitions, `OnEnter`/`OnLeave`, and transitions scheduled while entering (#33)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.11.0)): A protected `StateMachine(IStorage<TStoredState>)` constructor, so a derived state machine can supply its own storage without a `[StorageProvider]` attribute (#33)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.11.0)): `ResolveActivationState()` lets a derived state machine decide which state to enter on activation, and the protected `CanTransitionTo(Type)` / `TransitionTo(Type)` allow transitions by type while still honoring each state's rules (#33)
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.42.0)): The proxy generator can write proxies into the backend slice folders. When the output is the artifacts folder, one of its ancestors, or a nested folder containing backend artifacts, the `.proxy` suffix is required, no barrels are written, and watch mode regenerates on backend edits. The generator only overwrites or deletes files it generated, and never analyzes generated, `.proxy`, `.tsx` or spec files as backend artifacts.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.42.0)): Runtime discovery (`builder.discover(...)`) skips co-located `*.proxy.ts`/`*.proxy.js` and `.tsx` files.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.42.0)): The Library sample keeps every slice together, uses `@cratis/components` (`DataPage` for the author listing, `CommandDialog` for registration) and runs frontend specs under jsdom beside the backend specs in `yarn test`. The Tasks sample generates its proxies in place too.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.42.0)): CI fails when committed proxies are stale, and when the Library web bundle contains decorator syntax a browser cannot parse.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.21.0)): `EventScenario` supports both `appendMany` overloads. Batches append atomically in input order and return one result per event. The mixed-source overload uses the production client's per-entry precedence for routing, subject, and occurrence, and merges per-entry and shared tags.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.21.0)): `given.forEventSource(id).events(...events)` accepts multiple setup events as sequential single appends; if any setup event is rejected, none from that call are committed. `when.forEventSource(id).events(...events)` appends one atomic batch and returns a result for each event.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.21.0)): The in-process sequence supports source, event-type, and route read filters, plus append notifications for single and batch appends. Constraints remain unsupported and are rejected rather than simulated.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.21.0)): Committed fixtures from the pinned Chronicle kernel verify batch ordering, metadata, causation, read filters, and rejection behavior.
- **Components** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): `--cratis-canvas-minimap-grid-color` and `--cratis-canvas-minimap-item-color` theme the Canvas minimap dot grid and items that have no `color` of their own. Both fall back to the current colors, so the minimap looks the same until you set them.
- **Components** ([Release v4.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.17.0)): `controlsIcons` on `Canvas` (`icons` on `CanvasControls`) replaces any of the minimap, zoom-out, zoom-in and help glyphs with your own icons. Unset fields keep the built-in icons, and the accessible names still come from `controlsLabels`.
- **Components** ([Release v4.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.17.0)): `controlsFollowViewportInsets` on `Canvas` (`followViewportInsets` on `CanvasControls`) turns off the offset by `--canvas-viewport-left`/`--canvas-viewport-right`, for hosts that already move the canvas by that inset. It defaults to `true`, so existing canvases are unchanged.
- **Components** ([Release v4.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.17.0)): The `--canvas-viewport-left` and `--canvas-viewport-right` custom properties the controls read are now documented.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.43.0)): Publish a slim `Cratis.Screenplay.Contexts` package containing the code-body context family without compiler dependencies. `Cratis.Screenplay` references it and forwards every moved public type, preserving existing binaries. Closes #283.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.20.0)): Add `EventScenario` to `@cratis/chronicle/testing` for kernel-free, scenario-local single-event appends. `given` seeds accepted events, `when` appends one event, and `results` and `appendedEvents` expose the act results and accepted history. The scenario supports global sequence allocation, `hasEventsFor`, next and tail positions, source/type reads, and inclusive reads from a sequence number within its documented input boundary.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.20.0)): Check the supported append metadata, results, and reads against committed fixtures generated by the pinned Chronicle 19.8.1 kernel oracle. Add an EventScenario guide and compiled documentation snippets.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.13.0)): `IUnitOfWork.HasEnrolledDecisionReads` reports whether a protected decision read was successfully enrolled, including after commit, rollback or disposal. Command owners can use it to refuse an unguarded returned-event fallback. Existing custom units of work default to `false`; no decision tokens or sequence numbers are exposed.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.13.0)): Release solution build (zero warnings), all non-integration specifications, client packaging, formatting and markdown validation passed.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.13.0)): Local MongoDB integration could not start: Docker Desktop LinuxKit kernel 7.0.12 is incompatible with the MongoDB image. A bounded reproduction failed before test assertions; integration CI on GitHub must pass before merge.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.41.0)): Experimental `DrizzleObservation.PostgreSQLNotify` mode: `withDrizzle({ observation: { mode: DrizzleObservation.PostgreSQLNotify, listener } })` listens for PostgreSQL `NOTIFY` on the fixed `arc_changes` channel through one dedicated connection per tenant, and re-reads observed tables after each committed change. The first read of an observation happens only after `LISTEN` is acknowledged and the table's trigger is validated.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.41.0)): `postgresqlChangeTrigger(table, { schema? })` returns the trigger SQL to add to a drizzle-kit migration. Arc never runs DDL at runtime; a missing, disabled or incompatible trigger fails the observation before any rows are read.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.41.0)): `nodePostgresListener(...)` adapts a dedicated node-postgres `Client` as the listener connection. No `pg` runtime import is added to the package root.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.41.0)): A PostgreSQL-only mode for `Source/Drizzle/run-integration.sh` and a CI job running the PostgreSQL integration specs.
- **Components** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): `confirmBeforeExecute` on `CommandDialog`, `StepperCommandDialog`, and `CommandStepper` asks a question after validation and `onBeforeExecute`, and before the command runs. Only `true` executes. Any other answer leaves the dialog or stepper open without running the command. If the command values change while the question is open, the command is not executed and you confirm again. Stepper fields are disabled until the confirmed submission settles. Without the prop, command execution is unchanged. The `ConfirmBeforeExecute` type is exported. (#317)
- **Components** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): `ChatSidebar` accepts `topicsStatus` and `messagesStatus` (the new `ChatStatus` enum), and `ChatSidebarForObservableQueries` sets them from its queries. The topic list and conversation show loading, failure, and unauthorized text from new `labels` keys, and never show server exception text. A failure keeps what is already shown and adds an alert above it. Unauthorized replaces the content and disables the composer and the New topic button. (#327)
- **Components** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): `PivotViewer` `labels` makes the toolbar text, the item count, and the filter panel's name and search placeholder localizable. The defaults are unchanged. `loadingLabel` still takes precedence over `labels.loading`. (#334)
- **Components** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): `PivotViewer` `labels.searchGroup` names the search box of each option group in the filter panel. It receives the group's label and defaults to `Search <group>`, so each group search now has its own accessible name. (#334)
- **Components** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): FilterPanel `aria-label` and `searchAriaLabel`, `FilterDefinition` `searchAriaLabel` and `autoFocus`, and `CheckboxListFilter` `searchAriaLabel` and `autoFocusSearch`. (#328, #244)
- **Components** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): A `DatePickerInput` reference page, and Common and Dialogs landing pages that help you choose a control. (#260)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.12.0)): The .NET client's generated JSON schemas give inline object types a `title` with the type name. A declaration generated from a fluent composite-key projection (for example in the Workbench) can then name the key type and recompile. The fluent `CompositeKeyBuilder` still emits untyped expressions, so older kernels are unaffected (#4253)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.12.0)): The ASP.NET Core causation middleware records the matched route template in a new `routeTemplate` causation property, alongside the existing request path (#4260)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.30.0)): `IFallbackAuthorizationEvaluator` supplies baseline authorization requirements for commands and queries that declare none of their own. It is consulted only when neither the method nor the type has an explicit declaration, so `[AllowAnonymous]` members are no longer ambiguous with a baseline. Commands consult only the `Type` overload. MVC controller actions, including direct HTTP and WebSocket requests to controller-based queries, stay on ASP.NET Core authorization: use `AuthorizationOptions.FallbackPolicy` there (#2751)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.30.0)): `ReceivedAt` on `CommandContext`, `QueryContext` and `AuthorizationPolicyContext`, plus an injectable `IOperationContextAccessor`, expose the time Arc received the operation. It is captured with `TimeProvider` at Arc's transport dispatch, before binding, or at pipeline entry for programmatic calls, and per subscribe operation for streams. It is Arc's receipt time, not network arrival. `ReceivedAt` takes part in record equality, so contexts from different receipts compare unequal (#2767)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.30.0)): Query filters can set `SubscriptionScope` on the query context. Arc snapshots it after filtering and before the query runs, and gives each emission guard an independent copy, so guards can base decisions on membership that the arguments don't carry. Only state that Arc's JSON options serialize is kept, and a scope that cannot be represented is rejected with `InvalidSubscriptionScope`. The scope is always `null` for controller-based observable queries (#2768)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.19.0)): `ReadModelScenario` now builds projection-backed read models in-process when no reducer applies. It compiles the same model-bound or declarative definition used at registration, validates the whole definition before replay, and then replays the given events: root `fromEvent`/`.from()` keyed by the event source ID, `setFrom` and schema-based AutoMap for scalar properties, `$eventSourceId`, supported `$eventContext(...)` paths, `$value(...)`, `$null`, `removedWith` and initial values. See [Test read models without a kernel](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/blob/v6.19.0/Documentation/testing.md#projection-capabilities) for the full capability table.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.19.0)): `UnsupportedProjectionOperation`, exported from `@cratis/chronicle/testing`. Anything the in-process evaluator does not support (arithmetic such as `@count`/`@increment`, children, joins, nested projections, compliance metadata, and other listed cases) raises this error when the scenario is created, before any event is replayed, instead of producing a result the kernel might not.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.19.0)): Kernel projection fixtures in `Source/testing/projections/fixtures/`, produced by the projection oracle against the pinned Chronicle kernel and compared step by step with the in-process results (`yarn oracle:check`).
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.40.0)): `ArcServer.runInScope(scope, callback, options?)` runs a callback inside an existing service scope, with `currentServices()` and `currentContext()` set as they are for a request. Only the correlation ID and an extra abort signal can be supplied (`RunInScopeOptions`); tenant, principal, transport and severity come from the scope. Only scopes created with `server.services.createScope(context)` can be borrowed; Arc's own request, query and observable-query scopes cannot. It rejects scopes from another server, singleton, closed or fabricated scopes, and scopes whose principal is not plain data. It is a trusted host API, not an authorization mechanism: commands still go through the normal pipeline.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.0)): Register a .NET reactor at runtime by stable ID, explicit event types and generations, and an awaited delegate that receives the event context, the JSON content and the generational content map. Unregister it by ID; the observer keeps its stored position. Tags and filters are not yet configurable on delegate reactors (#4196)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.0)): Reactor side-effect handlers can claim synchronous return types with `CanHandleReturnType(Type)`, including a declared `IEnumerable<T>`. Existing handlers stay compatible (#4121)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.0)): `IReactors.RetryFailedPartitionFor<TReactor>(partition)` retries a reactor's failed partition from the .NET client and returns a `ReactorPartitionRetryOutcome` (#3909)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.0)): Batch event payloads accept an optional per-event `causation` field. Older kernels ignore it and store only the ambient chain (#4179)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.0)): Append results expose the event types the operation wrote, including for batch appends (#4132)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.18.0)): `usingKeyFromContext(...)` and `usingParentKeyFromContext(...)` on declarative `.addChild(...)`, as in Chronicle for .NET, so a child can be keyed from the event context. The context path is validated when the projection is registered.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.29.0)): JavaScript `RuleBuilder.withSeverity` and `PropertyRule.withSeverity` APIs for explicit per-rule validation severity in generated command validators.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.28.0)): EF Core `Observe`, `ObserveSingle` and `ObserveById` accept `ignoreQueryContext: true` for auxiliary sources in composed observable queries. Opted-in sources read their full filtered results without client paging or sorting and do not update `TotalItems`; the primary observation keeps its paging, sorting and count. Existing composed queries must opt in on their auxiliary observations to get this fix, as with the MongoDB overloads in v22.27.0 (#2804)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.28.0)): `AuthorizationPrincipalScope` is public, so a query or command pipeline can be composed without `AddCratisArc`. The query pipeline guide shows the registrations it needs (#2753)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.17.0)): `ActivatedArtifact.run(callback, invocation)` now receives an `ArtifactInvocationContext` for every call: the handled event's `EventContext` and handler name, or the replay notification. A host can then run each reactor or reducer invocation in a context that matches that event, for example with its correlation ID.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.17.0)): `ActivatedArtifact.complete()` is an optional hook called once per activation after its batch has been processed, also when processing failed, and before the delivery is acknowledged. If it rejects, the delivery fails with `ArtifactCompletionFailed`, which keeps a processing error if there was one; `dispose()` still runs afterwards. A host can use it to fail a delivery whose resources could not be released cleanly.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.10.0)): A join in a projection declaration can set its own `automap` or `no automap`, which overrides the projection's setting. The setting is stored with the definition, and applies to live join events and to backfill. Older clients and stored definitions keep inheriting the projection's setting. The fluent .NET `JoinBuilder` and generated client code don't express it yet.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.10.0)): Joins inside a `nested` object are wired: joined values are set on the nested object, both when the join event arrives and through backfill.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): Stage renders more of the executable model onto Cratis now that the upstream fixes are released:
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): projection literal mappings in `from` blocks, using only literals Chronicle's fluent value expression can carry exactly;
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): removing a child through a join;
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): `$context.occurred` on produced events, with one timestamp for the payload and the event's occurrence;
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): contract and production tags that aren’t duplicated. This is part of #117.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): Validation rules and command requirements with warning or information severity reject the command, using Arc's `[BlockOnValidationSeverity]`. (#116)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): Specifications for protected queries run through Arc's query pipeline, including denials. They are admitted only when every seeded event comes from the queried key.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): The semantic runtime rebuilds its state from stored events on restart through Screenplay's `EstablishWorld`. It refuses to start on a history it can't reproduce.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.16.0)): Reactor handlers receive a third argument, `services`, with the `eventStore` and `readModels` of the event store and namespace the reactor observes, plus the delivery's abort `signal`. A handler can now append explicitly and check the `AppendResult`, or look up a read model before acting, as reactors in Chronicle for .NET can through injected services.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.16.0)): `ChronicleOptions.artifactActivator` lets an application or framework construct reactors and reducers, for example from a dependency injection container, like `ClientArtifactsActivator` in .NET. It returns an `ActivatedArtifact` with the instance and optional `run` and `dispose` hooks. With an activator, an artifact is activated once per delivered batch (and separately for replay notifications); `run` wraps each handler call and its returned events, and `dispose` runs when the batch is done. Activation failures fail the partition like handler failures; cleanup failures are logged.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.16.0)): `ClientArtifactsActivator`, `ActivatedArtifact`, `ArtifactActivationContext`, `ReactorServices` and `ReplayState` are exported from the package root.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.27.0)): MongoDB `Observe`, `ObserveSingle`, and `ObserveById` accept `ignoreQueryContext: true` for auxiliary sources in composed observable queries. Opted-in sources read their full filtered results without client paging or sorting and do not update `TotalItems`; the primary observation keeps its paging, sorting and filtered count. Existing composed queries must opt in on their auxiliary observations to get this fix; the existing overloads remain available (#2756)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.15.0)): `@noAutoMap` now works on model-bound child and nested types, as in Chronicle for .NET. A child or nested definition stops auto-mapping event properties when its own type, or the type that declares it, carries `@noAutoMap`. The policy applies to that type's direct child and nested definitions and doesn't cascade further.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.15.0)): `@noAutoMap` on an individual property of a child or nested type now excludes that property from auto-mapping. Previously it was ignored.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.39.0)): Paired conformance checks against Arc for .NET 22.23.0 for commands whose handlers return `OneOf`/`Result` alternatives: a DTO or primitive answers 200 with it as `response`, a validation branch answers 400, a denial 403, a tuple's validation result replaces its response, and no discriminator is added to the wire. An arbitrary `Result` error DTO in .NET is an ordinary 200 response; in TypeScript, return it with `response(...)` to get the same result, or use `rejected(...)`/`denied(...)` for failures. The suite now runs 279 checks.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.9.0)): Decision reads: `IDecisionReads.Get<T>(key)` returns a read model instance (or its absence) with an opaque guard, and the unit of work's commit is rejected if an event that could change what was read has been appended since. Developers never handle sequence numbers. Admitted read models are projections keyed on the event source without joins, children, custom keys or `all`; everything else is refused with a typed reason. Text keys and canonical (lowercase) GUID keys are admitted. `Chronicle.Testing` supports decision reads in-process. This is the client part of the design for #4138.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.9.0)): A unit of work that holds a decision read is completed only by its owner, so application code cannot commit it early and bypass the guard. The ASP.NET Core unit-of-work middleware owns its request's unit of work.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.9.0)): `GetDecisionConflicts` explains a rejected guarded append without exposing sequence numbers.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.38.0)): Opt-in, tenant-scoped observation for Drizzle read models: `withDrizzle({ observation: DrizzleObservation.InProcess })`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.38.0)): `DrizzleReadModels.observe(filter?)` and `observeById(key)` return a live RxJS observable with `current()`, which model-bound observable queries can return and serve over SSE and WebSocket like any observable query.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.38.0)): `observePage(filter, options)` emits `QueryPage` results for low-level `defineObservableQuery` definitions; generated proxies don't support observable pages yet.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.38.0)): `DrizzleHandle.notifyChanged(tableOrReadModelType)` announces a write. Inside an Arc command, notifications from the command and its nested commands are published once, when the outermost command finishes (also when it fails). Outside a command they are published immediately.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.38.0)): The proxy generator recognizes query results whose class derives from an RxJS `Observable` or `Subject`, such as the observables returned by MongoDB and Drizzle read models, and asks for an `Observable<...>` annotation when it can't resolve the item type.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.37.0)): `ChronicleQueryScenario.for(Model, 'method', ...artifacts)` runs a model-bound query against Chronicle read models without a kernel. Seed state per event source with `given.forEventSource(id, tenant?)`: `.events(...)` builds reducer-backed read models with the Chronicle SDK's `ReadModelScenario`, and `.readModel(instance)` pins an instance, which wins over seeded events. Reads are tenant-scoped, and an unseeded key resolves as missing.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.37.0)): Listing, watching and projection-backed history need `ChronicleKernelScenario`, and the scenario says so rather than returning a partial answer.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.37.0)): `snapshotStreamSource(result)` in `@cratis/arc.core` returns the stream a snapshot query returned by mistake, so a test host can release it.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.36.0)): ARCCHR0006 in `@cratis/eslint-plugin-arc-core`'s `recommended-type-checked` preset. It reports a Chronicle `@reactor()` event handler that returns an Arc `@command()` without a replay decision: `@onceOnly()` on the reactor or handler, or a `@replay()` handler for the same event. Replaying the reactor would otherwise execute the command again.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.36.0)): It follows `async` handlers, returned arrays and tuples, and helpers in the same class whose result the handler returns, including class-expression reactors.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.36.0)): It does not follow inherited or external helpers, handlers whose event parameter isn't typed as the event class, decorated classes that only come from `.d.ts` files, or commands executed manually through an Arc server. The ARCCHR0006 page lists these limits.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.26.0)): Select a tenant with `ITenantScope.Begin` for commands, validation, queries, and tenant-scoped reads in background work. The selection takes precedence over request tenant resolution inside the current flow. Dispose to restore the previous tenant; disposal is idempotent, and disposing an outer scope early leaves an active inner tenant selected until it ends (#2747)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.26.0)): Custom `ITenantIdAccessor` registrations are preserved whether they are registered before or after Arc. Explicit tenant scopes require Arc's `TenantIdAccessor` as the effective accessor; resolving `ITenantScope` with a custom accessor throws `ExplicitTenantScopeRequiresArcAccessor` instead of silently selecting a tenant that downstream services cannot see.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.26.0)): Independent HTTP requests reset inherited tenant selection when they enter Arc's request pipeline, before authentication and tenant-bound resolution. On ASP.NET Core this covers all application middleware, but not middleware added by another `IStartupFilter` registered before Arc's. Observable emissions use the subscriber's tenant, not a producer's explicit tenant, on every streaming transport. Tasks and timers started inside a tenant scope keep its tenant after the scope is disposed: do not carry that captured context into unrelated work.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.26.0)): Configurable access to the command and query introspection catalogs (`/.cratis/commands` and `/.cratis/queries`) through `Cratis:Arc:Introspection` (`ArcOptions.Introspection`) (#2764):
- **Arc** ([Release v22.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.26.0)): `Enabled` (default `true`): set to `false` to leave both routes unmapped.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.26.0)): `RequireAuthentication` (default `false`): deny anonymous callers. Arc.Core returns 401; ASP.NET Core uses the configured scheme's challenge behavior.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.26.0)): `Roles` (comma-separated, any one grants access, requires `RequireAuthentication`): deny callers without a listed role. Arc.Core returns 403; ASP.NET Core uses the configured scheme's forbid behavior.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.26.0)): `TrustForwardedIdentityHeaders` (default `false`): lets the Microsoft Identity Platform header handler authenticate protected catalog requests; enable it only behind a trusted ingress that replaces those headers.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.35.0)): `/openapi.json` describes `POST <command route>/validate` for every command, with the execute operation's request body, tag, and security requirements. Its responses are the untyped command result envelope, and its operationId is `<command operationId>:validate`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.35.0)): `GeneratedReturn` is exported as a type, so low-level `defineQuery` and `defineObservableQuery` descriptors can declare their return cardinality.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.25.0)): A model-bound command can declare `[BlockOnValidationSeverity(ValidationResultSeverity.Warning)]`, or `Information`, so that warning- or information-level validation failures reject it. The declared severity is the lowest severity that blocks. It applies to HTTP execution, validation, `Provide()` and in-process `ICommandPipeline` execution, and derived command records inherit it. (#2760)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.25.0)): Generated TypeScript proxies carry the declared severity and apply the same threshold on the client. The server stays authoritative. An out-of-range declared severity fails proxy generation.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.34.0)): `ValidationResult.information(message, options?)`, `ValidationResult.warning(message, options?)`, and `ValidationResult.error(message, options?)` construct validation results at a chosen severity.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.34.0)): The options object sets `members`, `state`, `reason`, and `reasonDetail`. `reason` defaults to `rule`, and omitted options are left out of the result.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.34.0)): `state` and `reasonDetail` reach the command and query HTTP result envelopes. `state` must be JSON-serializable.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.34.0)): `ValidationResultOptions` is exported for typing those options.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): Stage accepts Screenplay's typed-context descriptors (Screenplay 4.41) alongside a model's implementation attachments. `LoadedSemanticModel.TypedContextDescriptors`, the matching `ArtifactRenderRequest` members and a new `CratisRendering.Plan` overload carry them from the same compilation. Stage admits descriptor contract revision 1 only, and refuses descriptors that are not ready for wrapper generation, come from another compilation, are malformed or incomplete, with `STAGE-ESM-021`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): Stage generates typed C# contexts from the descriptors for validation, never falling back to `dynamic`. No generated code uses them yet: they are emitted once reducer bodies render. This is part of #151.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.33.0)): `scenario.given.forEventSource(sourceId, tenant?).events(...events)` seeds ordered history for an event source.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.33.0)): When a command reads a reducer-backed read model for that source (through `commandReadModel`, `readModelForValidation` or `provide()`), the scenario builds it from the seeded events. It uses the Chronicle SDK's in-process `ReadModelScenario`, so the kernel isn't needed.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.33.0)): Seeded events never count as events the command appended.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.33.0)): Seeding history needs `@cratis/chronicle` 6.14 or later. It is loaded only when history is seeded, so the package's peer range stays `^6.7.0`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.33.0)): `scenario.given.forEventSource(sourceId, tenant?).readModel(instance)` pins a read model for a source. A pinned instance wins over seeded history.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.24.0)): `QueryScenario<TReadModel>` runs model-bound snapshot queries in-process through the same hosted pipeline as the HTTP endpoint, including argument conversion, authorization, validation, interceptors and owned query scopes. The principal comes from an `ICurrentPrincipalAccessor` registered in `Services`, as with `CommandScenario`. (#2762)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.24.0)): `IQueryScenarioExtender` is a seam for extending query scenarios. `Cratis.Arc.Chronicle.Testing` uses it to seed read models and event history for Chronicle-backed queries, including other read model types the query reads.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.24.0)): Query scenarios materialize lazy results that depend on scoped services before releasing the query scope, so the result stays readable after `Perform` returns.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.32.0 — Chronicle lint rules](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.32.0)): **ARCCHR0003:** a reactor appends to an event store field it got from its own client or runtime, instead of returning the event. The rule cannot prove the store is the reactor's own, and the documentation says so.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.32.0 — Chronicle lint rules](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.32.0)): **ARCCHR0007:** a command's `handle()` or `provide()` appends to the event log directly. That includes stores reached through injected `ChronicleReadModels` or `ChronicleRuntime`, and `.transactional` appends. Such appends escape the command's batch; return the events instead.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.32.0 — Chronicle lint rules](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.32.0)): **ARCCHR0009:** a command property with a secret-looking name that Arc does not mask at runtime, such as `passphrase`, `privateKey`, `accessKey`, `pin`, `otp`, `cvv`, `securityCode` or `authorizationHeader`. It is reported only when `@cratis/arc.chronicle` is installed, skips number, boolean, date and GUID fields, and is silenced by `@notAudited()` or `@pii()`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.32.0 — Chronicle lint rules](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.32.0)): **ARCCHR0010:** a keyless command returns a `Guid` beside an event instead of an event source ID response. This rule needs type information.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.32.0 — Chronicle lint rules](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.32.0)): The Chronicle code analysis page maps every .NET `ARCCHR` diagnostic to a TypeScript rule, a runtime check, or the reason it does not apply.
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): `DataTableForQuery`, `DataTableForObservableQuery`, and `DataPage` show a loading row while the first result is on its way, and a failure row when the query fails or the user is not authorized. A table that is refreshing rows it already shows keeps them visible and marks itself busy (`aria-busy` on the table, `data-busy` on the root). Server exception text is never shown. (#327)
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): `loadingMessage`, `failureMessage`, and `unauthorizedMessage` props on `DataTableCore`, both query tables, and `DataPage`, with provider-wide defaults in `CratisComponentsProvider` `messages.dataTable.loading`, `.failed`, and `.unauthorized`. (#327)
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): `DataTableCore` accepts `status` (`DataTableStatus.Ready`, `Loading`, `Failed`, or `Unauthorized`) so a table over local rows can show the same states. (#327)
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): New stable parts `loading-row`, `loading-cell`, `failure-row`, and `failure-cell` (typed as `loadingRow`, `loadingCell`, `failureRow`, and `failureCell` in `DataTableParts`). The failure cell carries `data-reason`, and the loading and failure text are announced to screen readers through `role="status"` and `role="alert"`. (#327)
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): `PivotViewer` announces its loading state to screen readers, labeled by the new `loadingLabel` prop (default `Loading…`). (#328)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.8.0)): Testing scenarios accept per-run artifacts. Pass `new Defaults(clientArtifactsProvider, serviceProvider)` to `EventScenario` or `ReadModelScenario`, so each test run discovers only the artifacts you give it, not the assembly-wide set.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.8.0)): `EventScenario(Defaults, ICanProvideConstraints?)` discovers constraints from the supplied defaults when no constraint provider is passed.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.8.0)): `ReadModelScenario<TReadModel>.WithProjection(Action<IProjectionBuilderFor<TReadModel>>)` defines a projection inline for a single scenario. It takes precedence over discovered projections and reducers, and it does not leak into other runs.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.31.0 — global command and query filters](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.31.0)): `AuthorizationCommandFilter` and `CommandPipelineFilter` apply to every command. `AuthorizationQueryFilter` and `QueryPipelineFilter` apply to every query snapshot and observable subscription. Register them through `ArcOptions`, the builder's `add…` methods, or the matching decorators.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.31.0 — global command and query filters](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.31.0)): Authorization filters always run before pipeline filters, validators, dependency construction and the handler.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.31.0 — global command and query filters](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.31.0)): A denial is a 403 that never discloses validation results. It wins over invalid input, and severity filtering cannot override it.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.31.0 — global command and query filters](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.31.0)): Command `/validate` runs the same filters without calling `provide()` or `handle()`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.31.0 — global command and query filters](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.31.0)): Observable queries run the filters once, when the subscription is admitted, not for each emission. That includes the health observable.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.31.0 — global command and query filters](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.31.0)): Helpers build filter results, for example `unauthorizedCommandResult` and `unauthorizedQueryResult`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.31.0 — global command and query filters](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.31.0)): Command and query contexts carry `operationName`, the declared name of the operation, the same as `CommandContext.Type` and `QueryContext.Name` in .NET. Framework-built contexts always set it, and it cannot be changed. Filters that rely on it must deny or handle a manually built context without it.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.31.0 — global command and query filters](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.31.0)): Paired .NET and TypeScript HTTP checks cover denied, denied with invalid input, allowed with invalid input, and allowed commands and queries.
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.2)): `ColumnsProps` is exported from `@cratis/components/DataPage` for the props of `DataPage.Columns`. `ColumnProps` from that subpath is unchanged and still describes a table column. (#333)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): Screenplay publishes typed-context descriptors: language-neutral data describing exactly what each code body can see, so providers such as Stage can generate typed wrappers instead of `dynamic` contexts. They cover reducer transitions (a typed `Event` per `on` event using its current generation, and a nullable `State` shaped like the read model), validation and rule code, code policies at each place they are used, and the context of command handlers, which are still not bound. This is the descriptor part of decision 0012 and advances #139.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): `CompilationResult` exposes the descriptors through `TypedContextDescriptors` and `ContextsFor(requirementId)`. `IsWrapperReady` is true only for descriptors from a successful compilation whose types all resolve; a failed compilation publishes only command-handler descriptors, marked not ready. Every descriptor carries the definitions of the concept and composite types it refers to.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): MCP has a paged `typed-contexts` view; `implementation-requirements` items gain a small `typedContext` reference (count and operation ids).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.41.0)): The descriptor contract has its own revision (1), a published vocabulary of portable type tokens and source kinds, and two readable vectors (`typed-contexts-v1.json`, `unbound-handler-context-v1.json`) packaged with the canonical vectors. Canonical executable-model bytes and revisions are unchanged.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.14.0)): `@cratis/chronicle/testing` exports `ReadModelScenario`. Seed history with `scenario.given.forEventSource(id).events(...)`, then read `await scenario.instance` or `await scenario.instanceForEventSourceId(id)`. `wasDeletedForEventSourceId(id)` tells a deleted read model apart from one that never materialized.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.14.0)): Events go through the same reducer dispatch and the same event serialization as production. Concept values are therefore flattened exactly as they are when appended.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.14.0)): The event context carries the same defaults as the kernel, sequence numbers are global in seed order, and each event source is reduced separately.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.14.0)): A reducer that fails is reported with the read model and reducer names and the original error as its cause.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.14.0)): A read model built by a projection is rejected with a clear message; test it against a kernel instead.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.3)): CHR0054 reports a reactor method whose return type Chronicle cannot discover as a handler, with a message about the return type (#4206)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.6.0)): `Chronicle.Observers` - lists what the event store knows about its observers and removes one by id. For the observer whose declaring code is gone: a deleted read model and its projection, or a removed reactor. Removal covers the whole event store, since an observer's definition (and its projection definition, where it has one) is a store-level record. The kernel refuses while the observer is running or still has a subscribed client in any namespace, naming the namespace that blocked it - there is no override. Read model data and sink containers are left untouched.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.13.0)): `eventStore.observers` - lists what the event store knows about its observers and removes one by id. For the observer whose declaring code is gone: a deleted read model and its projection, or a removed reactor. Removal covers the whole event store, since an observer's definition (and its projection definition, where it has one) is a store-level record. The kernel refuses while the observer is running or still has a subscribed client in any namespace, naming the namespace that blocked it - there is no override. Read model data and sink containers are left untouched.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.6.0)): `store.observers` - lists what the event store knows about its observers and removes one by id. For the observer whose declaring code is gone: a deleted read model and its projection, or a removed reactor. Removal covers the whole event store, since an observer's definition (and its projection definition, where it has one) is a store-level record. The kernel refuses while the observer is running or still has a subscribed client in any namespace, naming the namespace that blocked it - there is no override. Read model data and sink containers are left untouched.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): `SemanticEvaluator.EstablishWorld(plan, facts)` establishes a deterministic reference world from ordered existing facts, with no read-model snapshots required. It checks every fact first: a default or null fact array, an unknown event, a payload that doesn't match the event, a missing or blank event-source identity, a source type other than the event's declared producer destination type, or a blank tag returns `SemanticRejected` with category `Contract`. A fact that affects a reducer-backed read model returns `SemanticUnsupported` with capability `Projection`, because reducer transitions are opaque. This is reference semantics and does not replace Chronicle replay. (#253)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.38.0)): A `StateView` slice's specification can use `when append … for`, as long as exactly one command produces the event and so determines the source type. (#262)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.12.0)): `@index()` marks a read-model property to be indexed, including properties in nested objects and in typed child collections. Paths are joined with dots, as in .NET, for example `address.zipCode` and `lines.productId`.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.12.0)): Indexes are registered with the kernel at application startup for declarative projections, model-bound projections, variants and reducers, and through `readModels.register()`. The MongoDB sink creates the indexes.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): Event generations now bind to the executable model. Events with several generations share one contract identity and carry their complete prior revisions, predecessor links and revision-specific property identities. Ordinary references use the current generation. This implements decisions 0011 and 0015 and advances #71.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): Persisted event contracts can advance through an explicit, catalog-revision-checked workspace transaction. Renaming or moving an event preserves its identities across generations; combining a rename with evolution still requires explicit advancement.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): The RegisterProject v2 canonical corpus declares two event generations, removes the duplicated `projectId` from the current event payload and uses the event source as its identity. Single-file, folder, reordered and relocated forms produce the same model. This advances #168.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.11.0)): `waitForCompletion({ timeoutMs, signal })` accepts an `AbortSignal`. Canceling the signal rejects the wait promptly with the signal's reason and cancels the underlying call, including while the client is still waiting for an access token. Other calls that wait for the same token are not affected. The existing `waitForCompletion(timeoutMs)` form and its five-second default are unchanged.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.10.0)): `RejectedChronicleCredentials`: the client now stops retrying and rejects `getEventStore(...)` and later calls when the credentials are rejected. This applies when the token endpoint answers `invalid_client`, `unauthorized_client`, or `invalid_grant`, or the kernel refuses an API key, on three consecutive attempts. Previously a wrong password made `getEventStore(...)` wait forever.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.10.0)): `@childrenFrom(Event, ChildType, identifiedBy?, parentKey?)` accepts the child class as its second argument. Use it under runtimes that emit no type metadata, such as `tsx`, so the client knows the element type of a children collection.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.10.0)): A root `yarn test` script.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.36.0)): MCP can export the compiled executable model: `read-workspace` view `executable-model` returns its schema, language and semantic versions, model revision and size, plus bounded base64 pages of the exact canonical bytes. It is available only when compilation succeeded; otherwise it reports `available: false` and points to the `executable-diagnostics` view. Continuation pages must pass `expectedModelRevision` and `expectedAttachmentManifestRevision`, and are refused if the model or any attached code changed, including a code edit that changes neither the workspace nor the model revision. The exported model alone is not a proof of equivalence or a runnable attachment bundle (decision 0016).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.36.0)): `implementation-requirements` responses include the attachment-manifest revision. Passing it back pins a continuation and refuses it if attached code changed; continuations without it work as before.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): `set(...).toValue(value)` on the fluent projection builder sets a constant. `toValue(null)` clears the property.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): Passive reducers (`@Reducer(isActive = false)`, or a reducer whose read model is `@Passive`) are folded in the client from the reducer's event sequence on `getInstanceByKey` and `getInstances`, so they return current state.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): `ChronicleConnectionFailed` is thrown by `awaitRegistration()` and by appends that wait for registration when the kernel rejects the connection: wrong credentials, missing permission, or an incompatible server. The client keeps retrying and recovers when the kernel accepts a connection.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): `ChronicleServerIncompatible` (an `IllegalStateException`) is thrown when the compatibility check fails.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): `ArtifactRegistrationFailed` is thrown from an explicit `registerAll()` that could not start one or more reactors or reducers, naming each one.
- **AI** ([Release v7.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.0.0)): A decision engine choice: the built-in Decision Engine or TypeSafe's Jev, selected with `UseBuiltInDecisionEngine` or `UseJevDecisionEngine`. Exactly one engine is in force at a time; until one is chosen, decisions go to the built-in engine
- **AI** ([Release v7.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.0.0)): Jev support: an API key (encrypted at rest and kept out of the causation chain), a model defaulting to `jev-latest` and an endpoint defaulting to `https://api.typesafe.ai`. Questions that share a context are sent as one request
- **AI** ([Release v7.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.0.0)): The built-in engine's address and model are deployment configuration under `Cratis:AI:Decisions:BuiltIn`, bound through `IOptions<BuiltInDecisionEngineOptions>`
- **AI** ([Release v7.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.0.0)): `DecisionEngineSettings.CurrentDecisionEngine` returns the engine in force, its model and a cached health check for a settings page. The API key is never returned
- **AI** ([Release v7.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.0.0)): Decision usage: `RecordDecisionUsage` and the `DecisionUsageByDay` read model, with `DecisionUsageForLastThirtyDays`
- **AI** ([Release v7.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.0.0)): A `DecisionRequest` can carry the question being asked, a description of each choice and a topic. The Decision Engine includes the question and descriptions in its prompt; without them it keeps the generic question
- **AI** ([Release v7.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.0.0)): `CratisAIBuilder.WithDecisions()` registers decision making, and `WithDecisions<TResolver>()` replaces how the engine is resolved
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.9.0)): Class-level `@onceOnly()` registers a reactor as not replayable, so the kernel never replays it. The marker is not inherited, as in .NET.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.9.0)): Method-level `@onceOnly()` skips that handler when an event is delivered during a replay.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.9.0)): `@replay()` marks a handler that runs instead of the regular handler for its event type during a replay.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.9.0)): The shared Chronicle documentation shows TypeScript examples for once-only and replay handlers.
- **AI** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): Kubernetes workers can keep their workspace on a volume of their own. Set `Cratis:AI:ContainerRuntime:ScratchStorageClassName` and `ScratchSize`, and each worker gets a volume provisioned from that class, mounted at `/workspace` and removed with the worker. A checkout and its build output then no longer fill the node's disk or compete with other workers on it. Use a storage class that deletes volumes with their claim and binds on first consumer. Leaving either setting unset keeps today's behavior.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.0)): The event-store dashboard, built from projections with composite keys rather than counting rows on demand, with per-namespace figures and drill-down, and key figures under each event store on the landing page (#3731)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.0)): A declarative surface for kernel-owned projections - identified with a `$system.` prefix, owned by the kernel rather than a client, and refused by replay, because replaying one rebuilds state the kernel keeps for itself
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.0)): `ProjectionScope`, so a read model can be built per namespace, once for the whole store, or both. A `Both` declaration lowers to two single-scope definitions, and a global one is keyed with the unset namespace
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.0)): A statistics query surface over the new projections, and the Workbench screens that show it
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.8.0)): `@unique()` marks an event property or an event class as unique. `@removeConstraint(name)` releases a named constraint, can be repeated, and is inherited like the .NET attribute.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.8.0)): The shared constraint pages on the Chronicle documentation site now show TypeScript examples for model-bound constraints.
- **AI** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): `cratis-content-length`: when content stays short, when it earns more length, and where to split it. It has pilot bands for LinkedIn, documents, video, X, Bluesky, Mastodon, community answers, blog articles, tutorials, newsletters, YouTube and release notes, and says which platform limit or soft trigger applies. Each band states how strong its evidence is. It also sets batch-variety targets so a feed doesn't read as one length.
- **AI** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): `cratis-developer-marketing`: plans how developer content earns real engagement and adoption. It covers positioning around what developers evaluate (docs, trials, peers, APIs, limits), hooks without bait, the move from pillar to canonical source to channel-native entry points, community participation, founder and company voice, a sustainable cadence, and a staged measurement framework with a small-sample experiment design.
- **AI** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): The voice and cadence skill gains a catalog of 21 AI-sounding patterns (lexical, syntactic, structural and tonal), each with a script heuristic and the human alternative. It checks a batch for sameness, fixes problems with substance, and includes a meaning-preservation check that handles curly apostrophes. It is explicitly an editing aid, never an authorship detector.
- **AI** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): The feed post skill leads with media that explains, and puts the Cratis mark on every image, document page, GIF frame and video; screenshots are framed, never stamped. It has guidance for adapting a post to other channels, and a dated evidence matrix with a URL for every source.
- **AI** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): Both new skills are in the `cratis/content` profile.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.35.0)): MCP can propose a fix for a diagnostic: `read-workspace` has a `repairs` view listing the compiler-authored repairs for the workspace's diagnostics (code, subject and typed operations), and `propose-repair` turns one into a retained proposal. Review it with `read-proposal` and write it with `apply`; nothing is written before that, and a stale workspace revision is refused with a typed conflict.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.35.0)): The first repair migrates a deprecated `validate csharp` block (`PLAY0397`) to a tagged ```` ```csharp ```` fence. The code body, its requirement identity and content hash, and the executable model are unchanged. The repair reprints the whole file canonically, so it is refused if that would drop a comment. Other `PLAY0397` forms are not offered a repair.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.35.0)): Library hosts can find and propose repairs through `WorkspaceDiagnosticRepairs.Find` and `ProposeRepair`, which require canonical formatting (`RequiredFormatting`).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.35.0)): Adding a node whose references don't resolve is refused under the safe reference policy, which is how decision 0014 covers "link" in #138.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.34.0)): Events can declare their generations: `event ProjectRegistered generation 2`. An event without the marker is generation 1 and prints as before. Each earlier generation is a complete declaration in the same slice, numbered consecutively from 1; an invalid, duplicate or missing generation is a compile error (`PLAY0446`, `PLAY0447`, `PLAY0448`). Printing, workspace and MCP authoring, and editor highlighting, completion and hover support the marker. This is the grammar part of decision 0011 for #71.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.34.0)): A model that uses the marker does not compile to the executable model yet: binding fails with `PLAY0449` until generation lineage ships (decision 0015). Models without the marker are unchanged.
- **cli** ([Release v3.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.19.0)): `chronicle observers remove` — removes an observer whose declaring code is gone (a deleted read model and projection, or a removed reactor), clearing its definition, state, handled counts and failed partitions across every namespace of the event store. Asks for confirmation first, stating what is deleted, that it reaches every namespace, that read models and their data are left alone, and that it cannot be undone. The kernel refuses while the observer is running or a client is still subscribed to it, and the command reports that refusal rather than reporting success.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): `QueryPagingRequired` is returned as a 400 `rule` validation result on `Size` in two cases:
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): an unpaged MongoDB or Drizzle query reads more than the provider's maximum page size;
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): a client asks for a page larger than that maximum.
- **cli** ([Release v3.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.18.0)): `cratis render` reads the implementation files a model references (`file ...` on policies, validation, reducers and other code) from the model folder, or from a single `.play` file's folder, and gives their contents to the Screenplay compiler, so file-backed code is identified by content. (#184)
- **cli** ([Release v3.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.18.0)): A file the loader refuses (outside the model folder, a link, missing, too large or not text) is reported as `PLAY0430`–`PLAY0434`, even when compilation fails. (#184)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.6.0)): `[NotProjected]` declares a read model member that the projection deliberately does not populate, because whatever reads the model assembles it. A projection may only consume events, never another read model, so a value joined in from a second read model can only be filled in by the query that serves it — and there was previously no way to say so. Declaring it also fences AutoMap off the member, so a subscribed event carrying the same name can no longer write over the assembled value
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.28.0 — Chronicle personal data in directly read models](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.28.0)): Chronicle read models that contain `@pii()` or `@encrypted()` data are decrypted before a query serves them, when the query reads them directly from their MongoDB collection through `MongoCollection`. This covers nested properties, array items, class-level markings, single results, arrays, pages and observable emissions.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.28.0 — Chronicle personal data in directly read models](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.28.0)): If decryption fails, the query or emission fails instead of serving encrypted values.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): Screenplay can show which read-model instances each projection event affects, derived from the executable model with no new syntax: one instance by key (`from`, removal), one per event source (`all`), or many through a root join (instances whose join property equals the event source id, never creating one) or a child join and `remove via join` (children whose identity equals the key, across every parent). `SemanticAffectedProjectionInstances.GetAffectedInstances` returns the relationships per event and block, with an overload that uses the application's types to resolve a child identity. This is the first part of decision 0007 for #132.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.5.0)): Removing an observer and everything the event store keeps for it — its definition, its state and handled counts in every namespace, its failed partitions, its in-flight events and, for a projection, its projection definition. Refuses while the observer is running or still has a subscribed client in any namespace, naming the namespace that blocked it. Read model data is left untouched (#4151)
