- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.5.0)): `EventStore.Observers` on the .NET client, for listing the observers of an event store and removing one (#4151)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.5.0)): A **Remove** action on the Workbench observers page, with a confirmation that states what removal deletes, what it leaves alone and that it cannot be undone (#4151)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.17.0)): Render inputs can carry a Screenplay model's implementation attachments: the compiler's requirements and each resolved body, keyed by requirement id. Inline bodies come from the source; file bodies are loaded through Screenplay's `AttachmentFiles` from the model root. `LoadedSemanticModel` exposes the requirements, the bodies and the loader's attachment diagnostics, and new `CratisRendering.Plan` overloads accept them.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.17.0)): Planning verifies every supplied body against its requirement's content hash. A body the model references that is missing, refused by the loader, or changed since compilation fails with `STAGE-ESM-020`, which names the requirement and the loader's reason (`PLAY0430`–`PLAY0434`), and no artifacts are produced.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.10.0)): Jobs can run their steps in ordered stages. Give a step a stage with `CreateStep<TJobStep>(request, stage)` (or `JobStepDetails.Stage`): every step in a stage reaches an outcome before any step in a later stage starts, and the steps within a stage still run in parallel. A job that never assigns a stage has every step in `JobStepStage.First` and runs exactly as before. (#27)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.10.0)): A stage with a failed step stops the job by default. Every step behind it is recorded with the new `JobStepStatus.Unreachable` status and counted in `JobProgress.UnreachableSteps`, and the job ends `CompletedWithFailures` because it can account for every step. Override `GetFailureBehaviorFor` to return `JobStageFailureBehavior.ContinueWithNextStage` for a best-effort stage. (#27)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.10.0)): `JobProgress.Stages` reports the progress of each stage. It stays empty for a job with a single stage. (#27)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.10.0)): A resumed job continues from the stage it had reached. This includes a job that a stopped silo left between two stages, whether that was after one stage completed or at a failed barrier that nothing had acted on yet. (#27)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.10.0)): `Job.KeepAfterCompletedWithFailures` lets a job remove its persisted state after completing with failures. The default is `true`, which keeps today's behavior. A job that runs on a short cadence can override it so it does not keep a record of every failed run.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.27.0 — Drizzle read models in commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.27.0)): A command that declares `@inject(commandReadModel(Model))` receives the Drizzle row whose primary key equals the command key. The row is read from the current tenant's database.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.27.0 — Drizzle read models in commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.27.0)): `DrizzleReadModels.findById(key)` loads a row by the table's single primary key. Arc's own custom columns get a typed key, and plain columns get a primitive. Invalid number and GUID keys are rejected with a clear error.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.27.0 — Drizzle read models in commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.27.0)): `DrizzleReadModelForCommandResolver` is exported.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): `read-workspace` has a paged `source-map` view: the compiler's map from each semantic id to its role, identity origin, document and exact source span. When compilation did not succeed it reports `available: false` and points to the `executable-diagnostics` view instead of returning an empty map.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): `find-fixtures` finds the payload values and `for` destinations of `when append` events, with the role `whenAppendedEvent`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): Compact specification summaries show a `then denied` assertion and the appended event of a `when append` specification.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): Reaction triggers can declare the views they decide from: `reads <View> [as <alias>] [by <trigger value>]`, with the same alias rules as command reads. Parsing, validation, printing, the syntax walker, MCP references, and editor highlighting, completion and hover all support it.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): Diagnostics for trigger reads: an unknown view, a repeated view without an alias, a duplicate alias, an alias that collides with a trigger value, a `by` that names no trigger value (`PLAY0442`, warning), a `by` under a clock trigger (`PLAY0443`, error), and a primitive type read as a view (`PLAY0444`, warning, pointing to `@reads`).
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.26.0 — MongoDB watcher and joined observations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.26.0)): `mongoDBWatcher` observes changes to a tenant's collections as RxJS observables for application code, the counterpart of .NET's `IMongoDBWatcher`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.26.0 — MongoDB watcher and joined observations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.26.0)): Joined observations re-emit a primary collection's results when it or one or two related collections change, like .NET's joined observe builder.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.26.0 — MongoDB watcher and joined observations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.26.0)): Fundamentals `Point`, `LineString` and `Polygon` values are stored as GeoJSON.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.26.0 — MongoDB watcher and joined observations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.26.0)): MongoDB client metrics through OpenTelemetry for clients Arc creates.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.26.0 — MongoDB watcher and joined observations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.26.0)): Reads retry transient MongoDB errors, at most twice.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.25.0 — kernel-backed Chronicle tests and read consistency](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.25.0)): `ChronicleKernelScenario` in `@cratis/arc.chronicle/testing` runs commands against a real Chronicle kernel: seed events, execute commands through Arc's pipeline, wait for projections through the kernel's own completion signal, and assert on appended events, read models and constraint violations. The in-memory `ChronicleCommandScenario` stays the fast default.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.25.0 — kernel-backed Chronicle tests and read consistency](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.25.0)): `completionTimeoutMs` makes a command wait, within a bound, until Chronicle has processed the events it appended, so a query right after the command sees the result.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.25.0 — kernel-backed Chronicle tests and read consistency](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.25.0)): `ChronicleReadModels` reads can ask for `immediate` consistency for passive projections.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.25.0 — kernel-backed Chronicle tests and read consistency](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.25.0)): Guides for kernel-backed testing and for choosing read consistency.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.24.0 — Core namespaces and clean builds](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.24.0)): `server.validateCommand(...)` and `app.validate(...)` run a command's authorization and validation without executing it, like .NET's command pipeline `Validate`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.23.0 — Chronicle read model queries](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.23.0)): `ChronicleReadModels` reads read models for the current tenant with `getAll(Type)` and `getById(Type, id)`, and observes them as RxJS observables with `observeAll(Type)` and `observeById(Type, id)`, so observable queries over Chronicle read models need no custom plumbing.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.23.0 — Chronicle read model queries](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.23.0)): `cratisArc(arc).close(server)` closes an Express host, draining open SSE and WebSocket connections first.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.22.0 — grouped options](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.22.0)): `createHonoWebSockets` for sharing one Node WebSocket helper between your Hono routes and Arc.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): The opt-in semantic Stage engine (`Stage__Runtime__Engine=semantic`) can start on a Chronicle event log that already holds events. It rebuilds its semantic world only when that can be done exactly: every projection is mirrored exactly and has caught up with its own events without failed partitions, every logged event converts back exactly, and the history satisfies the model's constraints. Otherwise it refuses with a specific `Unsupported(World)` status. (part of #23)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): A render profile can carry the model's `.strings` catalog. Generated validators then resolve `$strings.<key>` rule and requirement messages for the request's UI culture, falling back through its parent cultures to the default locale. The key stays in the validation result's state, and generated rejection specifications compare that key, as the Screenplay reference does. A key missing from the default locale fails planning with `STAGE-ESM-018`. Without a catalog, `$strings` messages are still rejected. (part of #116)
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.21.0 — vertical slices and the Library sample](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.21.0)): The Library sample: an event-sourced Arc and Chronicle application with a React frontend using generated proxies. Commands return events, a Chronicle constraint keeps author names unique, projections build the read models, and observable queries update the frontend live. A Docker Compose file starts the Chronicle kernel.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.21.0 — vertical slices and the Library sample](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.21.0)): A vertical slices guide: one file per slice holds the command, its validator and its events, or the read model with its queries and projection, as in Cratis C# applications. Discovery, proxy generation, generated artifact metadata and the ESLint rules all support several artifacts in one file.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.20.0 — Fetch runtimes](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.20.0)): `@cratis/arc.core/fetch`, an entry for runtimes that speak the Fetch API: build an application from explicitly added artifacts (or generated artifact metadata) and serve commands, queries and observable queries over server-sent events with `app.fetch(request)`. Its only platform import is `node:async_hooks`, which Deno, Bun, Cloudflare Workers (with Node.js compatibility) and Next.js provide.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.20.0 — Fetch runtimes](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.20.0)): Verified on Deno 2.9: a command, a query and an observable query over SSE. Bun, Cloudflare Workers and Next.js route handlers have examples but are not yet verified.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.19.0 — setup, configuration and host integrations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.19.0)): `builder.withChronicle(...)`, `builder.withMongoDB(...)` and `builder.withDrizzle(...)`, named like .NET's `WithChronicle` and `WithMongoDB`. Importing an integration package adds its method to the builder through a typed extension that also works when two copies of `@cratis/arc.core` are installed.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.19.0 — setup, configuration and host integrations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.19.0)): Configuration like .NET's: `ArcApplication.createBuilder()` reads the `Cratis:Arc`, `Cratis:Chronicle` and `Cratis:MongoDB` sections from `appsettings.json` and `appsettings.{Environment}.json`, and from `Cratis__...` environment variables. Values are converted per setting, .NET key names such as `CorrelationId:HttpHeader` and `GeneratedApis:EnableQueryHttpMethod` are accepted, other sections are ignored, and options set in code win. Use `configuration: false` to opt out or `configuration: { file, env }` to choose the source.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.19.0 — setup, configuration and host integrations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.19.0)): `@cratis/cratis`, the counterpart of the C# `Cratis` package: `CratisApplication.createBuilder()` or `builder.addCratis()` sets up Arc with Chronicle in one call. Unlike C#, it installs no authentication handler; choose one explicitly.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.19.0 — setup, configuration and host integrations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.19.0)): Express: `app.use(cratisArc(arc))` and `cratisArc(arc).injectWebSocket(server)` for observable queries over WebSockets.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.19.0 — setup, configuration and host integrations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.19.0)): Fastify: `await app.register(cratisArc, { arc })` registers HTTP routes and WebSockets in one call, in either order with `@fastify/websocket`, and respects mount prefixes.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.19.0 — setup, configuration and host integrations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.19.0)): Hono: `app.use(cratisArc(arc))` with `cratisArc(arc).injectWebSocket(server)`, or `serveCratisArc(app, arc, { port })`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.19.0 — setup, configuration and host integrations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.19.0)): `app.fetch(request)` and `app.handle(request)` on the built application, usable as detached functions, for hosts that speak the Fetch API. Only Node.js is verified.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.18.1 — package contents](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.18.1)): Every package has a README that describes what it contains and links to its documentation.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.18.0 — RxJS observable queries](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.18.0)): Observable queries can return RxJS subjects and observables, just as a C# query returns `ISubject<T>`. A `BehaviorSubject` answers plain HTTP requests with its current value (200), while a `Subject`, `ReplaySubject` or `Observable` without a current value answers 202 or waits, as in .NET. Completion, errors and client disconnects end the subscription. Async iterables keep working.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.18.0 — RxJS observable queries](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.18.0)): The proxy generator and generated artifact metadata recognize RxJS `Observable`, `Subject`, `BehaviorSubject` and `ReplaySubject` return types as observable queries.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.18.0 — RxJS observable queries](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.18.0)): `observeIterable()`, `observeByIdIterable()` (MongoDB) and `watchIterable()` (Chronicle) for code that prefers async iterables.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.18.0 — RxJS observable queries](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.18.0)): `arc-proxygenerator --watch` reports `Watch ready` once the first generation has finished and the watcher is armed, and notices edits to referenced files outside the artifacts folder.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.17.0 — OpenAPI results and proxy improvements](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.17.0)): `/openapi.json` describes results: command and query result envelopes with the typed response or data (from generated artifact metadata), paging on paged queries, and the 200, 400, 403 and 500 responses, plus 202, 408 and 503 for observable queries.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.17.0 — OpenAPI results and proxy improvements](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.17.0)): GET query parameters, including paging and sorting on queries that return collections, and operation summaries from JSDoc comments.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.17.0 — OpenAPI results and proxy improvements](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.17.0)): Bearer security schemes for operations authenticated by `jwtBearer()`, and an `openApiVersion` option for the document's `info.version`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.17.0 — OpenAPI results and proxy improvements](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.17.0)): Generated proxies for two models with the same class name in different namespaces, using import aliases.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.17.0 — OpenAPI results and proxy improvements](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.17.0)): Identity details types from `@identityDetailsProvider()` classes are generated even without an endpoint that returns them.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.17.0 — OpenAPI results and proxy improvements](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.17.0)): Generated query proxies carry the query's HTTP method preference, and command proxies carry warning handling, as with Arc on .NET's generator.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): A conformance suite runs every canonical-corpus specification through Screenplay's reference runner, Stage's semantic engine, and the specifications of the rendered application, and checks that all three agree wherever each can execute it. (part of #15)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.7.0)): Read models are recognized from the projections, reducers and model-bound projection decorators that build them, the way the .NET client discovers them, so a read-model class needs no extra decorator. Use `static readModelId` on the class to choose a custom identifier.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.7.0)): Event types can be constructed naturally, for example `new TaskCreated(title)`: an event class with a constructor and `@field` properties serializes, generates its schema and deserializes correctly in both standard and legacy decorator modes.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.16.0 — Chronicle aggregates and reactor commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.16.0)): `AggregateRoot` and `@inject(commandAggregate(Author))`: a command receives the aggregate for its key, rehydrated from the event log on the command's route. Events passed to `apply()` join the command's batch and are appended when the command succeeds, with a concurrency scope on the event source so a concurrent change is rejected. Aggregates without handlers work too, and handlers are registered per event type with an optional event context.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.16.0 — Chronicle aggregates and reactor commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.16.0)): Reactor commands: a Chronicle reactor can return Arc commands, which run through Arc's pipeline in the event's namespace with causation from the event. A failed command fails the reactor partition.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.16.0 — Chronicle aggregates and reactor commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.16.0)): Transactional commands: returned events can be combined with command operations again. If Chronicle rejects the append, the operations are compensated; if the outcome is unknown, the command is reported as indeterminate.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.16.0 — Chronicle aggregates and reactor commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.16.0)): `readModelForValidation()` gives validator predicates the read model for the command's key.
- **AI** ([Release v6.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.1.0)): The GitHub Copilot provider: device-flow sign-in, API-token exchange, harness support, and model listing. It was left behind when the provider backend moved into the package, so Copilot could not be configured at all through `Cratis.AI` - every other vendor could.
- **AI** ([Release v6.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.1.0)): `CopilotCredential.ExpiresAt`, which the device-flow refresh uses to tell when a token has run out.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.6.0)): `reactorResultHandler` in `ChronicleOptions` lets an application handle values a reactor returns that are not events, for example commands for a CQRS framework to execute. The handler receives the returned value, the triggering `EventContext`, the reactor type, the event store name and the namespace, and returns `true` when it handled the whole result or `false` to let Chronicle append the events it recognizes. A handler that throws fails the partition, so a side effect is never acknowledged without having run.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.15.0 — generated artifact metadata](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.15.0)): `arc-proxygenerator --metadata <file>` writes a metadata module from the same analysis that generates proxies: parameter bindings for `handle()`, `provide()` and `@query` methods (services by class, query arguments by name and type, provided values and markers such as `commandContext()`, `abortSignal()` and `commandReadModel()`), result shapes, field options and validator targets. It is the counterpart of Arc on .NET's source-generated metadata.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.15.0 — generated artifact metadata](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.15.0)): `builder.useGeneratedMetadata(metadata)` applies it. Explicit decorator arguments still win, and a stale module fails at startup with a message to regenerate. `--check-metadata` verifies in CI that the committed module matches the source, and `--watch` regenerates it while you develop.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.15.0 — generated artifact metadata](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.15.0)): Nullable service parameters receive `null` when the service is not registered, as in .NET. Unions of several classes and other ambiguous parameters are reported with file and line.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.15.0 — generated artifact metadata](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.15.0)): The Tasks sample uses the C#-shaped commands and queries with generated metadata, and keeps one example with explicit tokens for projects without a generation step.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): The Stage sandbox can run a model on the Screenplay executable semantic model with the opt-in `Stage__Runtime__Engine=semantic`. The default engine is unchanged. (part of #23)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): Commands run through Screenplay's reference evaluator. That covers authorization, validation rules and requirements, `produces when`, destinations, `$context` values and constraints, so the sandbox behaves as the model specifies.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): Accepted events are appended to Chronicle in one atomic call.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): Unauthorized commands return 403. Validation and constraint rejections return 400 with the modeled messages and constraint name. Anything the engine cannot realize exactly returns a typed 501.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): `/stage/semantic/admission` reports which commands, queries, events and projections the semantic engine supports, and why the others are not supported. (part of #23)
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): Model catalog discovery: a provider's published models are read and recorded, so a tier can be resolved from what the vendor actually serves. Includes a background refresh, a `RefreshAvailableModels` command, and an `AvailableAIModelDiscovery` query.
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): ChatGPT subscription credentials - sign-in, storage, expiry-aware refresh, and the classification of whether a provider was given an API key or a subscription record.
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): Claude Code and OpenAI Codex as provider transports. The Anthropic client now routes to Claude Code when the credential is an OAuth token, which the Messages API will not accept.
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): Provider pools with failure- and capacity-aware member selection, plus `PoolMemberSelector.Candidates` for callers that fail over rather than picking once.
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): Rate-limit cooldowns, per-provider usage ceilings, configurable concurrency, and usage snapshots.
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): `IDefaultAgentInvocationModes`, which says whether a purpose runs as a job or a chat when no configured agent claims it.
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): Generated TypeScript proxies for all of the above, published in `@cratis/ai`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.14.0 — SQL read models with Drizzle](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.14.0)): `builder.addDrizzle({ database | databaseFactory, dialect })`, or the standalone `addDrizzle(builder, options)` function, with a Drizzle database per tenant. A single `database` serves only the default tenant, and a missing tenant fails instead of reading shared data.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.14.0 — SQL read models with Drizzle](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.14.0)): `drizzleDatabase()` injects the tenant's Drizzle database into commands and queries, and `drizzleReadModel(Model)` injects a read-only handle for a read model with `find`, `findOne` and `queryPage`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.14.0 — SQL read models with Drizzle](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.14.0)): `queryPage` pushes Arc paging and sorting down to SQL with a count and a page-size cap. Only the read model's declared fields that are table columns can be sorted or selected; other fields answer 400.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.14.0 — SQL read models with Drizzle](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.14.0)): Column codecs for concepts, Guids, `DateOnly`, `TimeOnly`, `TimeSpan` and JSON on PostgreSQL, MySQL and SQLite, mapped from Arc field metadata.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.14.0 — SQL read models with Drizzle](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.14.0)): Guides for getting started, column types and conversions, tenancy, read-only access and paging, including how the Drizzle choice compares with Kysely, Prisma and TypeORM and which Entity Framework Core features are not ported.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.13.0 — observability, interceptors and derived types](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.13.0)): OpenTelemetry tracing and metrics through `@opentelemetry/api`, using Arc on .NET's `Cratis.Arc` source, span names and tag names for commands, command filters, queries and query filters, plus spans for HTTP handling, observable subscriptions and identity resolution, and duration histograms. Spans carry the correlation id. Install and configure any OpenTelemetry SDK in your application; without one, tracing is a no-op.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.13.0 — observability, interceptors and derived types](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.13.0)): `QueryRenderer` to turn a query result into what is sent (the first renderer that can render a result wins), and `ReadModelInterceptor` to adjust read models before they are sent, including every emission of an observable query. Both are registered on the builder or discovered, and run in the request or subscription scope.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.13.0 — observability, interceptors and derived types](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.13.0)): Derived types on the wire: a field declared with a base type accepts and returns classes marked with Fundamentals `@derivedType('id')`, using .NET's `_derivedTypeId` discriminator, and JSON Schema in introspection and OpenAPI describes the alternatives.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.13.0 — observability, interceptors and derived types](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.13.0)): `@field(Number, { namedFloats: true })` accepts `"NaN"`, `"Infinity"` and `"-Infinity"` for a field, matching .NET's named floating-point literals.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.6.0)): `Orleans.Storage.Sql.Specs`. There was no specs project for the SQL provider at all, which is why a provider that could not create its own schema looked healthy. The specs run against a real SQLite database rather than substitutes - a mocked `DbContext` never touches a schema, so it cannot see this class of defect.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.6.0)): An end-to-end integration spec for a job with a genuinely failing step, against a real silo and a real MongoDB, asserting the failed step stays readable.
- **AI** ([Release v5.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v5.0.0)): `TierModelAssignment`, which infers the tier ladder from a provider's own catalog: the words vendors use at the small and large ends of their range, with the highest version winning inside a rung.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.12.0 — Chronicle event sourcing](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.12.0)): `builder.addChronicle({ client | connectionString, eventStore, ... })`, or the standalone `addChronicle(builder, options)` function. Each tenant uses its own Chronicle namespace, and discovered event types, projections, reducers and reactors are registered with the event store.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.12.0 — Chronicle event sourcing](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.12.0)): Events returned from a command's `handle()` are appended after the command succeeds: a single event, an array of events, `tuple(...)` with a client response, or `eventForEventSourceId(...)` for another event source. The event source id comes from the command key (`@key()`, `getKey()` or a key resolver), and `eventSourceIdResponse(id)` lets a command choose it explicitly.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.12.0 — Chronicle event sourcing](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.12.0)): `@eventSourceType`, `@eventStreamType`, `@eventStreamId`, `@eventSubject` and tags route appended events, and `eventsWithConcurrencyScopes(...)` sets expected sequence numbers, including "before first". Constraint and concurrency violations come back as validation results.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.12.0 — Chronicle event sourcing](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.12.0)): Events returned from nested commands in the same request are appended in one batch with the outer command's events, only when the outer command succeeds.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.12.0 — Chronicle event sourcing](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.12.0)): `@inject(commandReadModel(Author))` gives `handle()` or `provide()` the read model for the command's key, from Chronicle (`ChronicleReadModelForCommandResolver`) or MongoDB, with `{ optional: true }` for a model that may not exist. Ownership conflicts between resolvers fail at startup.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.12.0 — Chronicle event sourcing](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.12.0)): `ChronicleReadModels` for reading Chronicle read models in queries, including observable queries.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.12.0 — Chronicle event sourcing](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.12.0)): `@notAudited()` keeps a command property out of the causation chain.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.12.0 — Chronicle event sourcing](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.12.0)): `@cratis/arc.chronicle/testing` with `ChronicleCommandScenario`, an in-memory event log and `shouldHaveAppendedEvent(Event, eventSourceId?, predicate?)`, scoped to each execution.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `CodeBlockSyntax.BodyStartOffset`, `BodyEndOffset`, `BodyStart`, `BodyEnd` and `BodyLines`: the body's span in the document (UTF-16 offsets, BOM-less, as editors address text) and, for each body line, the original line and the column where its content starts after the common indentation is removed. This covers fenced bodies with a language info string and the legacy tag-line form, CRLF documents, tabs, blank and under-indented lines, and bodies at the end of a file.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `SemanticImplementationRequirement.BodySpan` and `BodyLines` (`SemanticImplementationBodySpan`, `CodeBlockSourceLine`): the same map for each implementation attachment. A file attachment whose contents were supplied spans the whole file; an unresolved file attachment and an inline body without parser positions have no span.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): The MCP `implementation-requirements` views (workspace and proposal) return `bodySpan` and `bodyLines`, run-length encoded where consecutive lines share a column, so a long body stays within a page.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.11.0 — authorization policies and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.11.0)): Named authorization policies: register a policy class (resolved from the execution scope with constructor injection) or a function with `builder.addAuthorizationPolicy('Name', policy)`, and require it with `@authorize('Name')` or `@authorize({ policy, roles })` on commands, read models and query methods. A policy receives the principal, the target and the resource, can be asynchronous, and an unknown policy fails at startup.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.11.0 — authorization policies and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.11.0)): `@authorize({ schemes: [...] })` selects which Arc authentication handlers may authenticate a caller for an operation.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.11.0 — authorization policies and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.11.0)): `jwtBearer({ jwksUri, issuer, audience, ... })` validates bearer tokens with `jose`: asymmetric algorithm allowlist, issuer, audience, lifetime and clock skew, and cached JWKS with configurable timeouts. Requests without a Bearer header are left anonymous for other handlers and anonymous operations.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.11.0 — authorization policies and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.11.0)): `microsoftIdentityPlatform()` authenticates callers from Azure App Service and Container Apps authentication headers (`x-ms-client-principal`, `-id`, `-name`), stripping caller-supplied identity claims the same way Arc on .NET does. It only reads these headers when you register it.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.11.0 — authorization policies and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.11.0)): `@identityDetailsProvider()` lets discovery find the identity details provider, as Arc on .NET does, and model-bound identity details types describe `/.cratis/identity-details/schema`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.11.0 — authorization policies and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.11.0)): The `development` tenant source, matching .NET's development tenant resolver.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.11.0 — authorization policies and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.11.0)): New documentation for authorization and identity (`Documentation/identity`) and tenant resolvers (`Documentation/tenancy`).
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.10.0 — ESLint rules for Arc artifacts](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.10.0)): `@cratis/eslint-plugin-arc-core` for ESLint 10 flat config, with a `recommended` config and a `recommended-type-checked` config for projects that lint with type information.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.10.0 — ESLint rules for Arc artifacts](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.10.0)): Rules that carry Arc on .NET's diagnostic ids where the check applies to TypeScript: ARC0002, ARC0003, ARC0004, ARC0005, ARC0010, ARC0012, ARC0014, ARC0015 and ARC0019. Each id has a documentation page with the reason, a failing example and the fix, and every other .NET id is listed with why it does not apply.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.10.0 — ESLint rules for Arc artifacts](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.10.0)): TypeScript-specific rules for the decorator programming model: properties without `@field`, decorated `declare` fields, `@inject` tokens and `@query` descriptors that do not match the method's parameters, decorators placed where they have no effect, artifacts that are not exported, and validators without a `@validator(Target)` target. An opt-in rule checks that query argument names match parameter names.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.10.0 — ESLint rules for Arc artifacts](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.10.0)): Arc decorators are recognized through their imports from `@cratis/arc.core` and `@cratis/fundamentals`, including aliased and namespace imports, so decorators with the same names from other libraries are not reported.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): A `policy` with a `csharp` body or a `file` binds as an opaque policy-predicate attachment with a stable requirement id and the capability `pure` (`SemanticOpaquePolicyCondition`). A model that has one is ESM v3, like models with reducer bodies or code validation; other models keep their bytes.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): `SemanticExecutionCapability.Authorization`, reported when authorization cannot be decided without running policy code.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): PLAY0440: a policy combines `require` with an inline code block or a file. A policy has one form or the other.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): PLAY0441: a policy has more than one `require` line. Combine the conditions with `and`/`or` in one `require`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): Proposed decision record 0005: policy predicates as an implementation-attachment role, and authored-order composition.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.12.0)): A planned artifact now names the semantic identities it realizes: `PlannedArtifact.Sources` is a sorted, distinct list of slice, command, event, read-model, projection, query, concept, type and specification ids. Scaffold artifacts have no sources. Artifact bytes and SHA-256 hashes are unchanged, so a render plan maps every artifact to both its semantic origin and its hash. (#11)
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.9.0 — testing scenarios](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.9.0)): `CommandScenario.for(RegisterAuthor)` runs a decorated command through the real pipeline (authorization, validation, `provide()`, `handle()`) with services you override (`scenario.services.addSingleton/addScoped/addTransient`), a principal, tenant and context values, and a serialization round trip through the wire format by default. `execute(...)` and `validate(...)` return results with assertions such as `shouldBeSuccessful()`, `shouldHaveValidationErrors()`, `shouldHaveValidationErrorFor(message)`, `shouldHaveValidationErrorForMember(member)`, `shouldNotBeAuthorized()` and `shouldHaveExceptions()`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.9.0 — testing scenarios](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.9.0)): Command operation assertions on scenario results: executed, compensated and indeterminate outcomes.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.9.0 — testing scenarios](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.9.0)): `QueryScenario.for(ReadModel, 'method')` performs a `@query` method with arguments, paging and sorting through the real query pipeline.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.9.0 — testing scenarios](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.9.0)): `ObservableQueryScenario` collects emissions from an observable query within one explicit deadline and reports whether the stream completed.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.9.0 — testing scenarios](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.9.0)): `shouldHaveValidationErrors()` refuses to pass when the only failures come from unavailable dependencies or failing validators, so a misconfigured test cannot pass for the wrong reason.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.9.0 — testing scenarios](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.9.0)): The Tasks sample includes command and query specs written with the scenarios.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.8.0 — MongoDB integration](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.8.0)): `builder.addMongoDB({ server | client | serverResolver, database | databaseNameResolver, ... })`, or the standalone `addMongoDB(builder, options)` function. Tenants get their own database (`tasks`, `tasks+acme`), and a server resolver can route tenants to different MongoDB servers.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.8.0 — MongoDB integration](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.8.0)): `mongoCollection(Model)` injects a tenant-scoped collection for a read model into a `@query`, with typed `find`, `queryPage`, `observe` and `observeById`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.8.0 — MongoDB integration](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.8.0)): A metadata-driven BSON codec that reads and writes documents the way Arc on .NET does: `@key()` or `id` as `_id`, Guids as UUID binary (subtype 4), concepts as their primitive values, `DateOnly`, `TimeOnly`, `TimeSpan` and `Date` in .NET's formats, nested models and arrays, and `@derivedType` discriminators in `_derivedTypeId`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.8.0 — MongoDB integration](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.8.0)): Naming policies: `defaultMongoNamingPolicy` matches an unconfigured .NET MongoDB builder, and `camelCaseMongoNamingPolicy` matches `.WithCamelCaseNamingPolicy()`. Custom policies are supported.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.8.0 — MongoDB integration](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.8.0)): `queryOptions()` gives a query Arc's paging and sorting options, and `queryPage` pushes them down to MongoDB with a count, a page-size cap and a field allowlist. An unknown sort field answers 400.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.8.0 — MongoDB integration](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.8.0)): `observe(filter?)` and `observeById(id)` turn MongoDB change streams into Arc observable queries, with a current value for plain GET and updates over SSE and WebSocket. Replica sets and sharded clusters are supported; a standalone server is rejected with a clear error.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): Render read-model and query specifications over read models built by scoped projections. Generated specifications replay every produced event in command order through the generated projection, including joins and removals. Replays that cannot be reproduced exactly fail with `STAGE-ESM-011`. (part of #14)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): Render complete given read models in query-only lookup specifications. Given read models before a command are still rejected. (part of #14)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): Render `then events in any order` with several events as a multiset match, checking each event's stream and payload. (part of #14)
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.7.0 — command context, response handlers and operations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.7.0)): Command response value handlers: implement `CommandResponseValueHandler` (`canHandle` and `handle`) and register it or let discovery find it. Every handler that can handle a value returned from `handle()` processes it, in a deterministic order, and a handler failure stops the rest. At most one value left unhandled becomes the client response; more than one fails the command.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.7.0 — command context, response handlers and operations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.7.0)): `CommandContext` for each command execution, carrying the command, its key, context values, correlation id, principal, tenant, allowed severity and cancellation signal. Context value providers and modifiers can add to it.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.7.0 — command context, response handlers and operations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.7.0)): Command keys: mark one `@field` with `@key()`, implement `getKey()` on the command, or register `CommandKeyResolver` rules ahead of the default resolver.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.7.0 — command context, response handlers and operations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.7.0)): Parameter markers for `handle()` and `provide()`: `@inject(abortSignal(), commandContext(), provided(Type))` binds the cancellation signal, the command context and values returned from `provide()` by type.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.7.0 — command context, response handlers and operations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.7.0)): Command operations: `handle()` can return operations (and operation batches) that are checked before any runs, executed in order, and compensated in reverse order within a timeout when the command fails before anything commits. Execution scopes report an explicit commit disposition, an unknown commit outcome is reported as indeterminate rather than success, and recovery details stay on the server. Operations are rejected from nested commands and from commands that ran nested commands, so every effect stays inside one recovery boundary.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.6.0 — proxies from TypeScript source](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.6.0)): `arc-proxygenerator --project <tsconfig> --artifacts <folder> --output <folder>` reads your TypeScript program with the compiler API, without running your application, and writes proxies for the published `@cratis/arc` and `@cratis/arc.react` clients: commands with `use()` hooks, snapshot and observable queries with parameters, paging, sorting, `use`, `useWithPaging`, `useSuspense` and `when`, models, enums and derived types.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.6.0 — proxies from TypeScript source](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.6.0)): Command and query result types come from the return types of `handle()` and `@query` methods, so nothing is declared twice. Field optionality, nullability, defaults and enums come from the same `@optional()`, `@nullable()`, `@defaultValue()` and `@enumeration()` annotations the server uses.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.6.0 — proxies from TypeScript source](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.6.0)): Client-safe validation rules from `@validator(Target)` classes are generated into the proxies. Rules the browser cannot evaluate stay on the server and are reported by the generator.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.6.0 — proxies from TypeScript source](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.6.0)): Routes and query names come from the same discovery walk and route rules the server uses, with `--root-namespace`, `--segments-to-skip`, `--api-prefix`, `--skip-command-name-in-route` and `--skip-query-name-in-route` options named after the .NET generator's settings.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.6.0 — proxies from TypeScript source](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.6.0)): Output management like .NET's generator: ownership headers with content hashes, unchanged files left untouched, stale generated files and barrels removed, hand-written files and barrel lines preserved, plus `--use-proxy-file-suffix`, `--emit-interfaces`, `--skip-index-generation`, `--skip-output-deletion` and `--js-import-specifiers`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.6.0 — proxies from TypeScript source](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.6.0)): The Tasks sample has a `generate-proxies` script.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): `readModels.findInstanceById(type, id)` returns `null` when no instance exists, so callers can tell a missing read model from an empty one.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): `EventSequenceNumber.beforeFirst` for expressing "no events appended yet" as an expected sequence number in a concurrency scope.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): `isRegisteredEvent(store, value)` tells whether a value is an instance of an event type registered with an event store.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): Scoped `run` methods on the identity, causation and correlation managers, so concurrent or nested operations on a server each keep their own ambient context and restore the previous one afterwards.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.10.0)): The semantic specification engine (`--engine semantic`) now runs specifications with a `given caller`, command authorization and `then denied`. Denied specifications check that nothing was appended. (part of #15)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.10.0)): It evaluates command validation rules and requirements at every severity, matching Screenplay's rule order, messages and default messages, and enforces unique-value and unique-event constraints, including releases, casing and re-claiming one's own value. A constraint rejection carries the constraint name as its code. (part of #15)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.10.0)): `when append` specifications that assert only events now run. (part of #15)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.9.0)): Render portable `unique <property> on <Event>` and `unique event <Event>` constraints as named Chronicle constraints, with every target, release, casing rule and message. Generated specifications prove violations, releases, casing and re-claiming one's own value. Constraints on optional, composite or non-text properties, and several events from one command that target or release a unique-value constraint, still fail with `STAGE-ESM-014`. Chronicle updates its constraint index after commit (Cratis/Chronicle#4123), so a storage failure there is not rejected at append time. (#118)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.9.0)): Commands that produce several unconditional events render, and generated specifications assert each event on its stream in order. A specification may now expect several read models and query results. (part of #14)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.9.0)): Specifications with a `given caller` fixture and `then denied` now render. The caller runs through Arc's principal override, and a denied specification checks that the command was unauthorized, that the policy is registered, and that nothing was appended. Protected-query expectations and claim conditions on the role claim type are still rejected. (part of #57)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.9.0)): Render nested `from` and `clear` blocks, supported `every` event-source mappings, and root and child `RemovedWith` / `RemovedWithJoin`. A differential specification now runs admitted projection shapes through Chronicle and compares the results with Screenplay's reference execution. (part of #117)
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.5.0 — model-bound commands, read models and validators](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.5.0)): Model-bound commands: a class decorated with `@command()` declares its input with Fundamentals `@field(Type)` and handles itself in `handle()`. An optional `provide()` loads data after validation and passes it to `handle()`, or short-circuits with `rejected(...)` / `denied(...)`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.5.0 — model-bound commands, read models and validators](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.5.0)): Model-bound read models and queries: a class decorated with `@readModel()` exposes static `@query(...)` methods. `argument(name, Type)` and `service(Token)` describe the parameters in order, and `@query({ observable: true })` serves live results over the same snapshot, SSE, WebSocket and hub transports as `defineObservableQuery`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.5.0 — model-bound commands, read models and validators](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.5.0)): Concepts from `@cratis/fundamentals` (`ConceptAs<T>` with `static readonly valueType`) on the wire: requests decode into concept, `Guid`, `Date`, `DateOnly`, `TimeOnly` and `TimeSpan` values and responses encode back to primitives. Invalid values answer 400 with `malformedRequest`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.5.0 — model-bound commands, read models and validators](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.5.0)): Field options `@optional()`, `@nullable()`, `@defaultValue(value)` and `@enumeration(Enum)`, which also shape the JSON Schema in introspection and OpenAPI.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.5.0 — model-bound commands, read models and validators](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.5.0)): Validators with the same vocabulary as the `@cratis/arc` client: `CommandValidator<T>`, `QueryValidator<T>`, `ConceptValidator<T>` and `ModelValidator<T>` declare rules with `ruleFor(...)` (`notEmpty`, `maxLength`, `emailAddress`, `greaterThan`, `must`, `mustAsync`, `when`, `unless`, `withMessage`, `withSeverity`, `withState` and more) and are bound to their target with `@validator(Target)`. Concept validators apply wherever the concept appears, member paths use client casing, and validators are created per execution scope.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.5.0 — model-bound commands, read models and validators](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.5.0)): Authorization decorators `@roles(...)`, `@authorize()` and `@allowAnonymous()` on commands, read models and query methods. A method declaration overrides its class, stacked declarations must all pass, and contradictory declarations fail at startup.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.5.0 — model-bound commands, read models and validators](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.5.0)): `@path(...)` to override a query route, and `generatedApis` options (`routePrefix`, `segmentsToSkipForRoute`, `includeCommandNameInRoute`, `includeQueryNameInRoute`) for conventional routes derived from the folder a command or read model lives in, matching Arc on .NET.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.5.0 — model-bound commands, read models and validators](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.5.0)): Dependency injection with classes as service tokens: `builder.services.addSingleton/addScoped/addTransient(Token, implementation?)`, `@singleton()`, `@scoped()`, `@transient()`, `@injectable(...)` and `@inject(...)`. Both TC39 standard decorators and legacy `experimentalDecorators` with `emitDecoratorMetadata` are supported; in legacy mode `@inject()` can read parameter types.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.5.0 — model-bound commands, read models and validators](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.5.0)): `ArcApplication.createBuilder(options)` with `discover(folder)` and `add(...)`, `build()`, a blocking `run()` that shuts down on SIGINT or SIGTERM, and `start()` / `stop()` for embedding. Express, Fastify and Hono mounts accept
- **Stage** ([Release v4.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.8.0)): Run Screenplay specifications in memory against the executable semantic model with SpecRunner's opt-in `--engine semantic`. It addresses specifications by semantic id through `--specification` and `--scope`, and writes a `stage-spec-run/1` report. A specification the engine cannot execute exactly is reported `Unsupported` with a typed capability before it runs, so it is never reported as passing; there is no `Inconclusive` outcome. The default structural engine is unchanged. (part of #15)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.8.0)): `Cratis.Stage.Specifications` provides `ISemanticSpecificationExecutor`, and `Cratis.Stage.Contracts` provides the semantic run report and a `.play` source loader. Commands run through Arc's command scenario against a fresh in-memory Chronicle event log for each specification. Results are checked against Screenplay's reference runner on the canonical corpus. (part of #15)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `AttachmentFiles.Load(root, documents)` reads the files named by a model's implementation attachments (reducer rules, validation rules and code validation, command handlers, policies, reactions, query performers, file constraints) from a model root and returns their contents with warning diagnostics for anything it refuses. Pass `Contents` to `SemanticDocumentSet.Create(documents, catalog, contents)`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `ScreenplayWorkspace.WithAttachmentContents(contents, diagnostics)` and a matching `ScreenplayWorkspace.Create` overload carry host-supplied contents through every workspace compilation, proposal and transaction. The workspace itself never reads files, and its revision and serialized bytes do not depend on attachment contents.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): PLAY0430–PLAY0434 warnings for an attachment that is refused or missing: a path that is absolute or leaves the model root, a link, a missing file, a file over 2 MiB (or a set over 8 MiB), and content that is not UTF-8 text. A refused attachment stays unresolved; it never fails a model that compiled before.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.7.0)): Render portable Screenplay policies for commands and queries as registered Arc authorization policies, including module- and feature-level `authorize`. A command or query is `[AllowAnonymous]` only when no authorization applies. Expressions Arc would enforce differently fail with `STAGE-ESM-015`: role-only or claim-only policies that do not require authentication, ambiguous command subjects, and non-text claim targets. (part of #57)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.7.0)): Render all thirteen ESM validation rule kinds, concept validation rules and command requirements as generated validators, with the reference default messages. Warning and information severities, `$strings.` messages, and combinations whose failure order Arc cannot preserve are still rejected. (part of #116)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.7.0)): Render ESM projection scopes: several `from` events, root joins, children identified by a property, and property and arithmetic mappings. Several read models and keyed queries in one state-view slice are now admitted. Nested blocks, `every`/`all`, removals, composite keys, event-context mappings, whole-number arithmetic, and scopes that leave a required property unset fail with `STAGE-ESM-017`. (part of #117)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.7.0)): Render `given` events that state their event source in generated command, read-model and query specifications. Read-model and query assertions without `exactly` now check only the stated properties, as the reference does, and query specifications check projected state. (part of #14)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.7.0)): Generated rejection specifications now check the first validation message against the modeled `then error`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): A named rule with a `csharp` body or a `file` on a command property or a concept (`invoiceNumber rule BeUnusedInvoiceNumber file Validations/BeUnusedInvoiceNumber.cs`), and a command's `validate csharp` block, bind as opaque rule-predicate and code-validation attachments. Each carries what it guards, its message (including `$strings` keys) and severity, a stable requirement id and the capability `pure`. A bare named rule with neither body nor file is still rejected.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): `SemanticCodeValidation`, `SemanticCommand.CodeValidations`, `SemanticValidationRule.Name` and `SemanticValidationRule.RequirementId`, and the `SemanticValidationRuleKind` members `RulePredicate` and `CodeValidation`.
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): `Column.filterOptions` supplies the values a column can be filtered by, rendered as a list instead of a free-form input, with the match modes narrowed to equals/not equals.
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): `selectionMode='multiple'` on the data tables and `DataPage`, with a checkbox per row, a select-all header checkbox, and `selectedItems`/`onSelectedItemsChange`.
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): `dataTable.selectAllRows` message for localizing the select-all accessible name.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): A reducer whose every `on <Event>` rule has a body, inline or in a `file`, binds to a reducer contract in the executable semantic model: the read model it builds, the events it consumes, a key fixed to the event source id, no initial state, and a result that is the new state or a delete, as Chronicle runs reducers. Inline and file bodies bind to the same contract. The body stays opaque code, not portable meaning.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): ESM v3. A model is serialized as v3 only when it contains a bodied reducer; every other model keeps its v1 or v2 bytes exactly. v3 includes all of v2. `LanguageVersion.V3`, `SemanticVersion.V3` and `EsmSchemaV3Support` describe it, and a full v3 golden vector pins its canonical form. Reducer transitions are canonicalized by event, so reordering `on` rules does not change a model's revision.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): Implementation requirements carry `RequirementId` (derived from the owning construct, role and member, never from a path), `ContextVersion` and `ResultVersion`, `RequiredCapability` (`pure` for reducer transitions) and `AttachmentResolution`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): `SemanticDocumentSet.Create(documents, catalog, attachmentContents)` lets a host supply the contents of `file` attachments, which are then identified by a hash of their content. Without supplied contents a file attachment is reported as `UnresolvedFile` with no hash; the file compiler, MCP server and Tool supplying contents is tracked in #244.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): PLAY0398: a reducer mixes rules with and without a body.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): PLAY0399: a reducer has more than one rule for the same event.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): `reads <View> as <alias> [by <property>]` names a read, so a command can read the same view twice, for example `reads Account as source by fromId` and `reads Account as destination by toId`. A `require` path can use the alias as its qualifier: `require source.balance >= amount`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): PLAY0410: a command reads the same view more than once and not every read has an alias.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): PLAY0411: two reads of one command use the same alias.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): PLAY0412: a reads alias has the same name as one of the command's properties.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): `ReadsSyntax.Alias`, an init-only property, carries the alias in the syntax tree, syntax JSON, the printer, the TextMate grammar and the Monaco language package (completion, keyword docs, symbols).
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): Observable queries with `defineObservableQuery`: sources can be async iterables, RxJS-style observables or `CurrentValueSubject.of(value)` / `.pending()`. Every subscription runs the query's authorization and validation and owns a service scope.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): HTTP snapshots of observable queries: 200 with the current value, 202 while no value exists yet, and 408 when `waitForFirstResult` times out.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): Direct server-sent events and WebSocket streams on a query's own route, and the multiplexed `/.cratis/queries/ws` and `/.cratis/queries/sse` hubs used by the `@cratis/arc` client, with subscription revisions, full and delta transfer modes, and keep-alive.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): Emission guards (`observableEmissionGuards`) that can allow, suppress or terminate each emission, and an opt-in `/.cratis/queries/health` endpoint that shows callers their own connections.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): Configurable limits for observable subscriptions, hub connections, queues and frame sizes, per caller and globally, plus `allowedOrigins` for WebSocket upgrades and SSE hub control requests.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): WebSocket support for Express (upgrade listener), Fastify (through `@fastify/websocket`, reusing an existing registration) and Hono (through `@hono/node-ws`, reusing an existing helper).
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): A standalone Node.js host in `@cratis/arc.core` (`runArc`, `createArcNodeHandler`): HTTP or HTTPS, WebSocket upgrades, static files, SPA fallback, a path base, cache validators and a bounded shutdown.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): Generated `ObservableQueryFor` proxies for observable queries.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): Declare event types, read models, projections, reducers, reactors and the other SDK artifacts with TC39 standard decorators, without enabling `experimentalDecorators`. Legacy decorators keep working.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): Schema support for explicitly typed arrays and for Guid-, Date-, and primitive-backed concepts, with a `static readonly valueType` hint for concepts when no decorator type metadata is emitted.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.5.0)): Admit ESM v2 models from Screenplay 4.20. A command with a typed `produces … for` destination appends to that event source without copying the identity into the event. When a specification states `for <value>` on its `when` or `then` event, the generated specification checks the appended event on that stream. It also seeds the projected read model there. (#128)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): A workspace transaction that finds two files declaring the same thing returns a typed `ConflictingOwner` conflict naming both paths, instead of a generic compilation failure. Ownership belongs to the file that declares a member, never to a module or feature header restated to place a child (#137)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): Specs pin the round trip of a commented model between a single document and a folder: same semantic revision, same identities, same canonical print, and every comment kept. A folder write is rejected before touching disk when the source changed since the proposal (#137)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.23.0)): `when append <Event>` specifications: the event goes through append-time constraints and projections, then the stated read models and query results are compared. If the specification asserts events, they must equal the complete set of new facts. Reactions do not run.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.23.0)): Explicit comparison qualifiers on specification outcomes. Event order defaults to authored order and can be relaxed; read-model and query properties default to subset matching and can be made exact. Conflicting actions and malformed qualifiers are reported (`PLAY0358`, `PLAY0359`).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.23.0)): An inventory of the code attachments the executable model cannot run yet: command handlers and validation, concept validation, rule and policy predicates, reducer transitions, query performers and reactions. Each is listed with its role, owning declaration, language or file, content hash and source location. It sits on the compilation result, and MCP pages it by workspace revision. Binding still fails closed with `PLAY0268`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.23.0)): A `policy` may name its implementation with `file <Path>`, as an alternative to inline code. The executable model treats it like a code policy.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.22.0)): Declarative policies bind into the executable semantic model and are evaluated for commands and queries. A command's or query's effective authorization is its own policies, every enclosing feature's and its module's, combined by AND (#142)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.22.0)): Specifications accept a `given caller` block (`authenticated`, `role "<role>"`, `claim "<type>" = "<value>"`; a claim may repeat) and a `then denied` outcome, distinct from `then error`. A denied command or query is rejected as `Unauthorized` before validation runs (#142)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): Modules and features accept `authorize`. It combines by AND with the policies of every command and query beneath them, and folder files merge it like behaviors; a repeated identical clause warns with `PLAY0394` (#75)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): Inline code blocks carry their language as a fence info string (` ```csharp `). The previous forms — a language line above a bare fence, and `validate csharp` — still parse with a `PLAY0397` warning, and printing writes the new form (#184)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): Typed accessors beside every `dynamic` context member (`CommandAs<T>()`, `ArgumentsAs<T>()`, `ArtifactAs<T>()`, …), so LINQ in inline code binds at compile time. A payload of another type throws `ContextPayloadTypeMismatch` (#84)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): Canonical corpus vectors: RegisterProject with reordered and relocated documents (same revision and bytes), a pinned ESM v2 vector, and an unsupported vector that fails closed with no artifacts. The corpus package now runs package validation (#168)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): Documentation gates: every construct the parsers accept must have a grammar production and a reference page; every relative link and anchor must resolve and every page must be reachable from navigation. Documentation-only pull requests now run these checks (#146)
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.3.0 — generated clients](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.3.0)): `exportClientManifest` writes a versioned JSON client manifest from registered commands and queries that declare an explicit `clientOutput` shape.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.3.0 — generated clients](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.3.0)): `@cratis/arc.server.codegen` renders `@cratis/arc` command and query proxies from that manifest, with `renderClientManifest`, `generateClient`, and a JSON-only CLI that refuses unsafe paths, symlinks, and overwrites of files it does not own.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.3.0 — generated clients](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.3.0)): Generated proxies for flat DTOs, basic and optional inputs, command responses, and DTO or array queries, verified against live Express, Fastify, and Hono hosts with the published `@cratis/arc` client.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): Executable semantic model version 2. It is selected automatically when a model uses a v2 construct and is otherwise unchanged at version 1 (#226, #167):
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): Typed state-change destinations: `produces … for <expr>` targets an event source without duplicating the identity into the event.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): Event-source context on facts, which projections read the event-source identity from.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): `for <value>` event-source assertions on `given` and `then` events, so a specification can name exact streams. For example, a unique constraint can be shown colliding between two event sources.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): `$context.occurred` and the caller's identity (`$context.identity.*`) in `produces`, matching Chronicle's event context.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): Projection variants execute. Each variant is its own read model; entering one removes the entity from its siblings, and projection-level handlers apply to every variant that has the property. This follows how Chronicle's variant support reclassifies them. New errors (`PLAY0382`–`PLAY0385`) cover a variant without `enters on`, an entering event claimed twice, and a shared mapping a variant's read model lacks (#185)
