- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): Validation rules and `require` conditions accept a severity: `information`, `warning` or `error` (the default). Every failing rule still rejects the command, and the rejection reports each failure's severity (#227)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): `require` bodies accept a localized `message $strings.<key>` (#231)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.0)): Register scoped, asynchronous authorization policies with `IAuthorizationPolicy` and `AddArcAuthorizationPolicy<TPolicy>()` on either Arc host (#2736).
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.0)): Use ASP.NET Core policies and authentication schemes on model-bound commands, queries, and observable subscriptions, following the [authorization contract](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/blob/main/decisions/0004-evaluate-authorization-policies-asynchronously.md) (#2736).
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.2.0 — services and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.2.0)): Explicit service tokens and singleton, scoped, and transient lifetimes, with owned resource cleanup and real-pipeline testing helpers.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.2.0 — services and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.2.0)): Registry-owned singleton initialization and graceful draining of admitted work without converting committed successes into shutdown failures.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.2.0 — services and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.2.0)): Identity-details endpoints and a size-bounded, Unicode-compatible display cookie that is never used as a credential.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.2.0 — services and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.2.0)): Explicit trusted native-principal integration for Express, Fastify, and Hono, with native TLS handling and preserved routing defenses.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.2.0 — services and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.2.0)): Ordered tenant selection with optional required-tenant and membership enforcement, plus opt-in development user and tenant discovery.
- **Arc.Kotlin** ([Release v7.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Kotlin/releases/tag/v7.6.0)): Opt into `Name.proxy.ts` filenames with `cratisArc.proxies.useProxyFileSuffix` or `--use-proxy-file-suffix true`. Generated imports and barrel exports follow the setting, while external mappings and routes stay unchanged. Switching either direction cleans up owned generated files when cleanup is enabled (#229).
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.1.0 — source preview](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Schema-backed command and query APIs with validation-only execution, authorization, tenant and correlation context, execution scopes, and paging.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.1.0 — source preview](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Express, Fastify, and Hono adapters for hosting Arc APIs on Node.js.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.1.0 — source preview](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Tenant-aware MongoDB read helpers with trusted filtering and database-owned paging.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.1.0 — source preview](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.1.0)): Introspection, basic OpenAPI descriptions, a runnable Tasks sample, and documentation of supported capabilities and limitations.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): Specification values accept typed single-line objects and lists, such as `lines = [{"sku":"A-1","quantity":2}]` and `tags = []`. Keys are checked against the declared composite type (`PLAY0291`–`PLAY0294`). The values bind to executable composite and array values and run end to end (#215, #207)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): Specifications may state a subset of read-model or query-result properties. A query result may omit the identifier its arguments supply. Specifications with only `given` and `then query`/`then readmodel` steps and no `when` run (#207)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): Commands can produce events conditionally (`produces when …`) with conditions over command properties, and can state `require` conditions over command properties. Each condition is evaluated independently (#210, #209)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): Literal tags on events and `produces` travel as append metadata (#210)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): Constraints accept `released by <Event>`, composite properties (`unique a, b on E`), several targets under one name, `message` and `ignore casing` (#212)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): `matches "<regex>"` takes an ECMAScript regular expression, and `matches email` is a named pattern with a documented definition (#209)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): Renaming a composite type's property through MCP rewrites the object keys that name it (#215)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.2.0)): Customize generated hosts through optional `ConfigureServices` and `ConfigureApplication` hooks and a `Customizations/Dependencies.props` import, without editing managed files. Includes a catalog adapter example and a customization guide. Modeled capture, reaction, and `file` bindings remain unsupported. (#121)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.2.0)): Load an optional product-owned `Customizations/styles.css` after component tokens and styles, before rendering the frontend. The renderer never emits files into this directory. (#122)
- **AI** ([Release v3.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.4.0)): The social feed post skill now teaches drafting in a human voice: open with something that happened or the reader's concrete problem, talk the way the people who built it would, show the default shape next to the better one, sell with specifics instead of adjectives, put each limit inside the sentence it limits, keep review and evidence language out of the copy, and stop when you are done.
- **AI** ([Release v3.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.4.0)): The voice and cadence skill recognizes five more tells of generated prose: punchline fragments ("They run."), signpost pairs ("That's the X. Here's the Y."), labeled caveats ("One honest limit:"), aphoristic closes and reflexive triplets.
- **AI** ([Release v3.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.4.0)): The voice and cadence skill explains hedged, noncommittal copy as review language leaking into the material, and how to fix it without dropping a true limitation.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.1.0)): Support scalar nested objects, all-event subscriptions, and recursive projection definitions when loading Screenplay models. (#125)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): The executable semantic model admits validation rules beyond `not empty`: `max`/`min` (length on text, value on numbers), `==`/`!=`, `>`/`>=`/`<`/`<=` on numbers, `length ==`, and `all >`/`all >=` over collections of numbers. The reference executor evaluates them all (#209)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): The executable semantic model admits every projection block the way Chronicle lowers it: several events in one `from`, `children`, `nested`, update-only `join`, `every`/`all`, `remove with`, `remove via join`, composite and literal keys, and the `add`/`subtract`/`increment`/`decrement`/`count`/`clear` mappings. The reference executor runs them all (#211)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): The executable semantic model admits `unique <property> on <Event>` and `unique event <Event>` constraints, and the reference executor enforces them with Chronicle's semantics (#212)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): One event-context catalog, matching Chronicle's `EventContext`, is shared by the compiler, the editor and the documentation. The compiler checks `$eventContext` paths against it (`PLAY0295`–`PLAY0299`) and the editor underlines invalid paths (#217)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): MCP `read-proposal` has a `dropped-comments` view, and every proposal reports how many comments it drops (#216)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): A workspace operation replaces one produced-event mapping source in place (#193)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): New warnings:
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): `PLAY0290`: an `import` names something the application declares itself (#208)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): `PLAY0340`: the same behavior is attached twice across folder files
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): `PLAY0380`: Chronicle's projection lowering drops part of a construct
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): `PLAY0381`: a projection-level key does not route events
- **AI** ([Release v3.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.3.0)): A hook guards agent-run `cratis chronicle` commands in Claude and Pi: read-only inspection and help run as before; replay, retry, quarantine clearing, job control, recommendations, and adding or removing users, applications and subscriptions are refused, as is any chronicle command the guard does not recognize.
- **AI** ([Release v3.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.3.0)): A human who has authorized a store mutation can allow it by starting the agent with `CRATIS_HOOKS_ALLOW_STORE_MUTATIONS=1`; the command lists live in `cratis-store-mutations.json` and can be extended per repository.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.22.0)): `CratisProxiesUseProxyFileSuffix` names generated TypeScript files `Name.proxy.ts` instead of `Name.ts`, so generated proxies can be told apart from hand-written TypeScript in the same folders. Imports between generated files and `index.ts` barrels follow the suffix; imports of modules you mapped yourself are left as mapped. Switching the option on or off replaces the previously generated files. Off by default.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.5](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.5)): `CHRONICLE_MONGODB_IMAGE` selects the MongoDB image the integration fixtures start. Current MongoDB refuses to start on some host kernels, and the refusal surfaces as a container start timeout that explains nothing, leaving integration scenarios unrunnable on that machine.
- **cli** ([Release v3.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.16.0)): Shell completions offer the AI and LLM command values offline, without calling out to a provider.
- **cli** ([Release v3.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.15.0)): `cratis llm-context` now gives every command an `effect` — `read-only`, `local`, `mutating` or `destructive` — and a `requiresConfirmation` flag, so agents and guards can tell observing commands from state-changing ones without keeping their own lists.
- **cli** ([Release v3.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.15.0)): The `cratis llm-context --schema` JSON Schema, the catalog's tips, the generated `CHRONICLE.md` safety notes and a new LLM Context reference page explain both fields and what each value means.
- **cli** ([Release v3.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.14.0)): `cratis run` accepts a single Screenplay (`.play`) file as well as a folder. Only that file is mounted, read-only, and handed to the Stage; the folder it sits in is not mounted (#128)
- **Chronicle.Mcp** ([Release v1.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Mcp/releases/tag/v1.4.0)): Every tool declares MCP tool annotations (`readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint`, `idempotentHint`, `openWorldHint`), so MCP clients can run read-only tools freely and ask before a tool changes state. `stop_job` and `resume_job` are state-changing, non-destructive and idempotent; `delete_job` is state-changing, destructive and idempotent; all other tools are read-only, and every tool is closed-world — it talks only to the configured Chronicle server. (#34)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.21.0)): Analyzer `ARC0021` reports a `Policy` or `AuthenticationSchemes` on an authorization attribute of a model-bound command or read model. Arc enforces authentication and roles but evaluates neither of these, so the artifact is less protected than its attribute reads (#2736)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.21.0)): `IAuthorizationAttributeEvaluator.GetAuthorizationRequirements`, so an evaluator can report every attribute it finds. It defaults to the existing single answer, so existing implementations keep working
- **AI** ([Release v3.2.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.2.1)): The Decision Engine image is built and pushed to Docker Hub with every release, as `cratis/decision-engine`
- **AI** ([Release v3.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.2.0)): `IDecisions` — submit context plus a finite set of choices, get back every choice with its probability, the highest-probability choice, and the margin to the runner-up. Thresholds are deliberately left to consumers so the raw distribution survives intact (#392)
- **AI** ([Release v3.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.2.0)): A batch overload on `IDecisions` for callers scoring many candidates against one task, with results guaranteed in request order (#392)
- **AI** ([Release v3.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.2.0)): `Decision` as an `AIProviderCapability` and an `AIModelCapability`, advertised independently of the conversational and agentic capabilities (#392)
- **AI** ([Release v3.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.2.0)): `AIProviderType.DecisionEngine` and `AddDecisionEngineProvider` / `ReconfigureDecisionEngineProvider` for configuring decision providers the same way as any other AI provider (#392)
- **AI** ([Release v3.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.2.0)): `AIModelCapabilities.For(AIProviderType, ModelName)` — capability for a decision model is declared by provider type rather than inferred from the model identifier, which carries no marker that would distinguish it from a chat model (#392)
- **AI** ([Release v3.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.2.0)): `AIProviderCapabilities` — what each provider type can be used for. A decision engine advertises `Decision` and neither `Conversational` nor `Agentic`, so existing compatibility checks refuse to schedule an agent onto it (#392)
- **AI** ([Release v3.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.2.0)): `AddCratisAI(ai => ai.WithDecisions<TResolver>())` registers the decision surface. Nothing decision-related is registered when it is not called (#392)
- **AI** ([Release v3.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.2.0)): Telemetry on `Cratis.AI.Decisions`: decision duration, count, and top-probability distribution. Context text is not recorded unless explicitly enabled (#392)
- **AI** ([Release v3.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.2.0)): The Cratis Decision Engine service under `Source/DecisionEngine/` — Decision API v1, CPU-first, model and calibration temperature configurable, with an artifact cache so restarts do not re-download weights (#392)
- **release-action** ([Release v1.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/release-action/releases/tag/v1.4.0)): The issues a release's notes say it resolves are closed when the release is created, each with a comment naming the release tag. Only a note bullet ending in `(#123)` counts; prose mentions and cross-repository references are left alone. Needs `issues: write`, and is turned off with `close-resolved-issues: false`
- **Arc** ([Release v22.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.20.0)): Analyzer `ARC0019` reports `[AllowAnonymous]` declared on the same class or method as `[Authorize]` or `[Roles]`. Arc resolves that contradiction differently depending on evaluator order — rejecting the call in one order and admitting anonymous callers in the other — so the declaration now fails loudly at build time instead. Overriding a class on one query method is supported and not reported (#2714)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.20.0)): Analyzer `ARC0020` reports `[Authorize]` or `[AllowAnonymous]` from `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authorization` on a model-bound `[Command]` or `[ReadModel]`. Arc enforces only its own `Cratis.Arc.Authorization` attributes on model-bound artifacts, so the ASP.NET Core attribute protects nothing: a command marked with it is open to every caller. Use the Arc attribute of the same name. Controller-based commands and queries are unaffected (#2719)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.5.0)): `encrypted/1,2,3` macro (`Chronicle.Confidentiality`) for values that need encryption at rest without being personal data subject to GDPR right-to-erasure — an API key, a webhook signing secret, a partner credential (Cratis/Chronicle#4095)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.5.0)): `Chronicle.Concept` gets its own concept-level `encrypted/0,1` macro, mirroring how `pii/0,1` is the concept-level counterpart to `Chronicle.Compliance.pii/1,2`
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.5.0)): `EncryptionScope` (`:subject` default, `:namespace`, `:global`) — the identity an `encrypted` value's key is provisioned under. A subject-scoped key is always disjoint from any `pii` key for the same subject; a namespace-scoped key is shared by every value marked that way in one event store namespace; a global-scoped key is shared across the whole installation
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.5.0)): `pii` and `encrypted` cannot both apply to the same value — rejected at schema-generation time with `Chronicle.Confidentiality.PiiAndEncryptedCombinedNotSupported`, because the two would otherwise encrypt the value twice under two different keys and be unable to release it correctly
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.5.0)): `encrypted/0,1` cannot be declared on a `Chronicle.Concept` created with `event_source_id: true`, rejected at compile time, mirroring the existing restriction on `pii/0,1`
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): `@Encrypted` annotation (`io.cratis.chronicle.confidentiality`) for values that need encryption at rest without being personal data subject to GDPR right-to-erasure — an API key, a webhook signing secret, a partner credential (Cratis/Chronicle#4095)
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): `EncryptionScope.Subject` (default), `.Namespace`, and `.Global` — the identity an `@Encrypted` value's key is provisioned under. A subject-scoped key is always disjoint from any `@Pii` key for the same subject; a namespace-scoped key is shared by every value marked that way in one event store namespace; a global-scoped key is shared across the whole installation
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): `@Pii` and `@Encrypted` cannot both apply to the same value — rejected at schema-generation time with `PiiAndEncryptedCombinedNotSupported`, because the two would otherwise encrypt the value twice under two different keys and be unable to release it correctly
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): `@Encrypted` cannot be applied to an `EventSourceId`-typed concept, flagged with `EncryptedNotSupportedOnEventSourceId`, mirroring the existing restriction on `@Pii`
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): `@encrypted()` decorator (`@cratis/chronicle/confidentiality`) for values that need encryption at rest without being personal data subject to GDPR right-to-erasure — an API key, a webhook signing secret, a partner credential (Cratis/Chronicle#4095)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): `EncryptionScope.Subject` (default), `.Namespace`, and `.Global` — the identity an `@encrypted()` value's key is provisioned under. A subject-scoped key is always disjoint from any `@pii()` key for the same subject; a namespace-scoped key is shared by every value marked that way in one event store namespace; a global-scoped key is shared across the whole installation
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): `@pii()` and `@encrypted()` cannot both apply to the same value — rejected at schema-generation time with `PIIAndEncryptedCombinedNotSupported`, because the two would otherwise encrypt the value twice under two different keys and be unable to release it correctly
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): `@encrypted()` cannot be applied to the `eventSourceId` property, flagged at decoration time with `EncryptedNotSupportedOnEventSourceId`, mirroring the existing restriction on `@pii()`
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.0)): `[Encrypted]` attribute (`Cratis.Chronicle.ProtectedValues`) for values that need encryption at rest without being personal data subject to GDPR right-to-erasure — an API key, a webhook signing secret, a partner credential (#4095)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.0)): `EncryptionScope.Subject` (default), `.Namespace`, and `.Global` — the identity an `[Encrypted]` value's key is provisioned under. A subject-scoped key is always disjoint from any `[PII]` key for the same subject; a namespace-scoped key is shared by every value marked that way in one event store namespace; a global-scoped key is shared across the whole installation
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.0)): `[PII]` and `[Encrypted]` cannot both apply to the same value — rejected at schema-generation time, and flagged at compile time by a new analyzer (CHR0053), because the two would otherwise encrypt the value twice under two different keys and be unable to release it correctly
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.0)): `[Encrypted]` cannot be applied to an `EventSourceId<T>`, flagged at compile time by a new analyzer (CHR0052), mirroring the existing restriction on `[PII]`
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.0)): No API anywhere can delete an `[Encrypted]` value's key — `PIIManager`'s erasure operations explicitly refuse an identifier belonging to an `[Encrypted]` value
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.0)): `Cratis.Chronicle.Testing`'s `EventScenario` and related scenario helpers now protect `[Encrypted]` values the same way they already protect `[PII]` values
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.0)): `Cratis.Chronicle.Confidentiality` — a security metadata provider system (`ICanProvideSecurityMetadataForType`/`ICanProvideSecurityMetadataForProperty`, `ISecurityMetadataResolver`) parallel to the existing GDPR compliance one, for classifying a value as needing protection for security reasons rather than compliance reasons. `[Encrypted]` is its first consumer; `[PII]` continues to resolve only through the compliance provider system and never gains `[Encrypted]`'s wider scopes
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.2)): A Cratis site reference for the documentation writing skill: which teaching components to use (`YouWillLearn`, `Recap`, client-language tabs), how to state an experimental product's maturity, how to state cross-product compatibility, what Prompter records as a documentation-gap signal, and who decides page structure.
- **cli** ([Release v3.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.12.0)): Render generated applications with explicit command inputs and editable keyed lookups using Stage 3.22.0.
- **Stage** ([Release v3.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v3.22.0)): Compose exact required String/Guid command inputs and an explicit submit action; show unsupported required fields instead of silently omitting them. (#100)
- **Stage** ([Release v3.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v3.22.0)): Generate editable lookup forms for supported optional-single queries alongside command forms, preserving exact entered identifiers and showing returned values or not-found states. (#100)
- **Stage** ([Release v3.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v3.22.0)): Bind generated queries by stable source identity so ambiguous names are not silently resolved to another query. (#100)
- **Scene** ([Release v3.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v3.7.0)): Let Scene command forms bind exact String and Guid inputs and submit through the native Arc form, showing unsupported required properties instead of silently omitting them. (#7)
- **Scene** ([Release v3.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v3.7.0)): Rebind mounted forms when their registered command changes, so submissions use the current proxy and authorization. (#7)
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.0)): Selecting the `cratis/screenplay` profile makes the Screenplay MCP server available through the Cratis CLI and each supported client’s native project integration. No separate Screenplay .NET tool installation or manual MCP JSON is needed.
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.0)): Pi receives a native Screenplay tool bridge that discovers live tool schemas, honors profile selection and opt-out, and reports errors without retrying an uncertain source mutation.
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.0)): Screenplay AI guidance now teaches scoped model navigation, reviewed typed-AST proposals, safe refactoring, durable identities and explicit recovery.
- **cli** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): Run the Screenplay MCP server with `cratis screenplay mcp`, bundled into the Cratis CLI with no separate Screenplay tool installation or startup download.
- **cli** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): Configure Screenplay MCP automatically when `cratis ai install` or `cratis ai update` selects the Screenplay profile, including profiles that compose it. Supported native configurations cover Claude Code, VS Code/Copilot, Cursor, OpenCode, Codex and Pi.
- **cli** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): Choose a project-local model directory or disable registration in `.cratis/ai.json`; dry-run and status report the planned and installed registrations.
- **Components** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): Let AutoCommandForm callers place optional footer content, including a native submit action, inside the form while retaining its fields-only default. (#314)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): Embed the Screenplay MCP server in .NET 10 hosts through the `Cratis.Screenplay.Mcp` package and `ScreenplayMcpServer.Run`, without installing or launching a separate tool. The existing `screenplay mcp` command remains supported with the same protocol and authoring behavior. (#203)
- **AI** ([Release v3.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.0.0)): `DayKey`, the daily counterpart to `WeekKey` and `MonthKey`.
- **AI** ([Release v3.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.0.0)): `AgentUsageBucketKey`, the day/provider/agent/purpose/model bucket a daily usage row accumulates under.
- **AI** ([Release v3.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.0.0)): `AgentSessionUsageRecorded` now carries a precomputed `DayKey` and `DailyBucketKey`, for the reason its own remarks already give for `WeekKey`/`MonthKey`: Chronicle's model-bound key resolution keys a projection on a single event property, so a composite bucket has nowhere else to come from.
- **AI** ([Release v3.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.0.0)): `UsagePeriod` now carries the resolved `Day` and `DailyBucket` alongside `Week` and `Month`.
- **AI** ([Release v3.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.0.0)): `AgentSessionUsageRecorded` and `UsagePeriod` gained required positional parameters. Code constructing either directly must supply them; `RecordAgentSessionUsage.Provide` resolves them for the normal path.
- **AI** ([Release v3.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.0.0)): `AgentUsageByDay`'s shape and the signature of `AgentUsageByDay.LastYear` changed as described above.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): Author Screenplay models through `screenplay mcp <model-folder>` with typed AST creation and editing, guarded cross-file renames, and reviewable multi-file proposals. Install the server with the existing `Cratis.Screenplay.Tool` .NET tool package. (#203)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): Navigate large, hierarchical models with scoped declaration searches, dependency views, fixture queries, and revision-safe pagination. (#203)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): Preserve model identities across server restarts and explicitly recover interrupted source writes using root-local identity state and recovery journals. (#203)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): Choose single-file, module, feature, or slice layouts, with whole-model validation before changes are applied. (#203)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): Use typed syntax schemas and source-authoring APIs independently of executable backend readiness, with safe reference validation and verified trivia-preserving renames. (#203)
- **AI** ([Release v2.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v2.31.0)): Add dedicated skills for release notes and migration guidance, verified technical examples, and LLM-friendly documentation delivery.
- **AI** ([Release v2.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v2.31.0)): Make the writing skills available through documentation, content, and engineering profiles appropriate to their tasks.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.19.0)): `SortDirectionIsNotRecognized`, the validation failure raised for an unrecognized sort direction (#2715)
- **AI** ([Release v2.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v2.30.0)): `cratis-social-feed-post` skill covering the feed truncation model, what the opening has to do to survive it, and the formatting and reach constraints that apply to a plain-text feed.
- **AI** ([Release v2.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v2.30.0)): `cratis-writing-voice-and-cadence` skill covering the sentence constructions and the structural sameness that make written material read as machine-generated, and the claim-preservation checks a voice pass must pass before it is accepted.
- **AI** ([Release v2.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v2.30.0)): `cratis/content` profile composing both skills.
- **AI** ([Release v2.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v2.30.0)): Executable verification scenarios for both skills.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.3.0)): `Cratis:Chronicle:Clustering:ResponseTimeout` sets how long a grain call waits for its response. Some startup work grows with the deployment — the projections for an event store are registered across every namespace it has — while Orleans' 30 second default does not, and a server that grows past that point cannot finish starting. Because it cannot finish starting it cannot start on the next attempt either, so it stays down with no way to give it more room. Defaults to the same 30 seconds Orleans uses. (#4088)
- **AI** ([Release v2.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v2.29.0)): `Providers.UsageReporting` - `ICanReportAIUsage`, `AnthropicUsageReporting`/`OpenAIUsageReporting` (Anthropic/OpenAI organization usage and cost report readers), the `IAIUsageReporting`/`AIUsageReporting` orchestrator, and a live `AIProviderUsageReport` query for a configured provider's trailing-30-day vendor-reported token usage and cost.
- **AI** ([Release v2.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v2.29.0)): `Providers.UsageReporting.SettingCredential` - `SetAIProviderUsageCredential`/`ClearAIProviderUsageCredential` for the separate Admin API key a vendor's usage report is read through.
- **AI** ([Release v2.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v2.29.0)): `ConfiguredAIProvider.UsageApiKey`.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.18.0)): `HttpRequestContextEndpointExtensions` on `Cratis.Arc.Http`: `SetEndpointMetadata`, `GetEndpointMetadata`, and `AllowsAnonymous()` on `IHttpRequestContext`, so an `IAuthenticationHandler` can tell whether the endpoint it is about to authenticate allows anonymous access (#2706)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.17.0)): `IHaveKnownChanges` lets an observable collection query state which of its items changed, so the delta for an emission is the one the provider already knows rather than one rediscovered by comparison. An emitted collection is still an `IEnumerable<T>` of the read model, so existing subscribers are unaffected, and a provider that cannot know what changed continues to be served by the comparison fallback.
- **Scene** ([Release v3.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v3.6.0)): Add query input forms that submit exact string or Guid arguments to an optional-single query, with visible validation, loading, result, and not-found states. (#39)
- **Scene** ([Release v3.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v3.6.0)): Add identity-aware query registration that detects ambiguous bindings while remaining compatible with existing query views. (#39)
- **cli** ([Release v3.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.10.0)): Add `--warnings-as-errors` so Screenplay validation can reject compiler warnings in CI. (#168)
- **Components** ([Release v4.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.12.0)): `icons` on `CratisComponentsProvider`, replacing Components-owned glyphs product-wide and resolved per site as per-component prop → provider icon → built-in glyph (#299)
- **Components** ([Release v4.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.12.0)): First cut of the icon vocabulary: `close`, `remove`, `expand`, `sortAscending`, `sortDescending`, `previous`, `next` and `clear` (#299)
- **Components** ([Release v4.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.12.0)): `useCratisIcon()` and `CratisIconResolver`, the resolver Components uses at every icon site, exported from the setup-only root barrel for product components that want to follow the same vocabulary (#299)
- **Components** ([Release v4.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.12.0)): `Chip`, `DataTableCore`'s sort indicators, `ComboBox`, `Dropdown`, `TablePaginator`, `DatePickerInput`, `ProgressSpinner`, a toast's dismiss and loading marks, `CommandForm`'s chips field, `ChatSidebar`, the `Chat` panel and PivotViewer's filter/detail panels now resolve their glyphs through that vocabulary instead of hard-coding them (#299)
- **Components** ([Release v4.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.12.0)): `Documentation/Common/cratis-components-provider.md` documents `icons` the way `messages` is documented: the whole vocabulary with what each name draws, its default, and every component that uses it (#299)
- **Components** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): Per-area CSS entry points for every JavaScript subpath — `@cratis/components/Dialogs/styles`, `@cratis/components/PivotViewer/styles`, `@cratis/components/DataTables/styles` and the rest — so an application downloads only the CSS for the surfaces it mounts (#301)
- **Components** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): `@cratis/components/styles/base`, the shared internal utilities and cascade-layer order that every per-area entry point needs, imported once and never duplicated per surface (#301)
- **Components** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): `Documentation/Styling/per-area-stylesheets.md` documentation covering when to prefer them over the aggregate, the full list of entry points and what each one covers (#301)
- **Components** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): Per-area CSS budgets in the published-archive gate: each entry point has its own reviewed gzip ceiling, and a new area cannot ship without a measured number (#301)
- **Components** ([Release v4.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.10.0)): `removeIcon` on `TagGroup` replaces the glyph inside each tag's remove button, defaulting to the multiplication sign it renders today. The button, its accessible name from `removeLabel`, and its keyboard behaviour are unchanged. (#293)

## Changed

- **cli** ([Release v3.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.21.0)): The CLI now bundles Screenplay 4.43.0 and Stage 4.20.0, and `cratis run` defaults to the matching Stage image. Model authors get Screenplay's fixes up to 4.43, including `given … for` and `when append … for` in view slices, which previously failed with PLAY0273 (#193).
- **cli** ([Release v3.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.21.0)): **Chronicle login tokens only go to the server that issued them.** `cratis chronicle login` saves the token, its expiry and the issuing server in the active context. Later commands send the token only when they connect to that exact server. If you log in with `--server`, the token is used when you pass the same `--server` again, and nowhere else. Login refuses multi-host and `chronicle+srv://` connection strings, because the server that receives the token can't be determined in advance. It also no longer follows redirects, and it honours the connection string's TLS options instead of accepting any certificate.
- **cli** ([Release v3.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.21.0)): **All startup hints share one wait.** The CLI, Stage image and Cratis AI hints get at most about 300 ms together after the command finishes, instead of up to 300 ms each. `CRATIS_NO_UPDATE_CHECK` now also skips the Stage image check's local Docker lookup.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.31.0)): Policies without the opt-in behave as before and still require an authenticated caller. Role requirements and authentication-scheme requirements always require an authenticated caller, even when combined with an opted-in policy. If several policies apply, a guest is only evaluated when every one of them opted in. (#2761)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.31.0)): Arc's method-level authorization replaces the type-level declaration. A method with an opted-in policy is therefore reachable by guests even when its type requires a role. The authorization docs now call this out. (#2761)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): Bundle the pinned .NET reference assemblies used for audited body binding, expose the Roslyn runtime dependency, and verify a standalone packed consumer in CI. Existing projection and body-less reducer refusal remains unchanged.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): Whole-solution Debug specs pass in serial execution (`dotnet test --configuration Debug -m:1`); one optional Mongo-backed test is skipped without a configured connection. Parallel local runs suffered external SIGTERM of testhost from another worktree command; isolated Rendering.Cratis 1,751 pass + 1 skip.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): Release build zero warnings, NuGet pack and standalone consumer, frontend test/build and Python compatibility/entrypoint checks pass. Full cross-provider review on the exact committed diff passed with no findings.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.14.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.14.1)): The kernel's observers now use the generic state machine from `Cratis.Orleans.StateMachines` (Cratis.Orleans 1.11.0), and Chronicle's own copy is removed. Runtime behavior and persisted observer state are unchanged. The transition trace log category is now `StateMachine<ObserverState>` (#4289)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.14.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.14.1)): Chronicle now builds against Cratis.Orleans 1.11.0, Arc 22.30.0 and Fundamentals 7.19.6 — the minimum versions Cratis.Orleans 1.11.0 requires. The Arc proxy-generator build package stays at 22.23.0 because 22.30.0 crashes on Core
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.43.0)): The development dependency on `@cratis/chronicle` is 6.19.0, and the samples use the same version. The peer range stays `^6.7.0`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.43.0)): With an older SDK, a projection-backed lookup fails with an error naming `@cratis/chronicle >= 6.19.0`. When a declarative projection without an explicit read-model type is registered, seeded lookups of read models that have no reducer or explicit projection now fail the same way instead of returning `null`, because Arc cannot tell which read model that projection targets. Give the projection an explicit read-model type (`@projection('', ReadModel)`) or upgrade the SDK.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.42.0)): The documentation recommends the co-located layout; a separate output folder remains supported, including a dedicated folder nested inside the artifacts folder, which keeps its barrels.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.42.0)): A bundler that reads slice files outside the web project must lower the proxies' decorators itself, because the web project's `tsconfig.json` does not apply to them. For Vite 8, set `oxc: { decorator: { legacy: true } }`; declare the client packages where the slice folder can resolve them. See [Set up proxy generation](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/blob/v0.42.0/Documentation/proxy-generation/getting-started.md).
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.13.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.13.2)): Document that a webhook's non-2xx response fails delivery: transient failures are first retried by the HTTP client, then the partition follows the observer retry settings and is quarantined when attempts are exhausted.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.13.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.13.2)): Document that an event source can re-claim its own unique value, and that changing the value frees the old one.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.30.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.30.1)): Update the Chronicle integration to Cratis.Chronicle 19.13.0, with the Cratis.Fundamentals, Cratis.Metrics and MongoDB driver versions it requires.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.43.0)): Document that C# code bodies use provider-generated PascalCase member names, while authored Screenplay identifiers remain unchanged. The portable default tenant remains the zero GUID; provider-side tenant translation is still pending. Refs #282.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.43.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.43.0)): Decision records 0012 and 0018 now use the supported `implemented` stage and retain their outstanding provider-verification notes.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): A projection block that repeats `automap` or `no automap` reports `PLAY0452` (warning). The last setting still wins, and canonical printing keeps each line's comments.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.20.0)): Reject constraints, batch appends (`appendMany` and plural `when.events`), and other unproven operations or inputs with `UnsupportedEventSequenceOperation`, which points to a kernel-backed test. Constraints can be disabled explicitly only for tests that do not depend on them.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.41.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.41.0)): In PostgreSQL mode, `notifyChanged()` validates its target but publishes nothing locally; the database trigger is the only change source. In-process mode is unchanged.
- **Components** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): `Tooltip` `content` accepts any React node, and `0` now shows. `undefined`, `null`, booleans, and an empty string still disable the tooltip. Keep tooltip content non-interactive. Renderer adapters that implement `common.tooltip` receive nodes and must render them as they are; the Conformance profile now checks this. (#230)
- **Components** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): FilterPanel is now a named, non-modal dialog. Opening it moves focus into the panel, and Escape closes it and returns focus to the element that opened it. It now renders as a `div` with `role="dialog"` instead of an `<aside>`, so it is no longer a complementary landmark: `getByRole('complementary')` no longer finds it, and CSS or tests that target the `aside` element must use `.pv-filter-dropdown` or `getByRole('dialog', { name })` instead. This also applies to the filter panel inside `PivotViewer`. (#328)
- **Components** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): Loading and failure rows in query tables now remount on each state change, so screen readers announce the change. (#327)
- **Components** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): Conformance merges a part variant's own `pt` attributes with the profile's pass-throu
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.12.0)): Pattern mining derives request features from route templates only, never from raw request paths, so identifiers embedded in a URL (such as `/customers/jane@example.com`) are no longer stored in mined patterns. Patterns stored before this release are not rewritten (#4260)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.12.0)): Registering an event type whose stored schema differs only in titles no longer rewrites the stored schema, and no longer invalidates caches on every reconnect. This matters when old and new clients run side by side (#4253)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.19.0)): Reducer replay in `ReadModelScenario` moved behind an internal read-model processor. Reducer behavior is unchanged.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.40.0)): A service scope captures an immutable snapshot of its execution context when it is created. `ServiceScope.identity` returns that snapshot, and the Chronicle, MongoDB and Drizzle integrations use it, so changing the original context object after a scope was created no longer changes the tenant those services use. Principals whose claims can't be cloned keep working for ordinary requests.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.0)): A reactor whose return value no side-effect handler claims now fails its partition, naming the return type, instead of logging a warning and acknowledging the event. Fix the handler, then retry the partition (#4156)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.0)): Duplicate reactor IDs, across discovered classes and runtime delegates, are rejected before existing subscriptions disconnect, and the error names both types. A duplicate `Register<T>()` now throws `ReactorAlreadyRegistered` instead of `ArgumentException` (#4196)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.0)): A `WaitForCompletion()` timeout returns a result with `TimedOut` set instead of throwing a gRPC cancellation. Supported servers also report which observers are still behind (#4132)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.0)): Read-model instance pages from MongoDB, SQL and in-memory sinks are ordered by key, so reading a page again returns the same instances while membership is unchanged (#3741)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.0)): A dynamic dictionary key that uses an unknown `$` expression fails with `UnsupportedDynamicPropertyPathExpression` instead of being written as a literal key (#4119)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.0)): SQL redaction content describes the original event (type, occurrence time, correlation id, caused-by identities and causation types) instead of the redaction itself, matching the other providers (#4145)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.0)): `Cratis.Chronicle.Testing` depends on `Cratis.Arc` and `Cratis.Arc.Core` 22.19.1 or later, which brings in the ASP.NET Core shared framework. Projects pinned to an older Arc must upgrade (#4158)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.0)): Aspire uses the development Chronicle image in run mode and the production image in publish mode. A configuration callback no longer implies the production image; `WithImageTag` still overrides (#3686)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.18.0)): Model-bound children now fill their identifier the way Chronicle for .NET does. When the child's identifier is a discoverable `id`/`Id` or given with `identifiedBy`, and the child key defaults to the event source ID, the identifier comes from the event source ID. When no `id` exists and a child property matches an explicit event key, that property becomes the child's identifier. Explicit identifier mappings and class-level `@noAutoMap` still take precedence.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.28.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.28.1)): Query argument parsing is stricter: values that previously fell back silently now return HTTP 400, including `?flag=1` or `?flag=yes` for a boolean, overflowing integers, whitespace-only values, invalid nullable value types that used to become null, and invalid concept values (including nullable or defaulted concepts). Clients must send valid values or omit optional arguments.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.28.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.28.1)): The new public `InvalidQueryArgument` exception represents an invalid supplied query argument; `QueryHttpExtensions.GetQueryArguments` can now throw it. Invalid collection elements use the same named malformed-request validation shape.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.27.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.27.1)): Creating several Arc Core containers over the same types is faster: model-bound command, query and read-model discovery runs once per type set and is reused. A local .NET 10 Release measurement of 50 containers over 581 types dropped from 58.8 ms to 23.7 ms median, about 60% faster (#2673)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.27.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.27.1)): Read models no longer expose private static methods or compiler-generated methods as queries through runtime discovery; public and internal static query methods remain supported (#2651)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.10.0)): `all` inside `children` or `nested`. Use `all` at the root with a dotted path instead.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.10.0)): `remove via join` at the root or inside `nested`.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.10.0)): `children` inside `nested`.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.10.0)): `join` inside a `nested` object beneath `children`.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.10.0)): More than one `every`/`all` block at the same level. Two `every` blocks inside `children` used to be merged; they are now an error.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.10.0)): The same event more than once at one level, including across join blocks, repeated `clear with`, and `remove with` plus `clear with` for the same event. These checks now also run when no schema is supplied.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): Stage targets Chronicle 19.8.1, Arc 22.25.0 and Screenplay 4.41.1. Generated applications and the Host image use the same versions, and CI checks the Chronicle projection oracle against v19.8.1.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): The semantic engine no longer registers Chronicle projection mirrors, so its read models no longer appear as projection views in the Workbench. The admission endpoint no longer reports `projectionMirror`. The default engine is unchanged.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): Specifications that assert a value derived from `$context.occurred` are rejected, because the generated handler uses the actual time.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): composite-key read models, which need https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/issues/4265;
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): `FromAll`, which needs https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/issues/4266;
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): literals in `every`/`all` blocks;
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): the projection shapes tracked in Chronicle #4125 and #4166;
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): role-only policies (Arc #2761);
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): per-run projection specifications (#15);
- **Stage** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): given read models (#14).
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.16.0)): Reactor handlers are always called with the third `services` argument. One- and two-argument handlers are unaffected; a handler that collects arguments with a rest parameter now receives one more.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.16.0)): Without an activator, reactors and reducers are still constructed once per observation with no arguments, exactly as before.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.16.0)): The reactor documentation covers explicit appends, read-model lookups, activation lifetimes, and replay: `@onceOnly()` keeps a handler out of replay but does not protect against retries or duplicate delivery, so explicit writes should be idempotent. The four TypeScript snippets for explicit appends and reading state inside a reactor now show working code.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.39.0)): The proxy generator analyzes every execution path of a command's return type, including `Outcome` helpers, promises, aliases and nested tuples, and accepts alternatives that resolve to the same client type. Literal unions such as `boolean`, enums and `'a' | 'b'`, with or without `null`/`undefined`, keep their full type, distinct concepts fall back to their shared primitive, and arrays that mix Chronicle event types are omitted from the client response as before.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.39.0)): Alternatives that need different client types, including two structurally identical but distinct DTO classes, now fail generation with guidance to return one response DTO with a status field. Arc for .NET's generator picks one of them instead, which the client can't decode reliably.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.39.0)): Generated metadata for queries returning `boolean`, enums or literal unions now names the element type, so OpenAPI describes their data.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.39.0)): The command outcomes guide describes how to model several business outcomes.
- **AI** ([Release v7.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.1.0)): Package unmodified Laya at commit 4066d5d5 with a CPU-only Decision API v1 adapter and pinned multilingual checkpoint.
- **AI** ([Release v7.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.1.0)): Preserve built-in v1 single/batch contract, add independent multi-label `/v1/labels` and typed `ClassifyLabels()` for callers.
- **AI** ([Release v7.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.1.0)): Test the packaged image in release CI and publish the Laya candidate image tag.
- **AI** ([Release v7.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.1.0)): Image build and five adapter tests passed; five C# classification specs passed; AI and Infrastructure builds passed.
- **AI** ([Release v7.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.1.0)): 10-issue workload against the packaged image: 30 sequential in 4.12s, batch in 3.97s, label requests in 6.96s.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.9.0)): An append with no events but at least one concurrency scope that will actually be checked is now accepted and only validates those scopes, so a command that decides to write nothing can still confirm its decision was current. Empty appends without such a scope are still rejected, and empty appends no longer notify append subscribers.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.9.0)): Requires Chronicle kernel 19.0.0 or later for decision reads. With plain ASP.NET Core the middleware commits after the response is written, so a caller that needs a conflict-aware response must commit before writing it.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.38.0)): `rxjs` is now a required peer dependency of `@cratis/arc.drizzle`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.37.0)): A query declared as a snapshot that returns an observable, subscribable or async iterable is rejected without touching the returned source, so a shared RxJS `Subject` stays usable.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.37.0)): `QueryScenario` releases such a stream before rejecting, with a one-second limit and cancellation support. Sources returned inside a scenario are treated as owned by the scenario and may be closed.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.37.0)): `QueryScenario.perform()` now rejects queries declared `observable: true`, as `QueryScenario` does in Arc for .NET; use `ObservableQueryScenario` for them.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.37.0)): `yarn ci` now type-checks the built declarations of every published package with `skipLibCheck: false`, so a declaration a consumer can't compile fails the build.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.36.0)): Projects that use `recommended-type-checked` can see new ARCCHR0006 diagnostics on reactors that return commands without `@onceOnly()` or `@replay()`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.36.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.36.0)): The code-analysis navigation lists the ARCCHR0003, ARCCHR0006, ARCCHR0007, ARCCHR0009, and ARCCHR0010 pages.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.14.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.14.1)): When a property-only model can't be found at store creation (for example, an import-only model in compiled JavaScript without discovery patterns), the client logs one diagnostic warning per process, naming the mapped properties and their event types. It suggests adding a class-level `@fromEvent(...)`, enabling artifact discovery, or registering the class explicitly. Mappings inherited from base classes or reachable through child types don't trigger the warning.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.14.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.14.1)): The getting-started, read-model and troubleshooting guides describe these registration options.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.26.0)): Plain minimal API responses that return concepts change from objects such as `{"value":"..."}` to primitive values such as `"..."`, matching Arc-generated endpoints and the TypeScript proxies. To keep the object shape for a concept, register your own `JsonConverter<YourConcept>` with `ConfigureHttpJsonOptions`; it takes precedence for individual concepts, concept collections, and, when it implements property-name conversion, dictionary keys, whether registered before or after `AddCratisArc` (#2755)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.26.0)): Only concept-related converters are added to ASP.NET Core's HTTP JSON options. Your converters keep precedence and the naming policy is unchanged; enums (including `long`-backed va
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.35.0)): Paging and sorting parameters are listed only for queries whose return is declared as an array or a `queryPage` result. A metadata-free array query no longer lists them; to list them again, set `generatedReturn: { cardinality: 'many', nullable: false }` on its descriptor. Queries that rely on a `QueryRenderer` to page are not advertised as pageable.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.35.0)): 401 responses are listed on command execute, command validation, and query operations exactly when authentication can produce them.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.35.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.35.0)): `page` and `pageSize` are described as `int32`, and the paging parameter descriptions match Arc for .NET.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.25.0)): For a command that declares a blocking severity, the caller's `X-Allowed-Severity` (or `allowedSeverity`) can make validation stricter, never looser. A failure with `Unknown` severity also rejects such a command. Commands without the attribute behave as before. This deliberately differs from the caller opt-out for controller actions in #21; decision 0005 records why.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.34.0)): `ValidationResult` is now both the existing interface and a value holding the helpers. Existing `import type { ValidationResult }` imports and the `validation(message, members?, reason?, severity?)` helper are unchanged.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.8.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.8.1)): Generating a declaration from a stored, untyped composite-key expression takes the key type from the read model's `Id` schema (including nullable types), or from the child item's `Id` inside a children block. When the schema has no composite type it falls back to `CompositeKey`, and in that case the generated declaration only recompiles if the schema defines that type.
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.1)): `yarn ci` in `Source` now also runs the public API TSDoc check and the packed-archive CSS budget check, which previously ran only in GitHub CI.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): Stage now uses Screenplay 4.41.0. Models with evolved events (ESM v4) are still refused with `STAGE-ESM-016`, which is now reported before any descriptor check.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): The reducer-body rejection (`STAGE-ESM-019`) names only what is still missing.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): Specifications attach typed event-source context for every executable-model version after v1, as Screenplay's reference evaluator does.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.33.0)): `givenReadModel(Type, sourceId, instance)` without a tenant now pins the model for the scenario's current `context.tenantId`, not always `Default`. It still falls back to `Default` when the context sets no tenant.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.33.0)): A read model with no seeded history and no pin resolves as missing. A required model reports the usual not-found validation result, and an optional one receives `null`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.33.0)): A projection-backed read model with seeded history, and aggregates, point to `ChronicleKernelScenario`. Projections need the kernel.
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): While a query table's first result loads, or when its query fails or is unauthorized, the table now renders the new loading or failure row instead of an empty `empty-row`/`emptyMessage`. `empty-row` still renders for a query that returns no rows. If your CSS or tests target `empty-row` to detect loading or errors, update them to the new parts. (#327)
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): An observable query that is missing a required argument never subscribes, so its table now shows the loading row until every required argument has a value; before, it showed a blank row. A snapshot query missing a required argument still shows `emptyMessage`. (#327)
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): `ToolbarButton` exposes `aria-pressed` when you pass `active`, so assistive technology announces the toggle state. Buttons without `active` are unchanged. (#328)
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.3)): `release.md` documents the release order and every place the Components major-version window is set.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.31.0 — global command and query filters](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.31.0)): Cancellation stops every stage that has not started: authorization policies, filters, validators, dependency resolution, `provide()`, `handle()`, query performers, renderers, read-model interceptors, observable producers, and command response handlers. Once a command handler has run, the command reports what actually happened. A completed outcome is not turned into a cancellation failure. A response handler that cancellation stops before it starts is reported as canceled. An effect that was already acknowledged is never reported as undone.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.31.0 — global command and query filters](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.31.0)): A defined Chronicle command whose append was acknowledged keeps its success when cancellation stops the wait for projections. A real observer failure is still reported.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.31.0 — global command and query filters](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.31.0)): Filter results are validated before use. Malformed authorization, readiness or severity values fail closed.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.31.0 — global command and query filters](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.31.0)): Each filter result's validation entries are filtered by the allowed severity before the chain decides whether to stop. A filtered-out warning therefore cannot switch off later authorization filters. This deliberately differs from Arc on .NET, which stops before severity filtering.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.14.0)): Reducer handler selection and invocation are shared by the production observer and the scenario. Production behavior is unchanged.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.6.0)): `cratis_chronicle_contracts` bumped from 19.4.7 to 19.6.1, the first release carrying the `RemoveObserver` rpc
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.13.0)): `@cratis/chronicle.contracts` bumped from 19.4.0 to 19.6.1, the first release carrying the `RemoveObserver` rpc
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.6.0)): `chronicle-contracts` bumped from 19.5.0 to 19.6.1, the first release carrying the `RemoveObserver` rpc
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.2)): Fluent `.FromAll()` and model-bound `[FromAll]` projections now subscribe to every event type in the event sequence, as a declaration `all` block already does. **Existing projections that use `FromAll` will be redefined, and will start creating or updating read-model instances for events from any event source**, keyed by event source id unless another key is given. Use `FromEvery` for mappings that should only apply to the projection's own event types. Combining `FromAll` with `FromEvery` applies both sets of mappings to all event types. Event types already mapped through `From`, a join or a derivative are not processed a second time by the all-events subscription (#4164)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.40.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.40.0)): Specification `given` events now go through the same establishment path. Existing specification outcomes are unchanged.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): A valid `persona` no longer stops a model from binding to the executable model. It is reported as information only, and models that already bound produce the same bytes. Personas and providers are still not part of the executable model; the authentication documentation describes where a renderer reads provider identities from the compilation result. This is part of #254.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): A persona whose policy cannot be resolved is now an error in compilation, Safe authoring and executable binding, so it never falls back to anonymous access. Draft authoring still accepts it and reports the unresolved reference.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.38.0)): PLAY0284 follows what Chronicle fills in a join. It counts a join's mapped fields and compatible AutoMap fields instead of the join's label, which Chronicle discards. It no longer counts a field that AutoMap cannot fill because an explicit mapping already uses the same event field. It also no longer counts fields that only a join inside a `nested` block would fill, because Chronicle does not currently apply joins there. Models that relied on those cases now get PLAY0284 for fields that stay empty at runtime. (#262)
- **cli** ([Release v3.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.20.0)): The CLI now bundles Screenplay 4.37.0 and Stage 4.18.1, and `cratis run` defaults to the matching Stage image.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.12.0)): Projections, reducers and `readModels.register()` build read-model definitions through one shared builder. Definitions for read models without `@index()` are unchanged.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): Models with multiple event generations select ESM v4. A lone `generation 1` keeps its previous ESM version, and v1–v3 canonical bytes are unchanged. Consumers must explicitly admit v4 before accepting these models; generation transformations, historical replay and Chronicle deployment support remain separate work under #71.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): ESM v4 no longer serializes projection-transition cardinality and rejects the legacy `ZeroOrOne` and `Many` values. Query cardinality is unchanged (decision 0007; part of #132).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.37.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.37.0)): References to removed current-generation properties fail binding instead of falling back to historical shapes. MCP understands generation-qualified addresses and exports canonical v4 models.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.4 — tested with Chronicle SDK 6.10](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.4)): Test the Chronicle integration, Cratis package, and Library sample against `@cratis/chronicle` 6.10.0. The supported peer range remains `^6.7.0`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.4 — tested with Chronicle SDK 6.10](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.4)): Mark the live command-returning reactor `@onceOnly()` and document the replayable-by-default behavior introduced in SDK 6.9.0. Failed-partition recovery still requires repeat-safe effects.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.4 — tested with Chronicle SDK 6.10](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.4)): Document subscription-marker filtering, compiled-JavaScript artifact discovery, and credential-rejection behavior. Correct the package README to describe its bounded live-kernel coverage.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.11.0)): `getInstanceById` is no longer deprecated. It is the canonical read-by-key API, as `GetInstanceById` is in .NET, and its behavior for a missing instance is documented. `findInstanceById` remains for code that wants `null` when no instance exists.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.10.0)): Artifact discovery no longer scans `.ts` sources when your program runs as compiled JavaScript. Artifacts register when their modules are imported. When the program runs from TypeScript (a `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.mts`, or `.cts` entry file, Vitest, or a `tsx`, `ts-node`, or type-stripping loader), the default still scans `**/*.ts`, and now also `**/*.tsx`. An explicit `discoveryPatterns` is always used as given.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.10.0)): `glob` is now a dependency of `@cratis/chronicle`, and `!` patterns in `discoveryPatterns` exclude files as documented. When a discovered file fails to import, the error names the file.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.10.0)): A failed token request no longer stops the call. The client logs the token endpoint and cause, then sends the call without a token, the same way the .NET client does. A kernel with authentication turned off is therefore reachable, and an unauthenticated rejection reports the token failure. When the kernel rejects a cached token, the client refreshes it and retries the call once.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.10.0)): With client credentials, tokens are requested from the server the client selected for the connection, not always from the first host in the connection string.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.10.0)): A declarative join without `.on(...)` now fails when the projection is built, with a clear message. A join inside `.children(...)` uses the children's `identifiedBy` as its default, whatever the call order.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.10.0)): The keep-alive reports the installed client version instead of `1.0.0`.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): A read model marked `@Passive` is registered without a sink and computed from its events on read, so a read right after an append includes that event.
