Data a previous version stored for such a model is no longer read.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): `registerAll()` throws `ArtifactRegistrationFailed` when a reactor or reducer cannot be started. It still starts the others, captures and seeders first. The automatic pass on connect logs each failure and retries the failed observers on the next reconnect.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): In Spring Boot, a kernel that rejects the connection at startup now stops the application with `ChronicleConnectionFailed`. It no longer starts and leaves every appending request waiting.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): A connection string with `apiKey` is rejected at client creation with `IllegalArgumentException`. The kernel has no API key authentication, and such a client used to connect with no credentials at all.
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): Seed entries without `forNamespace()` are sent as global seed
- **AI** ([Release v7.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.0.0)): `DecisionResult.Provider` is replaced by `DecisionResult.Engine`, a `DecisionEngineType`, and the `decision.engine` telemetry tag replaces the provider tag
- **Stage** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): Stage now uses Screenplay 4.35.0 and its canonical corpus.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): Reaction trigger `reads` (Screenplay 4.31) is accepted and adds no runtime behaviour, since Screenplay binds no runtime meaning to it yet. Stage's documentation says so, and that matching children in a child join is left to Chronicle.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.9.0)): **Reactors are now replayable by default, as in .NET.** Existing reactors accept explicit replays. They also accept kernel-initiated replays, such as rewinds after an event revision or redaction, and replays after a definition change when that option is enabled. Before upgrading, mark reactors that cause side effects with class-level `@onceOnly()`, or mark individual handlers when only those handlers must be skipped.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.9.0)): Reactor registration fails for a `@replay()` handler that matches no registered event type, and for two replay handlers for the same event type.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.9.0)): Inherited replay handlers are found, and the most derived method wins.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.2)): During a scenario, code that reads Arc's process-wide provider directly, such as the MongoDB and EF Core `Observe()` extensions, now sees the host's provider, or throws `ServiceProviderNotConfigured` when no host has set one, instead of using the scenario's provider. Command validation in a scenario is unaffected (#2763)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.0)): The job system is now `Cratis.Orleans` rather than Chronicle's own. The local engine, its storage for MongoDB and SQL, and its specs are gone; what remains here are the job scenarios that are about Chronicle
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.0)): Job storage is resolved through `IJobsStorage`. The kernel asked for the concrete MongoDB type, which the container satisfied by building a second, unconfigured instance - grains wrote to one database and the kernel read from another, and reading an always-empty database does not throw. Six integration specs asserted job steps were empty and passed for the wrong reason
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.0)): The kernel names its own assemblies for BSON serializer registration. Adopting the package's registration silently dropped every serializer the kernel ships, including the one that upgrades constraint definitions written before 16.12
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.0)): A development wipe tells the job system its database was emptied, so the tables it creates on resolution are made again
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.0)): A replay specification no longer depends on catching a transient job. A replay job is removed the moment it completes, and two events against a fast database finish before the first poll looks; the replay is verified from the rebuilt read model and the absence of a recommendation instead
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): The `README.md` generated into every project lists the prerequisites the templates really need: the .NET 10 SDK (every .NET template targets `net10.0`), JDK 17 for the Kotlin and Java templates, Docker with the Compose plugin, and Node.js `^20.19.0` or `>=22.12.0`
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): The post-creation instructions and generated READMEs use `docker compose` instead of the standalone `docker-compose` command
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): The generated READMEs now show expected output, the Chronicle health check, how to stop without losing data, and a warning that the development containers publish their ports on every network interface with well-known credentials
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): The Kotlin and Java READMEs explain that the Cratis Gradle plugin and dependency float on `latest.release`, how Gradle caches that, and how to pin versions for reproducible builds
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): The template documentation's getting-started page is now a step-by-step tutorial that ends with a registered item. Each template page lists its options, how to run it, and troubleshooting
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.1 — identity, tenancy and authorization paired with .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.1)): The paired conformance suite now covers:
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.1 — identity, tenancy and authorization paired with .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.1)): the identity endpoint and its display cookie;
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.1 — identity, tenancy and authorization paired with .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.1)): tenant resolution from headers, claims, fixed tenants and subdomains;
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.1 — identity, tenancy and authorization paired with .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.1)): class- and method-level authorization, including role replacement and anonymous overrides;
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.1 — identity, tenancy and authorization paired with .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.1)): malformed command inputs and query failures;
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.1 — identity, tenancy and authorization paired with .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.1)): correlation IDs on success, error and pending responses;
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.1 — identity, tenancy and authorization paired with .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.1)): observable-query waits and completion.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.1 — identity, tenancy and authorization paired with .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.1)): The authorization docs now state the rules the suite checks:
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.1 — identity, tenancy and authorization paired with .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.1)): a query method's authorization declaration replaces the class declaration, as in Arc on .NET;
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.1 — identity, tenancy and authorization paired with .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.1)): authorization decorators on command methods fail the build, because command authorization is declared on the class.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.1 — identity, tenancy and authorization paired with .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.1)): The capability reference records the remaining deliberate differences: identity denial bodies, display-cookie trust, 401 versus 403 for anonymous callers, and redacted message text.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.8.0)): Same-named fluent `uniqueFor` declarations across classes, together with decorated event types of the same name, are merged into one definition. Before, each registered its own definition with the same name.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.8.0)): `hasFor` looks constraints up by their registered name, as `HasFor` does in .NET.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.8.0)): A message configured with `withMessage` now replaces the kernel's message in append results.
- **AI** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): Keeping an external link out of a LinkedIn post's body is an editorial default with a reader-need exception, not a claimed ranking penalty. The feed skill no longer says that expanding a post is a ranking signal, or that pseudo-formatting defeats search.
- **AI** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): Hacker News: AI doesn't write or edit anything Cratis posts there.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.0 — enums instead of string-literal unions](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.0)): `@cratis/arc.core`:
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.0 — enums instead of string-literal unions](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.0)): `ServiceLifetime` for `ServiceRegistration.lifetime` (`ArcOptions.services`, `new ServiceRegistry([...])`). `addScoped`/`@scoped()` and friends are unaffected.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.0 — enums instead of string-literal unions](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.0)): `TenantResolverType` for `TenancyOptions.resolverType` and `sources` in code. `appsettings.json` and environment variables are unaffected.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.0 — enums instead of string-literal unions](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.0)): `SortDirection` for `SortRequest.direction` (`QueryOptions.sorting`, `queryPage(...)`). The HTTP `sortDirection` parameter is unaffected.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.0 — enums instead of string-literal unions](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.0)): `CommandCommitDisposition`, `CommandRecoveryStatus`, `CommandOperationCompensation` and `CommandOperationFailureSource` for command operation outcomes and custom execution scopes.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.0 — enums instead of string-literal unions](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.0)): `ClientAuthentication` and `ClientOperationKind` for typed client-manifest entries.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.0 — enums instead of string-literal unions](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.0)): `@cratis/arc.testing`: `SortDirection` for the `sorting.direction` option of `QueryScenario` and `ObservableQueryScenario`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.0 — enums instead of string-literal unions](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.0)): `@cratis/arc.mongodb`: `SortDirection` in `queryPage` sorting.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.0 — enums instead of string-literal unions](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.0)): `@cratis/arc.drizzle`: `DrizzleDialect` for `DrizzleOptions.dialect` and the codec helpers, `ConceptCodecKind` for `conceptCodec`, and `SortDirection` in sorting.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.0 — enums instead of string-literal unions](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.0)): `@cratis/arc.chronicle`: `ChronicleReadConsistency` for the `consistency` argument of `ChronicleReadModels` reads.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.0 — enums instead of string-literal unions](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.0)): `@cratis/arc.proxygenerator`: `SourceOperation.kind` uses `ClientOperationKind`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.34.0)): Two unmarked declarations of the same event in one slice are now a syntax error (`PLAY0447`, a duplicate generation 1); before, only binding rejected them.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.34.0)): References to an event and the checks on its shape (produced mappings, specifications, projections, reaction trigger values) use the current, highest generation.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.34.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.34.0)): Tools that read `EventSyntax` directly will now see one declaration per generation. Until the executable model carries lineage, they should reject or skip events that carry a generation marker (`HasGenerationMarker`).
- **cli** ([Release v3.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.19.0)): Chronicle bumped from 18.1.0 to 19.6.1 to pick up `RemoveObserver` and the `RetryPartitionResponse` outcome
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): **GET paging**
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): `pageSize` is parsed like .NET's `int.TryParse`: leading zeros, `+` and ASCII whitespace are accepted.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): A nonnumeric, empty or out-of-range `pageSize` means unpaged (200) rather than 400.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): A nonnumeric `page` falls back to page 0.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): A negative `page` or a nonpositive `pageSize` gives 400 `rule` results on `Page` and `Size`, in that order.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): **Invalid sort direction:** a GET or QUERY request with one gives `malformedRequest` naming `sortDirection` or `sorting.direction`, with .NET's message.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): **QUERY bodies**
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): A negative `pageSize` means unpaged.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): A negative `page` gives a `rule` result on `Page`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): `null`, and `null` `arguments`, `paging` or `sorting`, are treated as absent.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): Unknown members, and a sort direction without a field, are ignored.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): Malformed JSON, a non-object member, a noninteger or out-of-range paging value, or a non-string sort value gives .NET's exception envelope. Details appear only when `exposeExceptionDetails` is on.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): Oversized bodies and undeclared argument names still get `malformedRequest`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): **In-memory paging:** offsets are capped at the int32 maximum.
- **cli** ([Release v3.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.18.0)): `cratis render` passes the model's implementation requirements and resolved code to Stage, so a missing or changed body stops rendering with `STAGE-ESM-020` and the loader's reason. (#184)
- **cli** ([Release v3.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.18.0)): The CLI now bundles Screenplay 4.32.0 and Stage 4.17.0, and `cratis run` defaults to the matching `cratis/stage:4.17.0` image.
- **cli** ([Release v3.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.18.0)): The render and run reference recommends a dedicated folder for models with file attachments: a single-file `cratis run` mounts only the `.play` file.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.28.0 — Chronicle personal data in directly read models](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.28.0)): Read models returned through `ChronicleReadModels` (find, getAll, observe, watch) and read models injected into commands are already decrypted by Chronicle. Arc does not decrypt them a second time.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.28.0 — Chronicle personal data in directly read models](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.28.0)): A protected read model read directly from storage needs a `@subject()` property or an `id` that matches the subject used when its events were appended. Otherwise the query fails.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.28.0 — Chronicle personal data in directly read models](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.28.0)): Reads through `MongoReadModels`, raw driver documents, derived types and mapped objects are served as stored. Release them yourself with the tenant store's `readModels.release`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.28.0 — Chronicle personal data in directly read models](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.28.0)): The Chronicle live-kernel suite needs `ARC_CHRONICLE_TEST_MONGO_URL` when `ARC_CHRONICLE_TEST_URL` points at an existing kernel.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): A join inside `children` now matches children by their identity, as Chronicle does, instead of by the join's `on` property. Specifications where they differ may produce different results.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.17.0)): Code validation, reducers with bodies and code policies are still rejected, and their diagnostics now name only what is still missing: Arc does not supply the received-at time for `RuleContext.Occurred` or `PolicyContext.Occurred`, Stage does not yet enforce the `pure` capability, and reducers wait for Screenplay's typed context descriptor.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.17.0)): The renderer guide describes the attachment envelope, what planning verifies, and that a single-file model's root is its folder.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.10.0)): A job completes `CompletedWithFailures` when any step is unreachable, not only when a step failed. `JobProgress.IsCompleted` and `IsStopped` count unreachable steps. (#27)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.10.0)): `Start` returns once the first stage is running for a job with stages. Its start errors describe that stage, and later stages report their start failures through the job. (#27)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.7.1)): The README, documentation, JSDoc and console samples now use inferred read models instead of the deprecated `@readModel` decorator. Existing applications need no changes, and `@readModel` is still supported.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.27.0 — Drizzle read models in commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.27.0)): Only read models whose table declares exactly one column with `.primaryKey()` and a model `@field` on it can be injected into commands. Other read models still serve queries. A command that injects one of them fails when the application is built.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.27.0 — Drizzle read models in commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.27.0)): A read model registered with both Chronicle and Drizzle, and injected into a command, fails when the application is built.
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): The Screenplay skills now describe Screenplay 4.31.0. Every example in them compiles with that version with zero diagnostics, and both complete examples pass the reference runner.
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): Reaction triggers can declare the views an automation decides from: `reads <View> [as <alias>] [by <trigger value>]`. The skills cover:
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): the alias rules, which are the same as for commands (`PLAY0410`–`PLAY0412`);
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): `by` naming a trigger value (`PLAY0442`);
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): clock triggers reading whole views only (`PLAY0443`);
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): the `@reads` escape (`PLAY0175`, `PLAY0444`);
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): `for each <View>` being reserved;
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): trigger reads, like command reads, giving no runtime protection yet.
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): Model authoring explains how the ESM version (v1, v2 or v3) follows from what a model uses. It separates four states: parsed, bound, reference-executed and target-executed. A table checked against the binder lists what binds, what binds only as opaque code, what binds but the reference execution plan refuses, and what blocks binding.
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): Model authoring covers code attachments (loading, content hashes, source maps) and describes how comments are kept and reviewed.
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): Command guidance covers read aliases, validation severity, the `matches email` pattern, authorization order, one `require` per policy, what `produces` can bind, and `unique` constraints with their lifecycle forms.
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): Projection guidance follows how Chronicle interprets projections: an unkeyed `from` routes by event source, `every` runs for a level's `from` and `join` events, and `join`, `all` and auto-map behave as Chronicle lowers them. It also covers bodied reducers as opaque code and projection variants.
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): Specification guidance is rewritten for `given caller`, `then denied`, `when append`, scenarios without a `when`, subset versus `exactly` comparisons, events in any order, and typed values. It includes one complete example whose six specifications pass the reference runner.
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): Query and screen guidance states what `from` parameters and `observable` guarantee, which query shapes bind, how to specify query authorization, and how UI interactions (`behavior`, `on`, `uses`) differ from application triggers.
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): Captures and reactions guidance scopes the unknown-trigger warning and describes reaction code as an attachment.
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): Decision 0006 is shown as shipped in 4.31.0. Decisions 0007–0014 are marked as not yet available.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): A trigger value named `reads` must now be written `@reads`. A bare `reads` line or `reads <PrimitiveType>` under a trigger reports a diagnostic that names the escape instead of being misread.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): Clock triggers (`every`, `at`) can read whole views but cannot use `by`, since they take no values.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.31.0)): Neither command nor reaction reads bind at runtime yet; editor guidance says so. `for each <View>` stays reserved.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.16.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.16.2)): A model whose authorization references an opaque (code) policy still fails with `STAGE-ESM-015`, and the diagnostic now names what is missing: the policy's implementation attachment is not part of the render request, and Arc has no exact received-at time for `PolicyContext.Occurred`. Stage never emits a policy with a substituted time or a predicate that silently denies.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.16.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.16.2)): The renderer guide documents that a keyed query's policy subject is the query key, matching Screenplay's reference evaluator.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.24.0 — Core namespaces and clean builds](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.24.0)): Builder methods added by integration packages (`withChronicle`, `withMongoDB`, `withDrizzle`, `addCratis`) are typed through a builder extension interface, so they type-check wherever the builder is used, including in `@cratis/arc.testing` and in installed packages.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.24.0 — Core namespaces and clean builds](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.24.0)): Core modules live in the namespaces that own them, as in Arc.Core.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.23.0 — Chronicle read model queries](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.23.0)): The Library sample reads and observes its read models through `ChronicleReadModels`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.16.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.16.1)): A model with reducer implementation bodies still fails with `STAGE-ESM-019`. The diagnostic now names what is missing: Screenplay's per-transition typed context descriptor for `State` and `Event` (ContextVersion 1, ResultVersion 1), tracked in Cratis/Screenplay#139. (part of #119)
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.22.0 — grouped options](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.22.0)): `ArcServerOptions` is now `ArcOptions`, grouped like .NET: `correlationId.httpHeader`, `tenancy.httpHeader` and `tenancy.resolve`, `generatedApis` (including `enableQueryMethod` and `openApiVersion`), `hosting` (`maxBodyBytes`, `applicationUrl`) and `query` (observable limits, allowed origins, emission guards, health and keep-alive). Configuration files and `Cratis__Arc__...` environment variables use the same .NET paths, and `Query:KeepAliveInterval` accepts `hh:mm:ss`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.22.0 — grouped options](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.22.0)): `exposeExceptionDetails` controls whether HTTP results include exception details and defaults to on only in the Development environment; `development` now only enables development discovery of users and tenants.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.22.0 — grouped options](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.22.0)): `builder.withChronicle()`, `withMongoDB()`, `withDrizzle()` and `addCratis()` are declared by their own packages, so `@cratis/arc.core` no longer depends on knowing them.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.22.0 — grouped options](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.22.0)): Tenant resolution is shared by HTTP requests and observable query connections.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): The semantic engine reports `loading` while it rebuilds, and refuses requests until it is ready. After any append by another client it stops serving requests rather than drift from the log. (part of #23)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.16.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.16.0)): A rebuild is refused for event contracts with date-time or decimal properties, because Chronicle's storage changes those values (offsets, precision). It is also refused for null optional values, non-text destinations, a `causedBy` name that changed over time, and duplicate tags. The bundled sandbox container resets its kernel on restart, so rebuilding applies when the host runs against an external or persistent Chronicle. (part of #23)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): Update to Screenplay 4.30.0 and Chronicle 19.4.8, including the Host's bundled kernel image and the Chronicle client and image pinned for generated applications.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): Screenplay ESM v3 models are admitted. Their new constructs fail closed:
- **Stage** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): reducers with implementation bodies are rejected with `STAGE-ESM-019`;
- **Stage** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): code validations and rule predicates are rejected with `STAGE-ESM-005`;
- **Stage** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): opaque policies are rejected with `STAGE-ESM-015`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.21.0 — vertical slices and the Library sample](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.21.0)): `@cratis/arc.chronicle` requires `@cratis/chronicle` 6.7.0. Read models are recognized from the projections, reducers and projection decorators that build them, so an Arc `@readModel()` class with Chronicle projection decorators needs no second read-model decorator.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.21.0 — vertical slices and the Library sample](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.21.0)): Documentation, shared examples and the Tasks sample use vertical slice files and create events with their constructors, for example `return new AuthorRegistered(this.name)`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.20.0 — Fetch runtimes](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.20.0)): `@cratis/arc.core` remains the Node.js entry with discovery, configuration files, the standalone host, static files and WebSockets.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.19.0 — setup, configuration and host integrations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.19.0)): Shutting down Express, Fastify and Hono hosts no longer waits forever for connected SSE and WebSocket clients.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.18.0 — RxJS observable queries](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.18.0)): MongoDB `observe()` and `observeById()` and Chronicle read model `watch()` return RxJS observables. Code that iterated their previous async iterables should switch to the `*Iterable()` methods.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.18.0 — RxJS observable queries](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.18.0)): RxJS 7 is an optional peer dependency of `@cratis/arc.core`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.17.0 — OpenAPI results and proxy improvements](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.17.0)): With `--root-namespace`, generated model files are written under the root namespace folder, like other generated files. Update imports that referenced the previous model paths.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.17.0 — OpenAPI results and proxy improvements](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.17.0)): Decorator options the generator cannot read statically fail generation with a diagnostic instead of being ignored.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): The structural specification engine is deprecated. `ISpecificationRunner`, `SpecificationRunner` and the structural run strategies are marked `[Obsolete]`, and SpecRunner prints a one-line notice on stderr when it runs the structural engine. The default engine and exit codes are unchanged; use `--engine semantic` or `ISemanticSpecificationExecutor`. The structural engine is removed in the next major version. (part of #15)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): The scoped projection shapes that still fail with `STAGE-ESM-017` now state the verified cause. Clearing and then re-creating a nested object fails the projection on MongoDB (Cratis/Chronicle#4166). Removing a joined child across several parents diverges only in Chronicle's testing engine (Cratis/Chronicle#4167). (part of #117)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.7.0)): `@readModel()` is deprecated. Existing identifiers set through it keep working; new code can drop the decorator.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.7.0)): Registering two read models with the same identifier fails with an error instead of letting one silently replace the other.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.16.0 — Chronicle aggregates and reactor commands](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.16.0)): `@cratis/arc.chronicle` requires `@cratis/chronicle` 6.6.0 or later.
- **AI** ([Release v6.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.2.0)): `Providers/Configuring` is documented as deliberately excluded from the flat barrel: it declares commands with the same names as those in `Adding`, `Reconfiguring`, `Removing` and `Renaming`, so exporting both is ambiguous. Import it by path.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): In the semantic engine, keyed queries are served with their modeled authorization. The compatibility `Get…ById`/`All…` queries are served only for read models whose keyed queries are unprotected. (part of #23)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.13.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.13.0)): The semantic engine refuses a model it cannot compile, or a Chronicle log that is not empty, with a typed status instead of starting partially. If an append's outcome is unknown, it stops serving requests rather than drift from the event log. (part of #23)
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): `ConfiguredAIProvider` carries the provider's endpoint, tier mapping, usage credential, usage ceiling, rate-limit cooldown and discovered catalog. Its properties are non-nullable with sentinel defaults: a nullable property on a `[Passive]` read model reads back null from a running kernel however the events were recorded, while in-process scenarios populate it happily - so a value could be configured, recorded, and invisible to the code that resolves a provider, with nothing failing.
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): `AnthropicProviderClient` takes the Claude Code transport alongside its quota tracker.
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): `ReconfigureOpenAIProvider` and `AddOpenAIProvider` record which kind of credential arrived, and refuse a subscription record that is too incomplete to be spent.
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): `ProviderConcurrencyGate` is a singleton. It holds per-provider semaphores in instance state and was registered nowhere, so every resolution got a fresh set and the gate limited nothing.
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): Claude Code usage reports cached tokens separately instead of folding them into the input count - a cache read is billed differently from a fresh input token.
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): The three event types that carried an explicit GUID identifier now key on their type name like every other event type. No stored event anywhere used those GUIDs.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.13.0 — observability, interceptors and derived types](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.13.0)): Properties of `@field`-declared models are written in camelCase on the wire, including PascalCase and acronym-leading names, the same way Arc on .NET names them. Plain objects, dictionaries and low-level Zod results are sent as they are, and generated proxies use the same names.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.13.0 — observability, interceptors and derived types](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.13.0)): In-memory query results are paged before read-model interceptors run, as in .NET.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.13.0 — observability, interceptors and derived types](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.13.0)): `@opentelemetry/api` is a peer dependency of `@cratis/arc.core`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.13.0 — observability, interceptors and derived types](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.13.0)): The `correlationHeader` option rejects invalid header names.
- **AI** ([Release v5.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v5.0.0)): A model tier that no operator mapped is now resolved from the models the provider actually published, rather than from a built-in per-vendor table of model names. A tier can no longer resolve to a model a provider does not serve.
- **AI** ([Release v5.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v5.0.0)): `TierModelResolution.Resolve` takes the provider's available models in place of its `AIProviderType`. An explicit mapping still wins, tier by tier.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.12.0 — Chronicle event sourcing](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.12.0)): Command execution runners in `@cratis/arc.core` are composable, so several integrations can wrap command execution.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.12.0 — Chronicle event sourcing](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.12.0)): Commands that return both Chronicle events and command operations are rejected before either runs, because the event batch does not yet take part in operation compensation.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.12.0 — Chronicle event sourcing](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.12.0)): `@cratis/arc.chronicle` requires `@cratis/chronicle` 6.5.1 or later.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.30.0)): `CodeBlockSyntax` and `SemanticImplementationRequirement` compare body line maps by value, so two parses of the same text are equal. Positions do not take part in syntax structural equality, so moving a body inside a document never produces a workspace change on its own.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): The reference evaluator does not run policy code. Authorization is evaluated left to right in authored order, as Stage renders it (`&&`/`||`) and Arc evaluates it (in declaration order, stopping at the first false): a portable operand that decides first still decides (`Portable and Custom` denies when `Portable` denies), but once policy code would run, the outcome is unsupported, never allowed or denied. The same order applies across module, feature and construct gates. A specification whose outcome depends on policy code never passes.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): The documented `IsAdultCustomer` example no longer reads the clock or throws on a malformed claim: it uses `context.Occurred` and `DateTime.TryParse`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.8.0 — MongoDB integration](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.8.0)): `@key()` also marks a read model's identity for storage integrations.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): Scoped projection constructs that remain rejected now name their exact blocker in the diagnostic. They are composite keys, `all` subscriptions, root join removals, nested clear followed by re-creation, child removal across several parents, literal mappings (Cratis/Chronicle#4124), and joins or children inside nested blocks (Cratis/Chronicle#4125). (part of #117)
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.7.0 — command context, response handlers and operations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.7.0)): `tuple(...)` groups values for response value handlers with at most one client response; two unhandled values fail the command.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.6.0 — proxies from TypeScript source](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.6.0)): `@cratis/arc.core` exports `routeFor`, `includeRouteName` and the artifact discovery walk so tools can compute the same routes as the server.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): `readModels.getInstanceById` is deprecated in favor of `findInstanceById`; its behavior is unchanged.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.10.0)): An accepted command with no destination and no stated event source is reported `Unsupported(IdentityAllocation)` after validation, as in the reference, instead of failing. Projections, queries, given read models and implicit identity allocation remain `Unsupported`. (part of #15)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.9.0)): Command properties that feed a constrained event property get a not-null validation rule, so a null value never reaches the unique index (Cratis/Chronicle#4122).
- **Stage** ([Release v4.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.9.0)): Projection shapes where Chronicle keys, clears or removes differently from the reference now fail with `STAGE-ESM-017`. This covers one event contract in several roles at the root, and nested `from`/`clear` blocks without a matching root `from`. It also covers clearing a nested block and then re-creating it, and removing a child joined to several parents.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.8.0)): The first increment executes commands with explicit-message `not empty`, `minimum` and `maximum` validation, unconditional event mappings, given events with a stated event source, event and rejection assertions, and cancellation. Projections, queries, given read models, authorization, requirements, constraints and identity allocation are reported `Unsupported`, because Chronicle 19.4.7 cannot run a projection per specification through public APIs. The executor replaces Arc's process-wide service provider, so run it in its own process, as SpecRunner does. (part of #15)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): The MCP server loads attachment contents from its trusted root, so `implementation-requirements` shows a content hash for each file attachment, and a changed attachment changes its hash. Unchanged attachments reuse the analyzed workspace, and proposals survive an attachment edit.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `SemanticDocumentSet.Create` normalizes attachment keys, so `./X.cs`, `.\X.cs` and `X.cs` name the same attachment and hash. Two supplied keys that normalize to the same path with different contents are treated as a conflict, not resolved by picking one.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): `PlayFileCompiler` and the standalone Tool compile syntax only and do not read attachments; the documentation now says so (#244).
- **Stage** ([Release v4.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.7.0)): Query arguments typed as a validated concept are rejected with `STAGE-ESM-010`, because Arc validates them and the reference does not.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): ESM v3 now means "ESM with implementation attachments": a model is v3 when it has a reducer with a body or code validation. Models with neither keep their v1 or v2 bytes exactly.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): The reference evaluator cannot run code validation. Executing a command guarded by it, directly or through a concept on one of its properties (including inside composite types and collections), reports an unsupported outcome naming the command or concept and the rule, and a specification that runs it never passes. Authorization is still evaluated first, so a `then denied` specification on such a command keeps working. Specifications that do not run the command are unaffected.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): A model with two validation attachments sharing a requirement id is rejected, as duplicate reducer transitions already were.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.1 — observable hosting fixes](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.1)): `runArc` owns WebSocket upgrades for Arc paths on `host.server`; attach your own upgrade handlers only for other paths.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.1 — observable hosting fixes](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.1)): Internal `ArcServer` hosting members are no longer part of the published type declarations; host integrations use `@cratis/arc.core/hosting`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): The reference runner does not compute reducer state. A specification that reads a reducer-built read model, through `given readmodel`, `then readmodel` or `then query`, reports an unsupported outcome naming the reducer and never passes; other specifications in the model run as before. Calling the evaluator directly reports the same for a query over, or returned state of, a reducer-built read model.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): A file-backed implementation attachment is no longer hashed from its path, so moving a file no longer changes its hash and editing it is no longer invisible.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.26.0)): A consumer that accepts only ESM v1 or v2 now receives a v3 model for documents with bodied reducers, where binding used to fail with "requires a portable reducer contract". Reducers without bodies are still rejected with the same message.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.5.0)): `Task Resume(JobId)` becomes `Task<bool> Resume(JobId)`. A call that ignores the result is unaffected at source level; an implementer of the interface is not. A job that fails to *start* is a different situation and keeps its existing handling.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): A `require` path qualified with a view the command reads more than once now says to use one of that view's aliases and lists them, instead of claiming the command does not read the view.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): PLAY0175 also reports `as`, `by` or `reads` used as an alias. Aliases, like `by` properties, are lower-case identifiers.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): Decision 0003 notes that its runtime precondition is only partly met (tracked in Cratis/Chronicle#4138).
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): Packages are renamed and laid out like Arc on .NET: `@cratis/arc.core` (was `@cratis/arc.server`), `@cratis/arc.express`, `@cratis/arc.fastify`, `@cratis/arc.hono`, `@cratis/arc.mongodb`, `@cratis/arc.chronicle` (still private and experimental), `@cratis/arc.testing` (was the `@cratis/arc.server/testing` export) and `@cratis/arc.proxygenerator` with the `arc-proxygenerator` CLI (was `@cratis/arc.server.codegen`). All source lives under `Source/<Package>`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): The standalone host that was prototyped as `@cratis/arc.server.node` ships inside `@cratis/arc.core`, the way Arc on .NET hosts itself from `Arc.Core`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): The build uses TypeScript 7.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.24.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.24.2)): The invoicing sample, the language's largest example, now declares every read model, type and event it uses and gives its authorized specifications an explicit caller. It binds to the executable semantic model with only the unsupported constructs it shows on purpose left, and a specification pins that list so the sample cannot drift from the language again (#240)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.6.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.6.1)): Update dependency versions and required runtime compatibility settings.
- **AI** ([Release v4.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v4.0.0)): `AIProviderApiKey` is `[Encrypted(EncryptionScope.Namespace)]`. Chronicle encrypts it on append and decrypts it on read, keyed per event store namespace — the same boundary the per-tenant protectors it replaces already used.
- **AI** ([Release v4.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v4.0.0)): Command handlers that only awaited the protector are now synchronous.
- **AI** ([Release v4.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v4.0.0)): `Cratis.Arc.Chronicle` is a direct dependency, which also enables the `ARCCHR` analyzers. `ARCCHR0009` fails the build when a command carries a credential-shaped property that is not excluded from the causation chain.
- **AI** ([Release v4.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v4.0.0)): Chronicle 18.1.6 → 19.4.7, Arc 22.14.0 → 22.23.0.
- **AI** ([Release v4.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v4.0.0)): Twenty event types no longer pass their own name as an explicit `[EventType]` id. The identifier each one resolves to is unchanged — the type name is the default.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.6.0)): Update Stage to Cratis/Arc 22.23.0, Chronicle 19.4.7 and Scene 4.2.0. The Host image now bundles the `cratis/chronicle:19.4.7-development` kernel.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.6.0)): Generated applications now pin Arc 22.23.0, an explicit Cratis.Chronicle 19.4.7 client with the matching `cratis/chronicle:19.4.7-development` image, and the Scene 4.2.0 frontend packages.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.6.0)): Update `Cratis.Screenplay` and `Cratis.Screenplay.CanonicalCorpus` to 4.24.0. Specifications may now use `then events in any order` and `exactly`, and they render as before. (#128)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.6.0)): Reject commands and queries that are authorized by a Screenplay policy (`STAGE-ESM-015`), and specifications that use `given caller` or `then denied` (`STAGE-ESM-011`). The Cratis planner does not render authorization yet, so a rendered application would let any caller through. (#57, #13)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.6.0)): Update the Billing sample to the fenced inline-code form, because Screenplay 4.24 deprecates the old form (`PLAY0397`).
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): Standard-mode schema errors are collected and reported together before the client registers any artifact with the Kernel.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): Documentation shows standard-mode `@field` declarations and labels constructor-property examples as legacy-mode.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.5.0)): Update `Cratis.Screenplay` and `Cratis.Screenplay.CanonicalCorpus` to 4.20.0. (#128)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.5.0)): Reject ESM v2 constructs the Cratis planner cannot yet render exactly, instead of treating them as v1. Rendering fails with a typed diagnostic in these cases (#128, #13):
- **Stage** ([Release v4.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.5.0)): `$context` occurrence mappings: `STAGE-ESM-013`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.5.0)): Stated specification sources that disagree, have the wrong type, or are not text or UUID: `STAGE-ESM-011`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.5.0)): Whenless specifications: `STAGE-ESM-011`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.5.0)): Projections keyed on the event source: `STAGE-ESM-008`.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.5.0)): Reject command requirements (`STAGE-ESM-005`), guarded productions and event tags (`STAGE-ESM-006`), and append-time constraints (`STAGE-ESM-014`). The planner does not render these yet, so the generated application would append events the reference evaluator rejects, or tag them differently. (#118, #116)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.5.0)): Reject validation rules at warning or information severity (`STAGE-ESM-005`) until the generated validator can report that severity. (#116)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.24.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.24.0)): `PlayFileWriter.Expand` uses the same folder-layout projection as workspace layout changes. `folders.md` explains which parts of `PlayFileCompiler.CompileFolder` and `WriteTo` stay independent of the workspace and why (#137)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.22.0)): Claim types compare case-insensitively; role names and claim values compare exactly; a claim carried more than once matches when any of its values matches. A missing claim, a null target or an unresolvable subject denies. This matches how Arc evaluates authorization through .NET's `ClaimsPrincipal`, and `Identity.ClaimValue`/`ClaimValues` now look claim types up case-insensitively too (#142)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.22.0)): A specification that exercises an authorized command without a `given caller` is reported when binding. Caller identity is never inferred (#142)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.21.0)): A `file` constraint warns (`PLAY0396`): Chronicle only supports unique constraints, so declare them with `unique …`, and put other rules in validation or `require`. The constraints page now states Chronicle's actual guarantee (#223)
- **Arc.Kotlin** ([Release v7.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Kotlin/releases/tag/v7.6.0)): The JVM parity reference links to the shared C#/Kotlin/Java capability matrix while retaining its implementation evidence (#217).
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): Canonical printing, folder expansion, MCP `expand-layout` and `propose-ast` edits keep comments with the declarations they annotate (#220)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): `$strings.` messages are symbolic string keys in the executable model. A malformed key is reported (`PLAY0357`), and a reference rejection marks its message as a key (#224)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.19.0)): `null` in a command or event value is rejected with the reason (`PLAY0350`): an optional fact is a separate event (#207)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.2.0)): Reserve the top-level `Customizations/` directory for product-owned files. Models that would generate files there, including case variants, now fail with `STAGE-CRATIS-005` and no candidate artifacts. (#121)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.2.0)): Reject malformed or version-mismatched authored Scene payloads with `STAGE-CRATIS-001` before producing artifacts. This validates the JSON wire shape, not component bindings or semantic revision provenance. (#100, #13)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.2.0)): Fail runtime queries when Chronicle returns an unreadable document, rather than silently omitting it and returning partial data. (#10)
- **AI** ([Release v3.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.4.0)): A feed post announcing what shipped may carry one plain list of real capabilities inside its argument, with no bolded labels and no slogan per item.
- **AI** ([Release v3.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.4.0)): A company account's copy says "we", the way the team would say it, instead of the company name in the third person.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.1.0)): Update Stage and generated applications to Cratis Arc 22.22.0, Chronicle 19.4.4, and Scene 4.1.0. Generated applications explicitly pin their Chronicle client, ASP.NET Core integration, and testing packages to the kernel version.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.1.0)): Update generated frontends to Components 4.13.1 and Fundamentals 7.19.4.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): Canonical printing keeps the authored order of declarations, also through MCP `propose-ast` edits and folder expansion (#221)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): MCP `PreserveTrivia` edits replace literal values and whole mappings in place at every mapping site, keeping comments and blank lines (#216, #222)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): `propose-rename` is refused only when opaque text actually names the declaration being renamed, and the conflict states the file, line and column (#215)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): A projection-level `key` no longer decides which instance an event updates; key it on `from`, as Chronicle does (#219)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): A reducer without a transition body is reported with guidance to declare a projection instead (#213)
- **cli** ([Release v3.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.14.0)): `cratis run` mounts a Screenplay folder read-only (#128)
- **cli** ([Release v3.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.14.0)): `cratis run` finds `.play` files in a folder in any casing on every platform (#128)
- **cli** ([Release v3.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.14.0)): `cratis run` rejects a missing path, a file without the `.play` extension, or a path containing a colon before Docker is started (#128)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.21.0)): Analyzer `ARC0020` now reports an ASP.NET Core authorization attribute only in a project without Arc's ASP.NET Core integration (`Cratis.Arc`), since that integration now enforces it (#2719)
- **AI** ([Release v3.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.2.0)): `ConfiguredAIProvider` now projects the Decision Engine provider events (#392)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.19.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.19.2)): `Cratis.Chronicle`/`Cratis.Chronicle.AspNetCore`/`Cratis.Chronicle.Testing` bumped from `18.2.0` to `19.4.2` to pick up `[Encrypted]` and the read-model release fix it depends on; `Cratis.Fundamentals`/`Cratis.Metrics.Roslyn` bumped from `7.19.2` to `7.19.3` alongside it
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.2)): `SecurityJsonSchemaExtensions` gains `HasSubjectIndependentSecurityMetadata`, built on a new predicate-based recursive schema walk in `SchemaMetadataExtensions`, so `Release` can tell a namespace-/global-scoped value apart from a subject-scoped (`[PII]`, or `[Encrypted]` with the default `EncryptionScope.Subject`) one before deciding whether a missing subject means "nothing to release"
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.5.0)): `cratis_chronicle_contracts` bumped from `19.3.2` to `19.4.0`
- **Chronicle.Kotlin** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Kotlin/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): `chronicleContractsVersion` bumped from `19.3.2` to `19.4.0`
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): `@cratis/chronicle.contracts` bumped from `19.0.1` to `19.4.0`
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.2)): Tutorials start from the smallest *safe* setup and a convention-first snippet, and link the paradigm explanation (event sourcing, event modeling) without delaying the first success.
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.2)): Explanations of what happened after a code block must match the backend the reader actually built.
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.2)): AI-drafted narrative gets a revision pass against source evidence and voice before it ships.
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.2)): Coming-from-X pages follow a consistent shape: the reader's current code, the Cratis equivalent, and what changed. The other technology's code is accurate at a named version or labeled illustrative.
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.2)): Diagrams, use-case indexes and diagnostic documentation are asked for where they help, not on every page.
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.2)): Release notes state verified cross-product pairings, include a fix's root cause and regression guard as observable behavior, credit external contributors, and state a migration table's scope without claiming compatibility that was not assessed.
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.2)): Guidance on how a merged documentation change reaches cratis.io, and that restructuring navigation is not a side effect of writing one page.
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.1)): The `write-documentation` prompt and the orchestrator's documentation step now load `cratis-documentation-writing` (with `cratis-engineering-docs-authoring` and `cratis-technical-examples`) instead of a skill that no longer exists.
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.1)): `cratis-documentation-writing` is now also available from the engineering core profile.
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.1)): Documentation guidance treats Diátaxis as a guide to each page's primary reader purpose rather than a purity rule. It allows brief supporting context, separate language or host start routes, and page sets scaled to the product.
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.1)): Example guidance distinguishes partial excerpts from runnable samples and states which snippets the Arc and Chronicle validators actually compile.
- **Stage** ([Release v3.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v3.22.0)): Use Scene 3.7.0 and Components 4.13.0 in generated frontends for editable command and keyed-query forms. (#100)
- **Stage** ([Release v3.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v3.22.0)): Align generated backend and frontend packages on Arc 22.19.1 while retaining the Chronicle 18.2.0 kernel. (#100)
- **Scene** ([Release v3.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v3.7.0)): Require Components 4.13.0 and Arc/Arc React 22.19.1 for the new form contract while retaining Arc-free unbound previews. (#7)
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.0)): AI setup preserves unrelated MCP servers, settings and comments; conflicting or modified entries are reported instead of overwritten.
- **cli** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): Preserve existing MCP servers, comments and unrelated settings during install, update and uninstall. User-owned or modified Screenplay entries are reported as conflicts rather than overwritten.
- **cli** ([Release v3.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.11.0)): Keep MCP standard output exclusively JSON-RPC and suppress CLI update checks during a protocol session.
- **AI** ([Release v3.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.0.0)): `AgentUsageByDay` is now a real accumulating projection instead of a `[Passive]` read model. It previously recomputed a year of buckets with an in-memory `GroupBy` over every session recorded in the trailing 371-day window on every read, a cost that grows without bound with the number of sessions to answer a question whose answer only changes by one session at a time. `AgentUsageByWeek` and `AgentUsageByMonth` were already real projections; the daily model is now keyed and accumulated the same way.
- **AI** ([Release v3.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.0.0)): `AgentUsageByDay.LastYear` now reads the accumulated buckets from its own collection and takes an `IMongoCollection<AgentUsageByDay>` and a `TimeProvider`, replacing the previous `IRecordedAgentSessions` parameter.
- **AI** ([Release v3.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.0.0)): `AgentUsageByDay` now exposes the day as a `DayKey` and carries `BucketKey`, `CachedTokens` and `DurationMs`; total session time remains available as the `Duration` property.
- **AI** ([Release v2.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v2.31.0)): Center documentation on reader journeys, intended idioms, minimal first-run setup, and visible results, with source-checked examples and explicit drift checks.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.19.0)): A query request reader that rejects input as invalid now surfaces as the documented `malformedRequest` validation failure rather than an exception envelope. Reader failures that are not validation failures are unchanged (#2715)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.19.0)): The shared HTTP contract documents SSE connection ownership and the sort direction vocabulary as single behaviours, with the remaining narrower difference in what each implementation compares for ownership stated explicitly (#2713)
- **AI** ([Release v2.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v2.30.0)): `cratis/documentation` gains the writing cadence skill, which applies to documentation equally.
- **AI** ([Release v2.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v2.30.0)): Harness skill-count assertions move from 63 to 65.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.1.8](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.1.8)): `IReadModels.Release` for a sequence now resolves the schema once for the whole sequence instead of once per instance, and hands the sequence back untouched when the read model carries no compliance metadata at all.
- **Components** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): Each `<subpath>/styles` is self-contained for that subpath — `@cratis/components/PivotViewer/styles` includes `Filter`'s rules because `PivotViewer` renders a `FilterPanel`, and `@cratis/components/DataPage/styles` includes the vendored Allotment split-view rules — so there is never a second stylesheet to remember (#301)
- **Components** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): Import order between per-area stylesheets does not matter: only `styles/base` establishes cascade-layer precedence, every area sheet just reopens `cratis-components`, and the cascade contract is identical whichever entry points are used (#301)
- **Components** ([Release v4.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.11.0)): Getting-started and styling documentation now present the per-area path alongside the aggregate, and `Documentation/ui-foundation.md` records the split instead of the criteria for a future one (#301)
- **cli** ([Release v3.9.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.9.0)): Rendering a Screenplay now produces a full application rather than a backend alone: the generated project gains a frontend that builds, a composed screen, and a module registering its generated proxies against that screen. (Cratis/Stage#100)

## Fixed

- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.14.2)): A reactor, reducer, read-model reactor or event type that carries `[EventLog]`/`[EventSequence]` is no longer also registered as an empty model-bound projection. Before this, a reactor pinned to an event sequence stopped receiving events. Model-bound read models that use those attributes, including record structs, are still discovered. (#4310)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.14.2)): Synchronous reactor handlers can return arrays and lists of events (`SomeEvent[]`, `List<SomeEvent>`, `IReadOnlyList<SomeEvent>`, any single-dimensional array or `IEnumerable<T>` of known events, `EventForEventSourceId` or `object`). Discovery and invocation agree on these return types, and the CHR0054 analyzer no longer reports them as unsupported. Multidimensional arrays are rejected. (#4121)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.14.2)): Release builds of the client and analyzer projects and their specs with 0 warnings.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.14.2)): `DotNET.Specs` 3,350/3,350 and `DotNET.CodeAnalysis.Specs` 415/415.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.14.2)): `Documentation/verify-markdown.sh`.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.14.2)): The local CI-equivalent gate is green.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.14.2)): A cross-provider review passed with no remaining findings after two rounds.
- **cli** ([Release v3.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.21.0)): **`cratis chronicle login` now saves the token** (#118). Before, it reported "Logged in as …" but threw the token away, so every later command fell back to the development client and got 401 from any secured server. Login now fails with a clear error when the server returns no usable token. When a saved token expires, commands report an expired login instead of silently switching to the development client, unless the context has its own client credentials. Tokens and certificate passwords are hidden in `--debug`, diagnose and workbench output.
- **cli** ([Release v3.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.21.0)): **The CLI update h
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.12.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.12.1)): `Cratis.Orleans` now depends directly on Orleans 10 Reminders, so the MongoDB provider's Orleans 9 dependency no longer pulls `Microsoft.Orleans.Reminders` 9.0.1 into consumers and trips the package's own alignment guard.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.9-hotfix.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.9-hotfix.1)): Hotfix on the 19.4 line for kernels that cannot yet move to 19.13: a replay of a projection no longer leaves its MongoDB read model frozen when the replay ends on more than one kernel silo at once, or overlaps another replay of the same projection. Also carries the replay-context fixes from 19.13.3. 19.13.3 has the same fix for everyone already on 19.13. (#4296)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.22.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.22.1)): Append results now carry the constraint name Chronicle returns, so constraint violations, including schema-validation failures, have their `constraintId`, and configured constraint messages resolve.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.22.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.22.1)): The TypeScript appending example shows schema constraint violations and their property paths.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.44.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.44.0)): Comments on persona descriptions, query descriptions, constraint headers, unique rules, `released by`, `ignore casing`, `message`, and query `by` / `scoped to` lines now stay attached to their line when a document is reformatted or edited.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.13.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.13.3)): A replay of a projection no longer leaves its MongoDB read model frozen when the replay ends on more than one kernel silo at once, or overlaps another replay of the same projection. The replay is promoted exactly once, and every silo goes back to writing the read model itself. Before, a silo that lost the race kept sending every live change to `replay-<container>`, with the observer still reporting `Active` and no error recorded (#4296)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.13.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.13.3)): A replay no longer fails to end because the revert container name from a previous replay of the same read model is reused (#4296)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.13.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.13.3)): Starting a replay on several silos at once no longer fails with a duplicate key on `replay-contexts` (#4296)
- **cli** ([Release v3.20.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.20.1)): `cratis new --language` help, validation errors and documentation now list C#, Kotlin and Java, including the `c#` alias. They also state that Kotlin and Java currently require naming the template, for example `cratis new cratis --language kotlin`.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.13.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.13.2)): The `MissingImmediateProjectionForModel` exception no longer tells you to implement an interface that does not exist.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.41.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.41.2)): When the PostgreSQL observation manager was disposed without awaiting it and a listener close then failed, the disposal failure surfaced as an unhandled rejection, which can terminate a Node.js process. The failure is now handled internally and still returned to callers that await disposal.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.41.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.41.2)): The listener manager specs no longer depend on wall-clock timing, which made one of them fail intermittently under load.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.13.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.13.1)): `WaitForCompletion()` no longer fails with an `InvalidOperationException` when the append result carries event-type tails. The request's repeated `EventTypeTails` field defaulted to an array, which protobuf-net cannot add to while decoding, so every non-empty request failed on the server (#4280)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.13.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.13.1)): A `WaitForCompletion()` timeout response that lists outstanding observers now decodes on the client, for the same reason (#4280)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.13.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.13.1)): Round-trip specifications under `Server.Specs/Contracts/` for non-empty tails, omitted tails and non-empty outstanding observers. The two non-empty cases reproduce the exception without the fix.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.13.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.13.1)): All Server specs pass; Release solution build with warnings as errors and formatting pass.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.13.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.13.1)): A search of every protobuf-net contract found no other `IEnumerable<T>` member with an array default.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([v0.41.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.41.1)): A snapshot query that returns a stream is still rejected with `SnapshotStreamError`, and Arc now releases the returned value exactly once through the first hook it owns: `Symbol.asyncDispose`, `Symbol.dispose`, `dispose()`, `close()`, or `return()` when the value is itself an iterator.
