Arc never subscribes to it or creates an iterator from it, never calls `unsubscribe()` (which would close a shared RxJS `Subject` for every consumer), and never releases values its service container owns. A release failure is reported alongside the rejection, including when the request is cancelled, and a release still running after one second is reported to the server logger. The same rule applies over HTTP and in `QueryScenario`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): Comments stay attached to their line during canonical printing and `expand-layout` for the remaining directive lines. These include:
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): enum values and concept reasons;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): policy requirements and persona policies;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): UI profile, theme and theme-compatibility lines;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): contribution and screen-action labels and order;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): implementation file references;
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): the scalar subdirectives in specifications, captures, projections and commands.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.42.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.42.0)): Comments on enum and other collection values follow their value when a syntax tree or workspace edit reorders, inserts or removes values.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.12.0)): A MongoDB bulk write that fails part way now applies the remaining operations instead of silently dropping them. Later changes for the partition that failed are held back, so they can't overtake its replay. Each failed partition is reported once, at its earliest sequence number (#4244)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.12.0)): Forwarding events to another event store's inbox no longer acknowledges events whose append failed. It stops at the first failure and reports the last event it actually forwarded, so the retry resumes from the failed event (#4259)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.12.0)): Projection keys for immediate (passive) projections round-trip read-model keys that contain `#` (#4241)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.12.0)): `add` and `subtract` projection mappings accept negative, decimal and exponent operands (for example `-1`, `-2.5`, `1e-3`), and reject malformed operands. Exponent literals and integer literals beyond 2^53 in declarations still depend on the declaration parser (Cratis/Screenplay#285) (#4239)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.12.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.12.0)): Composite-key validation for generated declarations resolves the key type from the read model's `Id` schema first, so a same-named type elsewhere in the read model can't be picked by mistake (#4253)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.1)): The local Chronicle image starts its embedded MongoDB only when configured to use it; connections to a separate MongoDB or another storage provider no longer start an unused replica set (#4111).
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.1)): Generating a declarative projection preserves nested mappings, including inner nested blocks and `clear with` rules. Nested composite keys use the nested read model's schema while generating their declarations (#4116).
- **Arc** ([Release v22.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.30.0)): An authentication scheme or policy supplied by a fallback evaluator is applied before command context values and execution scopes are built, instead of failing every such command (#2751)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.30.0)): The ambiguity error for conflicting authorization declarations names the conflicting evaluators (#2751)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.30.0)): Observable queries over collections whose items have no `Id` send complete snapshots instead of unreliable change sets, and log one warning per subscription explaining that deltas need a stable identity. Collections with an `Id` are unchanged (#2428)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.30.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.30.0)): The manual query pipeline composition recipe registers `AuthorizationDeclarations`, which the pipeline needs to authorize queries (#2753)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.11.0)): Observer tail lookups and in-process reactor scenarios recognize cla
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.10.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.10.1)): A projection can clear a nested object and later set it again, including an object nested inside another nested object. The MongoDB sink no longer fails the projection, and the testing engine no longer throws. (#4166)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.10.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.10.1)): Clearing a nested object in MongoDB removes the field with `$unset` instead of storing `null`. A document already stored with a `null` parent from an earlier version is repaired before the nested value is written again. The same applies to a nested object inside an array element. The repair only touches that document's parent, or that one element, and only when it really holds `null`.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.10.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.10.1)): The in-memory sink and the testing engine clear the right property for deeper paths such as `outer.info`. Clearing an object whose parent is missing no longer creates that parent, which matches MongoDB.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.29.0)): Command filters continue past non-blocking validation results, so a later Error rejects execution and preflight validation even when an earlier Warning is allowed (#2817).
- **Arc** ([Release v22.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.29.0)): Client-side command validation blocks only at the effective severity threshold; non-blocking results remain advisory, server preflight still runs, and CommandForm validity follows the validation verdict.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.29.0)): Generated validators emit severity calls only for non-Error rules, retaining compatibility with existing client libraries for default rules. Rules with runtime-dependent severity are left to the server. Documentation describes the default-severity assumption at build time.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.28.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.28.1)): Invalid scalar, concept, and collection arguments on GET and QUERY endpoints now return HTTP 400 with a named malformed-request validation error instead of invoking model-bound or controller queries with default values or reporting invalid values as missing. (#2794)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.27.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.27.2)): API path joins collapse repeated separators at segment boundaries without damaging `http://`, `https://`, or leading and trailing slashes (#2593)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.27.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.27.2)): Path-prefix hosting guidance now covers `apiBasePath`, router basename, ASP.NET Core `UsePathBase` ordering, and Arc.Core static files and fallback (#2593)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.27.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.27.1)): Local functions and other compiler-generated methods inside read models no longer create query endpoints, generated proxies or route conflicts. The runtime, the metadata generator, the proxy generator and the `QueryScenario` testing helpers apply the same rule (#2651)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.27.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.27.1)): Proxies are no longer generated for query methods returning a collection that is not of the read model, such as `string` or `IEnumerable<Other>`, which the runtime never served; covariant collections of a derived read model still get proxies (#2651)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.27.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.27.1)): Multiplexed SSE and WebSocket observable queries keep paging totals on an empty first result and pick up updated totals on later results, without mixing metadata between subscriptions (#2707)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.16.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.16.1)): Event-context projection mappings and context-derived keys now reach the kernel in the form it resolves, `$eventContext(<Property>)`, as Chronicle for .NET sends them. Previously declarative `toEventContextProperty(...)`, `usingKeyFromContext(...)` and `usingParentKeyFromContext(...)` sent `$context.<name>`, which the kernel doesn't recognize, and model-bound `@setFromContext(...)` sent a bare name that the kernel read as an event content property.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.16.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.16.1)): Invalid event-context paths now fail when the projection is registered, naming the projection, read model and path, instead of failing for every event in the kernel. Registration accepts any `EventContext` property, `causedBy.subject`, `causedBy.name`, `causedBy.userName` and `causedBy.onBehalfOf` as a whole, and `occurred.week()`, the kernel's only derived function. Paths into the `causation` and `tags` collections are rejected. The error's cause, `InvalidEventContextPropertyError`, is exported.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.10.0)): `every` mappings, such as `count`, `increment` and `add`, ran twice for join events. They now run once. **Existing read models keep their doubled values until you replay the affected projections.** Their definitions don't change, so no replay is triggered automatically.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.27.0)): ARCCHR0006 recognizes inherited `[Replay]` handlers when checking whether reactor command execution has a replay decision, including handlers on generic base classes (#2790)
- **AI** ([Release v7.1.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.1.1)): A valid 8,000-character context with 32 labels OOM-killed the published image on the production x86 compute pool (exit 137). Groups of four and even a single 8K label also OOM-killed the 2 GiB Apple Silicon container.
- **AI** ([Release v7.1.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.1.1)): Score labels one at a time; keep the first and last 750 characters of longer inference text. The wire contract still accepts 8K and returns all supplied scores, with documented information-loss/latency trade-offs.
- **AI** ([Release v7.1.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.1.1)): Add tests for long-context truncation and 32-label requests.
- **AI** ([Release v7.1.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.1.1)): Local 2 CPU / 2 GiB Docker test: 8K input with 32 labels returned HTTP 200, all 32 scores, in ~16s without OOM.
- **AI** ([Release v7.1.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.1.1)): Packaged image builds and seven adapter tests pass. Follow-up x86 staging validation is required before merging Infrastructure#82.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.14.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.14.3)): A projection definition's `LastUpdated` value now changes when a nested mapping changes, such as a property expression inside a `from`, `join`, `removedWith`, child or nested definition. The hash used to drop nested keys, so those changes left `LastUpdated` unchanged. The kernel compares definitions by structure, so replays were already triggered correctly; only the `LastUpdated` metadata was wrong.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.14.2)): A property-level `@clearWith(Event)` on a member of a model-bound child or nested type now clears that member, as in Chronicle for .NET. Previously the TypeScript client registered it as a removal of the whole child or nested object, so the event could remove the object, or match nothing, instead of setting the member to null.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.26.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.26.1)): The NuGet and npm packages for the changes listed in [v22.26.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.26.0) are published with this release. Publishing v22.26.0 stopped before any package was pushed, because two independently merged changes together broke the TypeScript build; this release contains everything in v22.26.0 and restores the build.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.14.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.14.1)): With standard (TC39) decorators, a read model whose mappings are all on properties, such as `@setFrom(Event, 'name')` without a class-level `@fromEvent(...)`, is now registered when the event store is created, if artifact discovery finds its class or it is registered explicitly. Previously it was registered only after an instance had been created, so queries could fail with "Unknown read model".
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.14.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.14.1)): Registering such a model after an instance has been created no longer risks registering it twice.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.8.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.8.1)): Composite-key expressions in projection declarations resolve on the kernel. The kernel, the declaration-language generator and the declarative code generator now share one `$composite(...)` parser. It accepts an optional leading key type name, and spacing between components doesn't matter. This is part of #4163.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.8.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.8.1)): Malformed composite-key expressions fail with a clear error instead of resolving to a wrong key. That covers an invalid type name, duplicate or empty components, and whitespace-only content.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.8.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.8.1)): Composite keys that contain characters such as `-`, `@` or `:` in their mappings are now resolved as composite keys. Previously they fell through to value resolution, which could put every event into one read model instance. **If a projection used such a key, replay it after upgrading.** New events go to the correct instances, but the old instance stays until you replay.
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.1)): `CommandStepper` and `StepperCommandDialog` now call `onChangeStep({ index })` once for every accepted move to a different step: header clicks, Previous, and Next. Previous and Next never reported a change before, and a header click reported one even when the move was then rejected. (#249)
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.1)): Each stepper panel is now labelled by its step header through `aria-labelledby`. If you pass `pt.header.id`, your id is kept and the panel keeps its text label. The wizard stays an ordered list of steps rather than ARIA tabs. (#249)
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.1)): PivotViewer boolean dimensions now group into true and false columns, and their categorical filters narrow the cards, including the None and Unknown options for missing values. (#334)
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.1)): PivotViewer keeps missing values missing instead of turning them into the text `undefined`, and infers each column's type from its first non-missing value. Missing numbers sort last. Because of this, a numeric column whose first value is missing is now treated as numeric, so a categorical selection on it no longer narrows the cards, as for every other numeric column. (#334)
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.1)): PivotViewer now applies a dimension's declared `formatValue` to column labels and `sort` to column order. Changing either no longer re-runs the grouping. (#334)
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.1)): PivotViewer no longer renders an empty viewport after regrouping or filtering when the new layout's first entries are off-screen. Cards that intersect the viewport are drawn during the transition, and cards that move off-screen are released when it ends. Refs #236: the intermittent crash reported there is not reproduced, so that issue stays open.
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.1)): A non-modal `ChatSidebar` can now be rendered open on the server. It portals after mount into the `CratisComponentsProvider` overlay container, and falls back to `document.body` when there is none. (#339)
- **Components** ([Release v4.15.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.15.1)): The non-modal `ChatSidebar` now plays the same open and close animation as the modal one, and skips it when the user prefers reduced motion. (#339)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.8.0)): `ReadModelScenario` seeds and reads read models through the artifacts provider of its own `Defaults`, instead of always using the process-wide provider.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.8](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.8)): Committing to an aggregate that already had events no longer fails with a concurrency violation when no one else wrote to it. Loading the aggregate left its expected position at zero, so any aggregate whose event source had a later event was rejected on every commit.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.8](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.8)): The expected position is now captured before the aggregate's events are read, so an event appended by someone else while the aggregate is loading is still detected as a conflict at commit instead of being silently overwritten.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.8](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.8)): For aggregates without event handlers, `IsNew` now reflects only the aggregate's own stream: events for the same event source in other streams no longer make it look existing.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.41.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.41.1)): Comments before or on a template's `fits slot` line stay with that directive when printed canonically or moved with `expand-layout`. The typed JSON syntax is unchanged. (#276)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.41.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.41.1)): Comments on contribution navigation lines and on declaration and screen file references also stay with their lines.
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.3)): Published Migrator packages refused to run with a stale-metadata error because their bundled compatibility manifest was still pinned to 4.0.0. Releases now bump every public package to the release version first, regenerate and verify all three compatibility manifests from those versions, and only then publish. A failure in any of those steps stops the release before the first package is published. (#232)
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.3)): The Migrator's version mismatch error now names both the package version and the manifest version, and says that the mismatch is a packaging defect. (#232)
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.3)): The post-publish registry check now also waits for `@cratis/components.conformance`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.31.0 — global command and query filters](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.31.0)): Malformed command input no longer runs context-value providers or key resolvers before it is rejected.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.31.0 — global command and query filters](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.31.0)): The existing per-definition `CommandFilter<T>` and `QueryFilter<T>` callbacks are unchanged.
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.2)): The baseline theme's forced-colors (high-contrast) tokens now win over the dark-scheme tokens in every scheme state: explicit `cratis-dark`/`cratis-light` classes, the OS dark preference, and scoped `.cratis-theme` sections. Before, high-contrast mode kept the dark palette. (#329)
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.2)): `Page` keeps its full-height flex layout when you pass a `className`; the class is added instead of replacing the layout classes. The `panel` prop is documented as what it is: a `panel` class hook on the content region for your own CSS. (#330)
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.2)): `CommandStepper` now calls `onException` and `onUnauthorized` when a command fails, and turns Submit busy before an async `onBeforeExecute` runs, so the command can no longer be submitted twice. `onFailed` now also fires for validation failures, as it already does in `CommandDialog` and `StepperCommandDialog`; if you show a generic error from `onFailed`, it will now also appear for validation failures. (#331)
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.2)): The Components 3 to 4 change-handler codemod no longer produces an `onChange` prop on CommandForm fields that fails type-checking. It leaves those call sites unchanged and tells you to move the handler to the enclosing `CommandForm`'s `onFieldChange`. `onValueChange` handlers are still migrated automatically. (#332)
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.2)): The `onbeforeexecute-must-return` ESLint rule now checks only the components that transform command values (`CommandDialog`, `StepperCommandDialog`, `CommandStepper`, and Arc's `CommandForm`, including aliased and namespace imports), so it no longer reports Arc `CommandScope` callbacks. (#332)
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.2)): Corrected public examples and descriptions that did not match the shipped code: the `FilterPanel` examples now compile, `SchemaEditor`'s `canEdit` and `DataPage`'s `onRefresh` describe their actual behavior, and the confirmation and busy-indicator dialogs point to Arc's `useConfirmationDialog` and `useBusyIndicator`. (#333)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.11](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.11)): The kernel no longer registers its MongoDB camel-case convention pack twice (#3589)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.10](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.10)): A projection declaration containing a block the engine cannot build, such as `variant`, now fails compilation with a compiler error that points at the block. Before this fix, compilation threw an exception. This also applies when compiling without read model or event schemas and to blocks inside `children` and `nested` (#4109)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.9](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.9)): Watching a read model before the kernel has finished registering it now waits for registration instead of throwing `NullReferenceException`. If it is never registered, the watch fails with `ReadModelNotFound` (#3925)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.8](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.8)): The .NET client now loads a passwordless PKCS#12 client certificate, including its private key, for mutual TLS on .NET 9 and later. Before, it read the file as a plain certificate and lost the key (#3680)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.7](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.7)): Webhook deliveries that get a non-success HTTP response (4xx or 5xx) now fail the partition and go through the normal failed-partition retry, instead of counting as delivered (#4200)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.5](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.5)): Append notifications raised by the .NET client now carry each event's subject: an explicit subject or one resolved from a `[Subject]` property. Before, they always reported the event source id. This covers single appends, `AppendMany` and batch appends across event sources (#4133)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.4)): The Chronicle health check now reports the failure status it was registered with (for example `Degraded`) when the kernel is disconnected or unreachable, instead of always reporting `Unhealthy` (#4201)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.3)): The reactor analyzer no longer reports a synchronous `EventsWithConcurrencyScopes` return, which the runtime supports. It also no longer reports unsupported return types as CHR0004, a parameter error (#4206)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.7](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.7)): Generated TypeScript query, observable query and command proxies no longer contain whitespace-only lines, so they pass whitespace lint rules without editing generated files (#2705)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.6](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.6)): GET queries honor paging and sorting parameters regardless of casing, so `sortBy`, `sortDirection`, `page` and `pageSize` sent by the generated TypeScript proxies are applied on both the ASP.NET Core and Arc.Core hosts; ordinary query argument matching is unchanged (#2758)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.2)): Literal set-mappings in projection declarations (`status = "draft"`, `isActive = true`, `version = 1`, and non-negative numeric `add`/`subtract` operands) now project their values. Before this fix they resolved to null. Stored definitions pick up the literal values without migration, but read models built before the fix keep their null values until they are replayed. An empty or otherwise unsupported value expression now fails when the projection is created instead of silently resolving to null (#4124)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.2)): Removing a joined child with `RemovedWithJoin` now removes it from every parent in the in-memory sink and in `ReadModelScenario`, matching MongoDB. Before this fix the parent that received the event kept the child (#4167)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.5](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.5)): ARC0013 no longer reports static members reached through a concept type, such as a `NotSet` sentinel or a static factory, in validator rules; nullable instance dereferences like `RuleFor(c => c.Name.Value)` are still reported (#2658)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.4)): Model-bound queries no longer risk binding a dependency to the wrong parameter under concurrent first use. Parameters are classified once when the query is discovered and bound by position, instead of by comparing reflection metadata instances across calls (#2765)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): A value indented under a clock trigger (`every …`, `at …`) reports `PLAY0450`, because clock triggers take no values. (#258)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.39.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.39.0)): A line indented under a `reads` line reports `PLAY0451`. It no longer becomes a property of the command or a value of the reaction trigger. (#259)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.38.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.38.0)): The documentation now describes what the executable model binds: one `then error` per specification, `produces … for` only on the command's identifier, and `all` projections keyed per event source instead of global counters. The shapes it rejects now get clearer diagnostics. (#261)
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.1)): `CommandDialog` now applies `subtitle`, `placement`, `closeIcon`, and every other `Dialog` prop it accepts. Before, these type-checked but were passed to the command form instead of the dialog, so they had no effect. A type-level check now fails the build if a `Dialog` prop is added without being forwarded.
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.22.4](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.22.4)): An expired session no longer strands a single-page app mid-use. Every unauthenticated request used to get redirected to the sign-in page, even a frontend's own `fetch()`/XHR call - the browser followed that redirect silently and handed the caller a plain `200` login page instead of a clear refusal, so a command kept failing without explanation and an identity check could crash trying to parse the page as JSON. A caller that is not navigating a browser now gets a `401` it can act on; an actual page load still redirects to sign-in as before.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.3)): A frontend using `useIdentity`/`IdentityProvider` no longer gets stuck showing "not signed in" while the tab stays open. When a session that was active ends server-side (expiry, revoked access) an identity refresh now reloads the page instead of quietly settling into an unset identity nothing on the page could act on - the reload sends the browser through the ordinary sign-in redirect (see Cratis/AuthProxy#136) instead of leaving every command failing silently against a session that is already gone.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.37.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.37.1)): Canonical printing and `expand-layout` keep comments inside `form` blocks, including `@input` notes and comments on `populate`, fields, `on submit` and inline `on` behaviors, in order and without duplicates. (#267)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.37.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.37.1)): Comments on navigation lines inside screen actions and tables stay with their lines when printed canonically.
- **cli** ([Release v3.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.20.0)): `cratis render` explains why a model whose events declare several generations cannot be rendered yet: alongside Stage's `STAGE-ESM-016`, it reports `CLI-RENDER-003` naming the evolved event. The command fails with a validation error and writes nothing. Screenplay itself validates such models; Stage does not yet accept their executable model (ESM v4).
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.1)): CHR0037 no longer reports valid migration chains where two or more generations written with `EventTypeGenerationFor` belong to the same event type. It still reports generations that resolve to different event types (#4207)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.11.0)): A completion wait no longer leaves its timeout timer queued after it finishes or is canceled.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.11.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.11.0)): A stale comment was removed and a sample comment uses American spelling.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.18.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.18.1)): Stage's semantic runtime now has Chronicle reject its append atomically when another writer appended to the event log after Stage captured the tail, including the first append to an empty log. Before, the append succeeded and the divergence was only detected on the next command. The runtime faults with "the event-log tail changed outside this session", and nothing is appended.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.18.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.18.1)): An accepted command that appends no facts no longer reports a world that changed during its checks as current.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.18.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.18.1)): Custom `IAppendSemanticFacts` implementations keep working unchanged; the built-in appender also implements the new `IAppendSemanticFactsAtTail`, which the runtime uses for the atomic check. Appenders without it keep the previous, non-atomic check.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.3 — proxies for commands that can reject](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.3)): Generating proxies no longer fails with "Multiple unhandled command response types" for commands that return events or a visible value alongside `rejected(...)` or `denied(...)`. This covers async, union and tuple returns. The rejection branches are not client response values; the client sees them as validation or authorization results, as at runtime.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.3 — proxies for commands that can reject](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.3)): Generated metadata for a command that returns either a rejection or an array of events now accepts the successful array.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.3 — proxies for commands that can reject](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.3)): Proxy generation rejects arrays of `Outcome` values with a clear error, because the runtime does not unwrap them.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.3 — proxies for commands that can reject](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.3)): The aggregate and Chronicle testing docs use the normal generate-and-discover path.
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.2)): `cratis --Database PostgreSQL`, `MsSql`, or `SQLite`: the sample listing now shows registered items. Previously the query failed because the client still sent read models to a MongoDB sink the kernel did not have, and the app connected to a database and user that did not exist. The generated `appsettings.json` now sets `Cratis:Chronicle:DefaultSinkTypeId` to `SQL` and points `ConnectionStrings:Cratis` at `chronicle+<event store>`, the database Chronicle projects into
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.2)): `cratis --Database SQLite` now keeps Chronicle's data in a `chronicle-data` folder in the project, so the backend can read the read models; it is listed in `.gitignore`
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.2)): `cratis-aspire` with the default MongoDB database no longer stops at startup, and the backend now connects to the Chronicle container Aspire starts instead of `localhost:35000`
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.2)): `cratis-aspire` with PostgreSQL, MsSql, or SQLite now starts Chronicle with the development client credentials the backend uses, waits for the database before starting Chronicle, and gives the backend its read-model database
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.2)): `cratis-aspire` SQL variants no longer add `Cratis.Arc.MongoDB`, and pin `Cratis.Arc.EntityFrameworkCore` to the current release like the other Cratis packages
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.2)): The sample `Listing` read model now records the event source id it is keyed by, which the listing page uses as its row key
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.2)): Every template now produces a `.gitignore`; NuGet had been dropping it from the packages
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.2)): `yarn build` no longer fails with Yarn 2 and later: the frontend templates include a `.yarnrc.yml` that sets `nodeLinker: node-modules`
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.10.0)): `@setValue(Event, value)`, `.set(...).toValue(value)`, and constant keys now write their value to the read model. They previously sent a form the kernel did not apply, so the property kept its old value.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.10.0)): Read models with a `@nested` property, or a declarative `.nested(...)`, now register. The kernel previously failed registration with a `NullReferenceException`.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.10.0)): Read-model queries (`findInstanceById`, `getInstances`, snapshots, watches, and materialized reads) now return every property the model declares. Before, they only returned properties declared with `@field`. Nested plain classes, `ConceptAs` values, dates, and booleans are restored as their declared types.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.10.0)): With legacy decorators, an event whose properties are constructor parameters without default values, such as `constructor(readonly name: string)`, now registers with its property types. Before, it regist
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.2 — Chronicle live lists and late registration](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.2)): Live Chronicle read-model lists no longer contain an empty entry when observation starts. Arc ignores the kernel's subscription acknowledgment, which arrives as a change with no key. `@cratis/chronicle` 6.9.1 also filters it.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.2 — Chronicle live lists and late registration](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.2)): Calling `withChronicle(...)` after `discover(...)` or `add(...)` now works. Before, command and query routes answered 500. An artifact observer added to the application builder now also receives the artifacts registered before it, in registration order.
- **AI** ([Release v7.0.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.0.1)): The Decision Engine no longer restart-loops when many decisions arrive at once. Its liveness and readiness probes queued behind waiting decisions, missed their deadline, and got a busy engine killed as if it had died - so every caller fell back to its default for as long as the backlog lasted. The probes and `/metrics` now answer immediately however many decisions are waiting.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.5.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.5.1)): **Projections map multi-word event fields.** Events are stored as camelCase JSON, but projection expressions sent atoms such as `:owner_name` as-is, so those fields were never set, with no error. Atom field references and key expressions are now sent as camelCase; a join's `on:` still names the read model property. Multi-word fields that the event and read model share are mapped automatically, following the kernel's own AutoMap rules: not for a `from` that only aggregates, never over an explicit mapping or a `no_auto_map` field, and never from an event field a join's explicit mapping already reads.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.5.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.5.1)): **Boolean and float constants in projections work.** `set: [on_loan: true]` was sent as a field name and never applied. It is now sent as a constant, like integers already were.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.5.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.5.1)): **Reducers register.** Every registration attempt failed with an `UndefinedFunctionError` for `Cratis.Chronicle.Contracts.Observation.Reducers.SinkDefinition`, so no reducer ever ran and its read model read as `{:ok, nil}`.
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.5.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.5.1)): **Constraints with `remove_constraint` register.** The releasing event type was sent in a form the contract can't encode. Because constraints register before read models and projections, one such constraint stopped the whole client fro
- **Stage** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): A model that declares event generations (Screenplay 4.34) now fails with `STAGE-EVENT-001`, naming the event, on every path that reads events from syntax, instead of rendering one generation's shape as if it were the current event. (#160)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.9.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.9.1)): `watch()` skips the kernel's subscription acknowledgment, as the .NET client does. Before, every new watch began with an empty changeset that had no key and no read model. Consumers that built lists from the changesets got an empty entry. Normal changes and removals are unaffected.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.2)): `CommandScenario<TCommand>` no longer replaces Arc's process-wide service provider. A host that runs in-process specs keeps resolving services from its own provider after a scenario is disposed, and overlapping scenarios no longer interfere with each other (#2763)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.0)): Jobs could not be written on PostgreSQL at all: the state column is `jsonb`, which refuses an implicit cast from `text`. Starting a job still reported success because the failure was inside the grain's write, so the kernel believed it had started work that never existed - a failed partition was never retried, and a redaction's replay never ran (fixed in `Cratis.Orleans` 1.10.1)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.0)): The SQL job storage shipped a context it could not create tables for, and the MongoDB one never registered the conventions its own queries assumed, so writes and reads disagreed on casing (fixed in `Cratis.Orleans` 1.6.1)
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): The generated READMEs gave the wrong frontend port: the Vite dev server runs on `http://localhost:9000`, not 5173, and `run.sh` in the Kotlin and Java templates now prints the right port
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): The documentation pointed to `dotnet new cratis-kotlin` and `dotnet new cratis-java`, which do not exist. The command is `dotnet new cratis --language Kotlin` or `--language Java`, and `Cratis.Templates` already includes both
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): The documentation stated fixed versions for the Kotlin and Java templates (Arc 7.2.0 with Chronicle 4.0.0), but those templates resolve the latest Cratis release when they build
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): `cratis new` examples now include the required `--language` option
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): The TypeScript command example in the web template documentation now compiles against the generated proxy
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): The Aspire template README gave a fixed dashboard address; Aspire prints a dashboard link with a login token and a different port each run
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): Links in the generated READMEs that pointed to retired `cratis.io/docs/...` pages now point to the current Arc, Chronicle, and template documentation
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): The AI setup table listed the wrong profile for the Java template; it uses `cratis/application/java`
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): The generated READMEs and documentation now describe known problems in the templates:
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): With the Yarn 2+ default Plug'n'Play mode, `yarn build` fails. Add `nodeLinker: node-modules` to fix it
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): The default MongoDB variant of `cratis-aspire` stops at startup
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): Read-model queries fail in the PostgreSQL variant of `cratis`
- **Templates** ([Release v1.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Templates/releases/tag/v1.7.1)): The `cratis-aspire` SQL variants keep `Cratis.Arc.EntityFrameworkCore` unpinned
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.30.1 — identity, tenancy and authorization paired with .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.30.1)): The client manifest now lists roles from nested authorization requirements, such as stacked role declarations. Enforcement was already correct.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.8.0)): Two `@constraint` classes with the same id now fail at registration. Before, the last one silently replaced the first.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.8.0)): Merging same-named constraints with conflicting scopes or ignore-casing settings now fails with a clear error.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.8.0)): The first message supplied among same-named unique event type declarations is kept.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.8.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.8.0)): A `$` in a violation value is inserted literally into the message.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.1)): An `EventForEventSourceId` returned from a command now keeps its `Occurred` and `Tags` when appended, for a single wrapper, a collection or mixed collection of wrappers, and `EventsWithConcurrencyScopes`, on both the transactional and the immediate append path. Previously both values were silently dropped, so imported or backfilled events were recorded with the append time and without their per-event tags (#2570)
- **cli** ([Release v3.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.19.0)): `clear-quarantine` failed against every kernel it was ever pointed at: it resolved its contract method by reflection, which cannot see explicitly implemented interface members, and the generated client implements the contract explicitly. It now calls the contract method directly (#185)
- **cli** ([Release v3.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.19.0)): `retry-partition` reported that a retry had started regardless of what the kernel actually did. It now reads the outcome the kernel reports and exits non-zero when the observer or the partition is quarantined, or the partition was not among the observer's failures, instead of leaving the impression that recovery was under way (#186)
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.29.0 — query paging, sorting and QUERY bodies like .NET](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.29.0)): An unpaged request to a MongoDB or Drizzle query that needs paging no longer fails with a 500. It returns the complete result, up to the provider's maximum page size. Arc on .NET returns every row; this bound is a documented deliberate difference.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.6.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.6.1)): Restored the Chronicle release pipeline: adding `IObservers Observers` to `IEventStore` as a required member broke the actual publish, because an existing external implementation of `IEventStore` (Arc's `CommandScenario` test harness) was compiled against the interface before this member existed, and does not have it. A type that implements an interface has to implement all of it, or the runtime refuses to load it before any of its own code runs — which is exactly what happened when the release pipeline tried to start Chronicle 19.5.0 beside the currently published Arc packages.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.6.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.6.1)): `Observers` is now an optional interface member with a default implementation that throws `ObserversNotSupported`, the same shape `IPatterns Patterns`/`PatternsNotSupported` already use on this interface for the identical reason. Every real implementation continues to provide it explicitly and is unaffected.
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.2](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.2)): The Decision Engine no longer answers the first decision after an idle spell in seconds instead of milliseconds. Model weights are now held in process memory instead of memory-mapped page cache the node could reclaim, and a warm-up decision is scored before `/readyz` reports ready, so a ready pod stays at full speed for as long as it runs.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.6.0)): CHR0024 no longer reports a `[Nested]` projection object as having no mapping source. The attribute was missing from the analyzer's set of recognized mapping attributes, so every nested object was reported as one that would never be populated (#4175)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.6.0)): CHR0024 no longer reports the properties of a `[ChildrenFrom<TEvent>]` child record as unmapped. The events that populate a child are named by its parent, but the child was judged against only its own attributes and so against an empty set of subscribed events (#4176)
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.1)): The provider `Configuring` namespace is now reachable from the package root as `import { Configuring } from '@cratis/ai'`. It was left out of the barrel because its command names collide with those in `Adding`, `Reconfiguring`, `Removing` and `Renaming`, with a comment directing consumers to import it by path — but the package's `exports` map exposes only the root entry, so no deep path resolves and the namespace could not be reached at all. It goes out under its own namespace, which carries it without reintroducing the ambiguity
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.7.1)): An inferred variant read model now uses the model's own members when its shared-handler mappings are validated.
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): Removed the advice that `reads` plus `concurrency` protects a decision. The executable model rejects both (`PLAY0271`).
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): Removed the projection-level `key` from the examples. It routes nothing (`PLAY0381`).
- **AI** ([Release v6.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.3.0)): Binding no longer implies the reference runner will run a model. The skills list the projection constructs the reference execution plan refuses: a projection-level `remove via join`, `all` beside removals, `children` or `nested`, `join`/`children`/`remove via join` inside `nested`, and event-context values other than the event source identity. One of these stops every specification in the
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.26.1 — standalone host peer addresses](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.26.1)): The standalone host (`app.run()`, `runArc` and `createArcNodeHandler`) now passes each request's socket peer address to Arc. Before, two things went wrong for anonymous callers:
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.26.1 — standalone host peer addresses](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.26.1)): All of them in a tenant shared one per-caller connection budget.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.26.1 — standalone host peer addresses](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.26.1)): SSE hub control requests were not tied to the address that opened the stream.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): `find-assertion-gaps` no longer reports a slice whose only assertion is `then denied` as unasserted.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): References and dependencies to an appended event are labelled `whenAppendedEvent` instead of `thenEvent`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.32.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.32.0)): The server's initialize instructions say identities persist when a proposal is applied; `export-workspace` is optional, for transfer or backup.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.16.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.16.2)): Stage's in-memory runtime and specification executor report an opaque policy they reach as unsupported instead of allowing or denying it, following authored order: a portable operand that decides first still decides (Screenplay decision 0005).
- **Stage** ([Release v4.16.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.16.2)): An unsupported authorization result is no longer replaced by a contract rejection when the command payload fails to bind.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.25.0 — kernel-backed Chronicle tests and read consistency](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.25.0)): A `@subject()` field or a `getEventSourceId()` result holding a `Guid` or a concept is converted to its string form instead of being ignored or failing the command.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.25.0 — kernel-backed Chronicle tests and read consistency](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.25.0)): The causation chain records concept, `Guid`, `Date`, `DateOnly`, `TimeOnly` and `TimeSpan` command fields, not only strings, numbers and booleans.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.24.1 — anonymous live queries over SSE](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.24.1)): Live queries work for anonymous callers over the SSE hub, the transport the `@cratis/arc.react` `<Arc>` provider uses by default, as they do with Arc on .NET. Previously the SSE hub accepted only authenticated callers, so observable queries in anonymous applications stayed empty unless the client switched to WebSockets.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.24.0 — Core namespaces and clean builds](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.24.0)): A clean build of the repository no longer fails on the Chronicle testing scenario's builder typing.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.24.0 — Core namespaces and clean builds](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.24.0)): The repository builds and passes its full gate from a fresh checkout on Linux and Node.js 22, and pull requests run it in hosted CI.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.21.1 — proxy response types](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.21.1)): Generated command proxies no longer declare Chronicle events, event wrappers, aggregate commit results or command operations returned from `handle()` as the command response. The response is the one value the server sends back, or `void` when every returned value is handled on the server, as with Arc on .NET.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.18.1 — package contents](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.18.1)): `@cratis/arc.core`'s type declarations no longer require RxJS, which is an optional peer dependency.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.18.1 — package contents](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.18.1)): Published packages no longer include test helpers, specifications or build metadata.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.18.1 — package contents](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.18.1)): `@cratis/arc.chronicle/testing` declares the Chronicle SDK version range it actually requires.
- **AI** ([Release v6.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.2.0)): Twenty-eight generated proxy folders were unreachable from `@cratis/ai`. Agents, skills, provider pools, model discovery, tier mapping, Codex, Copilot, rate limiting and usage snapshots all generate proxies, but the package root's hand-maintained barrel had no export line for them, so importing any of them failed as a missing export rather than as a build error.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.6.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.6.1)): The new `Orleans.Storage.Sql.Specs` project was multi-targeted along with everything else, but the provider it specifies is `net10.0` only because EF Core 10 is. Restore failed with `NU1201` on a project reference that cannot resolve for `net8.0`/`net9.0`. It now carries the same `IsPackaging` condition the provider itself uses.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.6.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.6.1)): `ConventionPacks` used `System.Threading.Lock`, which does not exist on `net8.0`. Now a plain `object`, with the reason recorded next to it.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.6.0)): SQL jobs storage now creates its own schema. The provider shipped a `JobsDbContext` with no migrations, and nothing else in the package created the tables, so provisioning silently fell to whoever consumed it - and a consumer that did not know to do it got a database where every job read and write failed with `relation "Jobs" does not exist`. A `v1_0_0` migration creates `Jobs` and `JobSteps`, and `SqlJobsStorage.GetFor` applies it before handing back storage. The column helpers come from `Cratis.Arc.EntityFrameworkCore`, so one migration is valid across SQL Server, PostgreSQL and SQLite.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.6.0)): MongoDB jobs storage now registers its own convention pack. It declared one by implementing `ICanProvideMongoDBConventionPacks`, but nothing ever called `Provide()`, so state serialized with Pascal-case element names while every filter was written against camel case - including the composite `_id`. No filter matched: reads returned empty and upserts were rejected for altering an immutable `_id`. A failed job step was written and then could not be found, which is indistinguishable from a job that never had steps.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.6.0)): Job and job step state are read back as their base types, but what is written is the consumer's own subclass. The first extra property made the read throw, so a job authored the way the documentation shows could never have its steps read. `IgnoreExtraElementsConvention` added to the same pack.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.5.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.5.1)): `readModels.findInstanceById` returns `null` when the Kernel answers a missing read model with a JSON `null` document, instead of throwing.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): A command or query with two or more `authorize` lines kept only the last one, so every earlier gate was silently dropped. The lines now combine with AND in authored order, as module and feature gates already did and as the documentation states. Printing writes them as one `authorize A and B` line. This makes such commands and queries stricter.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): A policy with two `require` lines silently kept only the last condition. This is now PLAY0441.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.29.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.29.0)): A policy combining `require` with code bound only the code and dropped the condition once policies with code were admitted; this is now PLAY0440 at parse time.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.8.0 — MongoDB integration](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.8.0)): `MongoReadModels.queryPage` applies Arc's requested sort before the application's own sort, so the order it reports is the order it returns.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.6.0 — proxies from TypeScript source](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.6.0)): Documentation no longer states that model-bound commands and queries cannot be turned into client proxies.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): `appendMany` sends every supplied concurrency scope label, not only the scopes derived from the events' event source ids.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): An event store now uses the `clientArtifactsProvider` configured in `ChronicleOptions` instead of always using the default artifact discovery.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): Causation links added while appending no longer leak into sibling operations.
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.19.6](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.19.6)): TypeScript projects that use `"moduleResolution": "NodeNext"` (Node.js ESM servers and libraries) can now import `@cratis/fundamentals` and its `geospatial`, `json` and `reflection` subpaths with full type checking. The published declarations use resolvable `.js` import paths, and each entry point resolves the ESM or CommonJS declarations that match the module format being loaded.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.7.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.7.1)): A generated application's event store data now survives `docker compose down` and `up`, container recreation and regenerating the application into the same folder. The generated `docker-compose.yml` keeps the development Chronicle image's MongoDB data in the named volumes `chronicle-data` and `chronicle-config`; before, it used anonymous volumes and a recreated container started from an empty store. Only `docker compose down --volumes` deletes the data. (part of #100)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.7.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.7.0)): The legacy syntax renderer no longer renders `[AllowAnonymous]` for a command or query that inherits `authorize` from its module or feature. It now fails with `AuthorizationCannotBeRendered`.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.27.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.27.0)): An ESM v3 model (released in v4.26.0 for reducers with a body) lost v2 execution semantics: produced facts carried no event-source context, so `then <Event> for …` assertions failed, and a mismatched typed `when … for` was not rejected. v3 models now execute with all v2 semantics, as documented.
- **Components** ([Release v4.14.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.14.0)): The column filter trigger rendered `U+2315 TELEPHONE RECORDER` in a `span` at the ambient font size, so it showed as a tiny mark that does not read as a filter. It is now a funnel SVG sized against the header row.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.1 — observable hosting fixes](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.1)): Closing an SSE hub connection while a subscription is still opening no longer crashes the process with an unhandled rejection after the shutdown timeout.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.1 — observable hosting fixes](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.1)): `mountFastifyWebSockets` no longer hides boot errors from plugins registered before it, so a Fastify app whose authentication plugin fails to load no longer starts serving. The mount now also works whether `@fastify/websocket` is registered before or after it.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.1 — observable hosting fixes](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.1)): The standalone host (`runArc`) now applies its `native` callback to WebSocket upgrades too, so host-authenticated principals, the request authority and the remote address are the same over WebSocket as over HTTP.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.1 — observable hosting fixes](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.1)): `runArc`'s `close()` stops accepting connections first and finishes within its timeout, even when a direct WebSocket query never finishes opening.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.1 — observable hosting fixes](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.1)): Oversized inbound WebSocket frames are rejected with close code 1009 even when the app shares its own `@fastify/websocket` or `@hono/node-ws` server with Arc.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.1 — observable hosting fixes](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.1)): Upgrade sockets for paths that belong to another handler are left to that handler.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.8](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.8)): `Cratis.Chronicle` now carries `Cratis.Chronicle.CodeAnalysis` as a package dependency, so a consumer actually receives the CHR analyzers. The analyzer project packed itself while setting `DevelopmentDependency`, which NuGet reads as "never emit me as a dependency" — so the client's nuspec never mentioned it and a consumer had to know the package existed and reference it by hand. The package identity moves into a package-only project the client can depend on, the same shape `Cratis.Arc.Chronicle` already uses for its ARCCHR analyzers.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.5.0)): `IJobsManager.Resume` reports whether the job was actually taken forward. Resuming is allowed to refuse - the job was never prepared, or its owner is no longer interested - and `Resume` computed that refusal, logged it, and returned `void`, so a caller could not tell a refusal from a resume. Nothing takes a refused job: it stays `Stopped`, so it will not run, will not finalize, and will never report back, and a caller that treats that as success waits forever for something that is not coming.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.6.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.6.2)): A command in the sandbox runtime no longer reports success when Chronicle rejects its events. A constraint violation now fails the command with the constraint's name and message as a validation failure. A schema rejection or any other append error fails it with an error. Before, the rejection was only logged, so nothing was appended and read models stayed empty while the command said it had succeeded. (#10)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.6.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.6.2)): `$context.tenant` and `$context.causedBy.subject`, `.name` and `.userName` in produced event mappings now resolve to the current tenant and caller. A produced mapping the runtime cannot resolve now fails the command instead of being dropped from the event. (#10)
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.19.5](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.19.5)): `JsonSerializer.deserialize` now fills arrays declared as `@field(Array, { genericArguments: [ItemType] })`, the form standard-decorator code uses. Items are deserialized as `ItemType`, including concepts and nested models; before, such an array came back empty.
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.19.5](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.19.5)): A concept that declares `static readonly valueType` (for example `Guid`) now receives its value converted to that type, so a `ConceptAs<Guid>` holds a `Guid` instead of a string.
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.19.5](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.19.5)): Falsy payload values (`""`, `0`, `false`) are deserialized through their field type, so a concept field holding an empty string or zero is wrapped in its concept like any other value.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.24.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.24.2)): A specification that uses a read model with no keyed query in `given readmodel` or `then readmodel` no longer reports a second error, "must state its identifier property ''", at every block. The one error where the read model is declared, which says why it cannot be identified, remains.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.24.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.24.1)): An MCP apply that renames a document only by case now succeeds and leaves the file with the new casing on disk. A destination is allowed only when it is the same on-disk entry as the document being moved away. A different file whose name differs only by case is still refused, and a failed case-only rename rolls back to the original name.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): Member-less events and read models work in standard mode.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.4.0)): An explicit `@field(Object)` keeps its object schema, and concepts and Guids are recognized across duplicate `@cratis/fundamentals` copies.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.5.0)): State the payment summary identifier in the Billing sample's payment-list specification. Screenplay now requires it (`PLAY0351`). (#124)
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.4.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.4.2)): `NullJob` collided with Chronicle's `NullJob` the same way `JobsManager` did, so a silo referencing both libraries still could not start
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.3.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.3.2)): Load `@cratis/chronicle` and every exported subpath directly in Node.js ESM without a bundler. Published JavaScript and declaration files now use explicit `.js` file specifiers, and each build verifies the packed package imports natively.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.3.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.3.2)): Preserve constraint violations and errors when a batch append is rejected before sequence numbers are assigned, so a rejected batch no longer reports an empty result set.
- **Orleans** ([Release v1.4.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Orleans/releases/tag/v1.4.1)): A silo referencing both `Cratis.Orleans` and `Cratis.Chronicle` could not start, because both ship a `JobsManager` grain and Orleans gave them the same derived grain type name (#16)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.20.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.20.0)): A preserved comment stays with its own declaration, even when two declarations in the same scope print identical lines (#229)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.7](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.7)): An observer whose stopped catch-up job refused to resume was left believing catch-up was under way — dropping every live event, never advancing, and eventually quarantined for a strand it could not escape
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.0)): Observable streams retain their identity and service lifetime through in-flight emissions, including disconnects and failing cancellation or disposal callbacks.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.0)): Authorization diagnostics now describe contradictory attributes accurately; ARC0021 is limited to authentication schemes without Arc’s ASP.NET Core integration.
- **Arc.Kotlin** ([Release v7.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.Kotlin/releases/tag/v7.6.0)): Corrected the parity reference: Arc .NET rejects contradictory authorization attributes in either evaluator order, and its generated proxies support XML documentation comments.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.2.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.2.0)): Bind Chronicle’s lowercase read-model identity for live by-id queries while preserving modeled `Id` and `ID` fields. Normalize numeric, boolean, and composite identities for consistent readback. (#10)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.1.0)): Reject unsupported ESM kinds and value combinations with a typed diagnostic and no candidate artifacts, instead of emitting placeholder types, default values, or silently omitting slices. (#123)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.1.0)): Preserve Chronicle projection mapping replacement, recursive child mappings, and auto-map inheritance. Legacy rendering now fails when it cannot preserve the modeled projection rather than producing incomplete code. This does not add support for new ESM constructs. (#125)
- **Stage** ([Release v4.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.1.0)): Reject ambiguous runtime projection keys before startup or remote registration, preventing keys from silently resolving to an unrelated value.
- **Stage** ([Release v4.1.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v4.1.0)): Correct the Billing sample’s customer-name and cancellation fields, validate issuance against the invoice’s read state, and replace its empty state reducer with a projection. (#124)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): An `import` of a declaration from the same application no longer hides consistency diagnostics (#208)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.18.0)): A dotted path that continues past a scalar, concept or enum field is reported (`PLAY0282`/`PLAY0287`) instead of compiling silently (#201)
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.3.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.3.1)): Repeated `removedWith()` calls on a unique property constraint now retain every distinct release event, so any declared event can free the value instead of only the last one (#77).
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.3.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.3.1)): Unique event-type release examples now explicitly state that the TypeScript client does not support that workflow, rather than demonstrating a constraint that enforced nothing.
- **AI** ([Release v3.3.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.3.1)): Cratis corpus agents inherit the model selected for the session instead of overriding it with outdated Claude model IDs, in every harness. (#396)
- **AI** ([Release v3.3.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.3.1)): Pi no longer offers two delegation tools when another extension already provides `Agent` (such as pi-subagents, which reads the same `.pi/agents`): the Cratis `subagent` tool stands down for that session and says so once. Without such a tool it works as before.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.6](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.6)): A failed partition is now retried within the retry interval even if the first retry reminder tick is lost. Previously the next attempt could be deferred by the 48-hour reminder period. (#4113)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.6](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.6)): SQL storage (PostgreSQL, SQL Server, SQLite) now loads and refreshes reminders from storage correctly. Range reads follow the Orleans reminder table contract, and reminders stored by earlier versions are rehashed on startup. (#4113)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.6](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.6)): The SQL reminder table now returns the stored ETag when a reminder is registered, and returns nothing instead of failing when a reminder does not exist. (#4113)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.5](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.5)): A projection whose children use a constant parent key now applies every event to them, not just the ones that create them. Previously, when the root carried event types of its own, later events for an existing child were dropped: a fixed-key document counting things kept an accurate root total while updates and removals to its children went nowhere, so a count of what was still open kept counting things that had closed. Creations still landed, so the document looked healthy. (#4105)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.5](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.5)): A projection can now combine a root-level constant key with constant-parent-key children in one read model. It previously failed to resolve the child's parent, forcing the total and the membership set into separate read models. (#4099)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.4)): Seeding a projection-backed `ReadModelScenario` with an initial state containing a nonempty `JsonObject` no longer crashes the test host with a stack overflow (#3993)
- **Chronicle.Mcp** ([Release v1.4.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Mcp/releases/tag/v1.4.0)): Merges to `main` publish again. The publish trigger's path filter matched only a file named `main`, so no labeled merge since the switch to releasing on push (#20) produced a release.
- **AuthProxy** ([Release v2.22.3](https://github.com/Cratis/AuthProxy/releases/tag/v2.22.3)): The 43 `<c>` elements in XML documentation that had no language attribute now declare `language="text"`, matching the rest of the codebase and keeping MA0219 clean.
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.19.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.19.4)): API documentation uses the standard `@public` tag instead of `@export`, and the decorator context types are marked `@inline`.
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.19.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.19.4)): References to `Date` link to its MDN page, so they resolve in the generated API documentation.
- **Fundamentals** ([Release v7.19.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Fundamentals/releases/tag/v7.19.4)): Remove a stale lint suppression and add the blank lines the markdown lint expects.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.21.0)): The authorization attribute evaluator specs could not fail: a null-conditional assertion skipped the assertion whenever the evaluator found nothing
- **AI** ([Release v3.2.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.2.1)): The Decision Engine image did not build at all
