- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.3)): An observer could be left permanently believing it was preparing catch-up — dropping every live event, never advancing, and eventually quarantined — when its catch-up job concluded without having prepared steps of its own
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.3)): a job that was already running,
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.3)): a stopped job that was merely resumed,
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.3)): a job found with every step already completed, which is finalized rather than resumed.
- **cli** ([Release v3.13.2](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.13.2)): The `<READ_MODEL>` argument is described as the read model identifier and names the column to read it from, matching what the commands actually send.
- **cli** ([Release v3.13.2](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.13.2)): No behaviour change: the wire value was always `ReadModelIdentifier`. This aligns the `[Description]`, the `[LlmOption]` metadata and the settings doc comment with it.
- **cli** ([Release v3.13.2](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.13.2)): The server side of the same mistake is fixed separately in Cratis/Chronicle `fix/read-model-lookup-miss-guard`, which turns the resulting `NullReferenceException` into a meaningful error. The two are independent; either can merge first.
- **cli** ([Release v3.13.2](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.13.2)): `GetReadModelInstancesCommand` catches an RPC error whose detail contains `"NullReferenceException"` and reports it as "read model is client-owned". That was papering over the server defect above and is the wrong explanation for an unknown identifier — worth re-scoping once the Chronicle fix ships. Left untouched here.
- **cli** ([Release v3.13.2](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.13.2)): `read-models occurrences` uses a different argument name (`<READ_MODEL_TYPE>`) and may be worth a consistency pass.
- **cli** ([Release v3.13.2](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.13.2)): New spec asserts the description says identifier, does not say container name, and names the `Identifier` column that `ListReadModelsCommand` prints — so help text and wire payload cannot drift apart again.
- **cli** ([Release v3.13.2](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.13.2)): `Cli.Specs`: 1,111 tests passing.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.19.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.19.2)): A read model injected into a command's `Handle()`, `Provide()`, or a `CommandValidator<T>` is now released (its `[PII]`/`[Encrypted]` properties decrypted) regardless of whether the *command* declares a `Subject`/`[Subject]`/`ICanProvideSubject` — most commands rely on Chronicle's implicit event-source-id subject fallback instead, which was never checked, so release was silently skipped for the common case independent of what the injected read model actually carried
- **Arc** ([Release v22.19.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.19.2)): A namespace- or global-scoped `[Encrypted]` value (Cratis/Chronicle#4095) now reaches release correctly even on a read model that has no `Id`/`[Subject]` at all — the ordinary shape for that kind of value, since its key does not depend on a subject
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.2)): `IReadModels.Release` no longer skips a namespace- or global-scoped `[Encrypted]` value on a read model that has no resolvable subject (no `[Subject]` property and no `Id` property) — that scope's key does not depend on a subject at all, and a read model carrying only that kind of value ordinarily has no subject-identifying property in the first place (Cratis/Chronicle#4095)
- **Chronicle.Elixir** ([Release v3.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.Elixir/releases/tag/v3.5.0)): `Chronicle.Connections.AppendCompatibility`'s announced protocol version was still pinned to `19.0.1` while `mix.lock` had already moved to `19.3.2`
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.4.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.4.1)): A kernel with a large artifact set could no longer start. Registering projections awaited a reconciliation pass whose cost scales with projections × namespaces, inside the caller's response timeout, so every registration timed out and the silo never became ready. Registration now schedules that pass instead of awaiting it. (#4088)
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.1)): New documentation pages are again added to the product's `toc.yml`, so they no longer silently drop out of the sidebar.
- **AI** ([Release v3.1.1](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.1.1)): Writing guidance no longer asks authors to hand-maintain page edit links that the documentation site now generates.
- **Stage** ([Release v3.22.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v3.22.0)): Include the component stylesheet and its font assets in generated frontend bundles. (#100)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): Diagnose invalid validation targets, incompatible read keys, unpopulated child fields, inconsistent specification outcomes, undeclared enum values, and unknown event fields. (#201)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): Preserve module forms and module/feature contributions exactly once when expanding and merging model folders. (#202)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): Report unsupported query-key combinations as diagnostics instead of throwing, and reject executable-only no-op transactions on non-executable workspaces.
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.3.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.3.2)): `Cratis:Chronicle:Clustering:ResponseTimeout` now governs the call it was added for. A grain service is a system target, and Orleans times system target calls out against `SystemResponseTimeout` rather than `ResponseTimeout`, so registering projections from the startup task stayed on the 30 second default while the setting appeared to have been applied. (#4088)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.19.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.19.1)): Prevent CommandForm from executing while custom field validation errors are present, including immediate and imperative submissions. (#2723)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.19.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.19.1)): Keep validation messages and displayed submission results consistent when custom errors change or clear. (#2723)
- **AI** ([Release v2.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v2.31.0)): Resolve conflicting tutorial and snippet guidance and remove handoffs to nonexistent documentation skills.
- **AI** ([Release v2.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v2.31.0)): Clarify release-intent authority and the limits of static skill verification.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.19.0)): `sortDirection=descending` sorted ascending. `asc`, `ascending`, `desc` and `descending` are all accepted case-insensitively now, and any other value is rejected as `malformedRequest` with 400 instead of being answered in the opposite order to the one asked for (#2715)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.3.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.3.1)): `Cratis:Chronicle:Clustering:ResponseTimeout` now also applies to the calls it was added for. A silo hosts a client of its own, and the startup task reaches its grain services through that hosted client, so those calls take the client's timeout rather than the silo's — which left the startup path that needs the larger budget still running on Orleans' 30 second default and made the setting look like it did nothing. (#4088)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.2.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.2.2)): A Chronicle kernel could reach a state where it was unable to start at all. Registering the persisted projection definitions was the one startup step that did not retry, and it is the step likeliest to exceed the response timeout, because registering fans a subscribe out across every observer in the store rather than doing a single grain's work — so the time it needs grows with the store. Once a store grew past that point the timeout terminated the host, the next attempt hit the same wall, and the kernel stayed down until someone intervened. (#4088)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.2.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.2.2)): The `Week` calendar accessor is now an extension method. It shipped as an extension property, which cannot be used for the one thing it exists for — the language does not permit an extension property inside an expression tree, and these accessors are only ever written inside one. A path built with it is now also rendered as the bare name rather than with a trailing `()`, matching the name the registry is keyed by; the parenthesized form is still accepted when parsing.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.18.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.18.0)): `AuthenticationMiddleware` now sets the matched endpoint's metadata on the request context before any `IAuthenticationHandler` runs, so a handler can check `context.AllowsAnonymous()` and return `AuthenticationResult.Anonymous` instead of rejecting - and logging a rejection for - a request the endpoint never required a credential for, such as a Kubernetes health probe (#2706)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.1.8](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.1.8)): Reading a collection of read models no longer regenerates the same JSON schema once per row. Generating a schema is a function of the CLR type alone, so it is now generated once per type and shared. The compliance release pass asked for it per instance, which made serving a collection of read models — every emission of an observable query, for every subscriber — spend time proportional to the number of rows on work that produces an identical answer each time. Measured at 328 ms for 916 rows of a moderately sized read model, now effectively zero.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.17.0)): An observable collection query backed by MongoDB no longer costs time proportional to the size of the collection every time a single document changes. The change stream already names the changed document, and the observable already decides what that meant for the observed set; that verdict is now carried on the emission instead of being discarded and then rediscovered by serializing every item of the new snapshot and the previous one.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.17.0)): The comparison fallback no longer serializes every item twice on every emission. Unchanged items are the very same instances the previous snapshot held, so reference equality settles most comparisons and record value equality settles the rest; serializing is now the last resort for an item type that overrides neither.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.17.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.17.0)): Removing an item from a **paged** observable query now reports the document pulled onto the page to replace it, so a client applying the delta no longer ends up one row short.
- **Stage** ([Release v3.21.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v3.21.2)): Synchronize the Stage Host kernel with updated Chronicle clients, verify the exact image exists, and propose the validated package and runtime changes together for review instead of pushing directly to main. (#107)
- **Scene** ([Release v3.6.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Scene/releases/tag/v3.6.0)): Cancel pending lookups and hide obsolete results when query inputs change. (#39)
- **cli** ([Release v3.10.0](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.10.0)): Compile a folder of `.play` files as one application so declarations resolve across files and diagnostics retain their source paths. (#168)
- **Components** ([Release v4.11.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.11.2)): `ColumnFilterMenu` now returns focus to its own trigger button when the filter popover closes having dropped focus, for both Escape and outside-click dismissal, instead of depending on React Aria's timing-sensitive automatic restore (#241)
- **Components** ([Release v4.11.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.11.2)): Dismissing a filter menu by moving focus elsewhere — for example pressing another column's filter trigger — leaves focus where the user put it, rather than pulling it back to the trigger whose menu closed (#241)
- **Components** ([Release v4.11.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.11.1)): A filtered `Dropdown` now commits the option ArrowDown or ArrowUp made active: Enter reports it once, closes the list, and shows its label. Escape still dismisses without changing the selection (#239)
- **Components** ([Release v4.11.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.11.1)): A `Dropdown` selection now closes its own overlay and settles its own filter text, instead of waiting for a `value` that may never come back or may resolve to none of the options (#239)
- **Components** ([Release v4.11.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.11.1)): An unfiltered single-select `Dropdown` now shows the label of the option that was chosen, rather than keeping its placeholder, when the application does not bind the value back (#239)
- **Components** ([Release v4.10.4](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.10.4)): `ComboBox` now offers every option when the list is opened by the trigger, by ArrowDown or on focus, instead of only the already-selected one; typing still narrows the list, and reopening after a selection shows everything again (#297)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.1.7](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.1.7)): Subscribing an observer no longer waits for every failed partition to be handed to the jobs manager for recovery. An observer with hundreds of failed partitions previously exceeded the 30 second grain-call budget inside `Subscribe`, so the client timed out and the observer stayed unsubscribed - after a kernel restart this left reactors quarantined and projections disconnected until the next restart. Recovery now runs in a turn of the observer's own once the subscription is established.
- **Components** ([Release v4.10.3](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.10.3)): A disabled tag group now refuses to remove a tag with Delete or Backspace, matching the mouse behavior (#305)
- **Components** ([Release v4.10.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.10.2)): The gap between tags in a `TagGroup` applies. (#293)
- **Components** ([Release v4.10.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.10.1)): A single-selection `ToggleGroup` is one tab stop. The selected option carries it — or the first enabled option when the value matches none — and it follows the selection as it changes. Arrow-key movement and `Space`/`Enter` committing are unchanged. (#292)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.1.6](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.1.6)): Projection subscribers now discard their silo-local cached pipeline when a projection definition changes, so events of a newly consumed event type are handled with the updated key resolvers instead of failing with `Missing key resolver` until the kernel restarts.
- **Stage** ([Release v3.21.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v3.21.1)): The binding module imports each generated proxy from the slice folder that declares it, instead of from the project root where no proxy is written. Every import previously pointed at a file that does not exist. (#100)
- **Stage** ([Release v3.21.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v3.21.1)): The generated frontend declares the types its own bundler configuration needs, so it type-checks and its build script runs. (#100)
- **Stage** ([Release v3.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v3.21.0)): A generated application renders the screen it composed, instead of mounting an empty shell around a placeholder. (#100)
- **Stage** ([Release v3.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Stage/releases/tag/v3.21.0)): The composed scene is written with the member names the Scene model declares, so it can be read at all. Serialized with .NET member names it parsed successfully and matched nothing, which would have rendered every screen empty without reporting an error.

## Removed

- **AI** ([Release v7.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.0.0)): `AddDecisionEngineProvider` and `ReconfigureDecisionEngineProvider`, and decision engines as a kind of configured AI provider. Choose the engine with the new commands instead
- **AI** ([Release v7.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v7.0.0)): `IDecisionProviderResolver` and `IDecisionProviderClient`, replaced by `IDecisionEngineResolver` and `IDecisionEngineClient`
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.24.0 — Core namespaces and clean builds](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.24.0)): The `validateOnly` flag on `executeCommand` and `execute`; use `validateCommand` and `validate`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.24.0 — Core namespaces and clean builds](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.24.0)): Internal result helpers (`malformed`, `status`, `emptyPaging`, `commandResult`) from the root export; `recordFailure` moved to `@cratis/arc.core/hosting`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.22.0 — grouped options](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.22.0)): `mountExpress`, `mountFastify`, `mountHono` and the `mount*WebSockets` functions; use `cratisArc`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.22.0 — grouped options](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.22.0)): `addChronicle`, `addMongoDB`, `addDrizzle` and the free `addCratis` function; use `withChronicle`, `withMongoDB`, `withDrizzle` and `builder.addCratis()`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.22.0 — grouped options](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.22.0)): The flat `prefix`, `segmentsToSkip`, `include*NameInRoute`, `correlationHeader`, `tenantHeader`, `resolveTenant`, `enableQueryMethod` and `maxBodyBytes` options and `identityDetailsSchema`; use the grouped options above.
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): `Cratis.AI.Providers.Resolving`, whose `ConfiguredAIProvider` collided by name with the one in `Cratis.AI.Providers`.
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): The duplicate `AIProviderPoolMember` in `Cratis.AI.Providers.Pools`; the one in `Pools.Listing` is the single definition.
- **AI** ([Release v6.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v6.0.0)): `Cratis.AI.Agents.Identity` is now `Cratis.AI.Agents.Naming` - a namespace called `Identity` shadowed Chronicle's own `Identity` type for every consumer.
- **AI** ([Release v5.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v5.0.0)): `TierModelDefaults`, the built-in table of per-vendor model names. It had already drifted from what the vendors serve - it named models that had been renamed, and one that never existed - and a tier pointing at a name the vendor does not serve fails silently, landing on whatever the harness falls back to.
- **AI** ([Release v4.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v4.0.0)): `ISecretProtector`, `ISecretRevealer` and `CratisAIBuilder.WithSecretProtection<T>()`.

## Security

- **Arc** ([Release v22.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.31.0)): For commands and queries protected by a policy or authentication scheme, Arc checks the caller and the authorization requirements again immediately before the handler, query method or controller action runs, and denies the request if either changed after the policy verdict. (#2761)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.31.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.31.0)): Missing or conflicting authorization configuration now denies the request with Unauthorized (403 at the HTTP endpoints) instead of running it or returning a server error, including when a custom command or query pipeline is registered. (#2761)
- **Chronicle** ([Release v19.7.6](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle/releases/tag/v19.7.6)): The kernel development Compose file (`Source/Kernel/Server/docker-compose.yml`) now publishes MongoDB, PostgreSQL, SQL Server and the Aspire dashboard on `127.0.0.1` only, instead of on all network interfaces. These services use well-known development credentials (#4208)
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.24.1 — anonymous live queries over SSE](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.24.1)): SSE hub control requests are accepted only from the caller that opened the connection: the same identity for authenticated callers, and additionally the same peer address for anonymous callers when the host provides one. Control requests must be JSON and pass the allowed-origins check, and each subscription still runs the query's own authorization.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.11.0 — authorization policies and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.11.0)): A principal's authentication scheme is always set by Arc from the handler that authenticated it, never taken from the handler's own result.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.11.0 — authorization policies and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.11.0)): Observable queries restricted to specific authentication schemes are rejected at startup, because the shared WebSocket and SSE connections cannot honor per-query schemes.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.11.0 — authorization policies and identity](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.11.0)): Empty `x-ms-client-principal-id` headers and oversized principal headers are rejected.
- **Chronicle.TypeScript** ([Release v6.5.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Chronicle.TypeScript/releases/tag/v6.5.0)): Reading a read model that holds personal data now fails when the data cannot be released, instead of returning the unreleased values.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): Attachment reads are confined to the model root. Paths are normalized and rejected if absolute, drive- or UNC-qualified, escaping the root with `..`, containing control characters, or naming a Windows device (`CON`, `NUL`, `COM1`, ...). Links and reparse points on the file or any directory of its path are refused, and the resolved path is checked against the root again before reading.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): Only regular files are read. On Linux and macOS the file type is checked with `lstat` before opening, so a FIFO or device cannot block the MCP server or CLI; where the type cannot be verified, the attachment is refused with a warning. Sizes are checked before reading.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.28.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.28.0)): Only a SHA-256 of an attachment's content leaves the loader; contents are never shown, logged or persisted, and diagnostics name only the authored path. Link checks run before opening and after reading, but not as one atomic no-follow open, so a concurrent local writer could swa
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): Anonymous observable callers are limited per connection or address instead of sharing one server-wide allowance.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): WebSocket upgrades and SSE hub control requests are checked against the request's Origin, with trusted proxy metadata for TLS-terminating proxies.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): Upgrade requests for paths that are not Arc's get a 404 instead of hanging, handshakes time out, and a client that resets its connection during an upgrade no longer crashes the process.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.4.0 — observable queries and the Arc package layout](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.4.0)): Observable errors are redacted outside development, and each subscription gets its own frozen copy of the principal.
- **AI** ([Release v4.0.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v4.0.0)): An AI provider's API key is no longer written to the causation chain in plain text. A command's property values are recorded there before `Handle` runs, so protecting the value inside the handler never covered them — the key was stored in the clear, permanently, beside the event that protected it. `AIProviderApiKey` is now `[NotAudited]`. Anyone upgrading should treat keys configured through earlier versions as exposed in their own event log.
- **cli** ([Release v3.17.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.17.1)): The `screenplay` MCP descriptor must be stdio, command `cratis`, with exactly `screenplay mcp` as arguments. Anything else is refused before any file is written, matching the rule the Pi bridge in Cratis/AI enforces.
- **cli** ([Release v3.17.1](https://github.com/Cratis/cli/releases/tag/v3.17.1)): `cratis ai status` flags a managed MCP entry whose launch command is not the allowed one.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.0)): `Policy` and `AuthenticationSchemes` no longer silently fall back to authentication-only checks. Unknown, ambiguous, malformed, or unsupported requirements fail closed; applications using previously ignored settings must register valid policies and schemes before startup (#2736).
- **Arc** ([Release v22.23.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.23.0)): Scheme-selected execution isolates the effective principal, tenant, and scoped services. Unsafe caller-supplied providers and identity-changing nested scheme calls are rejected rather than reusing services bound to another identity. Standalone Core does not support named authentication schemes (#2736).
- **AI** ([Release v3.3.0](https://github.com/Cratis/AI/releases/tag/v3.3.0)): A refused command tells the agent not to retry or work around it, and to report the exact command, its target server or context, and what it would change, so the user decides.
- **Components** ([Release v4.13.1](https://github.com/Cratis/Components/releases/tag/v4.13.1)): Every checkout in `javascript-build.yml` now sets `persist-credentials: false`. None of the build jobs push, so the token no longer stays in the local git config where later steps (dependency scripts, tests) could read it.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.21.0)): `[Authorize]` and `[AllowAnonymous]` from `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authorization` are enforced again on model-bound commands and read models when Arc is hosted on ASP.NET Core. Since v18.2.0 a model-bound artifact protected only with Microsoft's `[Authorize]` was open to every caller — over HTTP, over hub subscriptions and through direct pipeline execution — because the evaluators meant to honour it read Arc's same-named attributes instead (#2719)
- **Arc** ([Release v22.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.21.0)): Every authorization attribute on a declaration now applies. Previously only the first one found was evaluated, so a second, stacked requirement — `[Roles("Admin")]` together with `[Authorize(Roles = "Auditor")]` — was silently dropped
- **Arc** ([Release v22.21.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.21.0)): A declaration marked both `[AllowAnonymous]` and `[Authorize]`/`[Roles]` is always rejected as ambiguous. Previously it either threw or admitted anonymous callers depending on which evaluator was discovered first (#2714)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.15.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.15.0)): Preserve restricted file access during model replacement, moves, and rollback; refuse recovery that would overwrite unexpected external edits.
- **Arc** ([Release v22.19.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.19.0)): SSE subscribe and unsubscribe now verify that the caller owns the connection they name. The identity that opened the stream is captured once and compared on each control request; a mismatch returns 404, exactly as an unknown connection id does, so the endpoints never confirm another caller's connection exists. Previously a caller holding another connection's id could attach a subscription whose results were written into that caller's stream, or cancel their subscriptions (#2713)

## Deprecated

- **Arc** ([Release v22.27.2](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc/releases/tag/v22.27.2)): `<Arc basePath>` is deprecated because it does not configure routing or API requests; its context value remains available for compatibility (#2593)
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.33.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.33.0)): `ZeroOrOne` and `Many` on a projection transition have no Chronicle meaning. An executable model that carries them, built in code or read from canonical bytes, reports warning `PLAY0445`; they will be removed in the next model version. Models compiled from `.play` sources never carry them.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.19.0 — setup, configuration and host integrations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.19.0)): `addChronicle`, `addMongoDB` and `addDrizzle`; use `withChronicle`, `withMongoDB` and `withDrizzle`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.19.0 — setup, configuration and host integrations](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.19.0)): `mountExpress`, `mountFastify`, `mountHono` and the `mount*WebSockets` functions; use `cratisArc`.
- **Arc.TypeScript** ([Arc for TypeScript 0.18.0 — RxJS observable queries](https://github.com/Cratis/Arc.TypeScript/releases/tag/v0.18.0)): `CurrentValueSubject` is deprecated; use RxJS `BehaviorSubject` instead.
- **Screenplay** ([Release v4.25.0](https://github.com/Cratis/Screenplay/releases/tag/v4.25.0)): PLAY0176 is retired and no longer reported. Two identical `reads` lines used to give one PLAY0176 with the second line dropped; now both lines are kept and reported as PLAY0410. The `DiagnosticCodes.DuplicateReads` constant remains for compatibility.
